# Reference

## Quick Skill Reference

| Topic | Skills |
|---|---|
| Development cycles | `/do-flow "topic"`, `/do-brainstorm "topic"` |
| Design & architecture | `/do-design "feature"`, `/do-constitution` |
| Planning & implementation | `/do-plan`, `/do-execute-plan` |
| Testing & code review | `/do-test`, `/do-code-review` |
| Analysis & diagnostics | `/do-diagnose path --focus quality\|security\|performance\|architecture` |
| Documentation & research | `/do-document path --type api\|guide\|impl\|index\|research` |

## Full Skill Reference

Arguments below are transcribed from each skill's `argument-hint` (`/do-code-review` declares none;
its `[target]` is informal). G8 (`test/guards/reachability.test.ts`) fails in both directions: when a row here
documents a flag that the skill's `argument-hint` does not declare, and when a declared flag is
missing from its row.

| Skill | Description |
|---|---|
| `/do [command\|request] [--depth shallow\|normal\|deep] [--estimate]` | Universal dispatcher, intent routing, tool capability selection, and estimation; `/do program <goal>` researches a programme-sized ask and hands you a checked roadmap draft |
| `/do-flow [feature description] [--from brainstorm\|design\|plan\|implement\|test\|review]` | Full-cycle development: brainstorm → design → plan → execute → test → code-review |
| `/do-brainstorm [topic/idea] [--intent <path>] [--depth shallow\|normal\|deep]` | Discover requirements through Socratic dialogue; seeds requirement.md in a branch-coupled feature dir |
| `/do-design [target] [--type architecture\|api\|component\|database]` | Design system architecture, APIs, and component interfaces; writes design.md |
| `/do-plan [--depth shallow\|normal\|deep]` | Generate implementation plan and dependency-ordered task checklist; writes plan.md |
| `/do-execute-plan [--scope next\|phase:N\|all\|resume] [--review] [--scaffold]` | Execute the plan.md task checklist, in session or through subagents, behind readiness gates |
| `/do-test [target] [--clean] [--watch]` | Execute project builds, automated test suites, and coverage verification |
| `/do-code-review [target]` | Code review automation: analyze complexity, risk, SOLID compliance, and code smells |
| `/do-implement [description of the change] [--from-review]` | Direct, standalone implementation from a description or `/do-code-review` findings — no chain artifacts required |
| `/do-git [intent] [args...] [--confirm]` | Cycle-aware git operations via named intents: start, save, sync, ship, release, hotfix, backport, status |
| `/do-constitution [principle inputs] [--amend]` | Create or amend the per-repo tier-2 constitution overlay and sync impact reports |
| `/do-diagnose [target\|issue] [--focus quality\|security\|performance\|architecture] [--fix]` | Unified diagnostics, root-cause investigation, and targeted code remediation |
| `/do-document [target\|query] [--type api\|guide\|impl\|index\|research] [--depth shallow\|normal\|deep]` | Unified technical documentation, architecture indexing, and deep web research |

## Full Skill List

The full installed skill set is: `do`, `do-brainstorm`, `do-code-review`, `do-constitution`, `do-design`, `do-diagnose`, `do-document`, `do-execute-plan`, `do-flow`, `do-git`, `do-implement`, `do-plan`, and `do-test`.

## Specialist Agent Archetypes

Specialist agent archetypes provide dedicated perspectives for planning, execution, and validation. Their definitions live in `core/shared/agent-specs/`:

| Archetype | Responsibilities | Default Mode |
|---|---|---|
| `spec-analyst` | Requirements elicitation, user story breakdown, effort estimation | Workspace-write |
| `system-architect` | System architecture, boundary design, API contracts, infrastructure | Workspace-write |
| `core-implementer` | Polyglot implementation, clean refactoring, algorithmic speedup | Workspace-write |
| `quality-guardian` | Automated test suites, security vulnerability auditing, root-cause diagnosis | Workspace-write |
| `research-writer` | Multi-hop cited web research, architecture indexing, technical documentation | Workspace-write |
| `delivery-lead` | Task planning, worker briefs and dispatch, evidence checks, commits and merges; no product code | Workspace-write |
| `reviewer` | Review of a change it did not write: evidenced findings, severity, review rounds and synthesis | Read-only |

## Agent binding, access and limits

Three registry facts connect the seven archetypes to the workflows and to each harness. The rows below
name where each is declared, who reads it and what guards it.

| Field | Declared in | Meaning | Guarded by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stage `agent` | `core/registry/workflows.json`, on every stage of every class | The archetype (a spec in `core/shared/agent-specs/`) that works the stage: a required lowercase kebab-case name, checked by the validator in `src/runtime/workflow-engine.ts`. `feature` planning and implementation and `operations` state-check and execution are bound to `delivery-lead`; every review stage is bound to `reviewer`. | G63 (the name is a spec, and every spec is bound by some stage) |
| Spec `access` | The frontmatter of each agent spec: `read-only` or `write` | What the agent may do to the repository. Only `reviewer` is `read-only`; `spec-analyst`, `system-architect`, `quality-guardian`, `research-writer`, `core-implementer` and `delivery-lead` are `write`. The key is never projected under that name into any file of any harness (Codex Markdown agents included, through the `codex-agents` transform): each harness turns it into its own limit keys (next row). The Default Mode column above is derived from it. | G63 (table mode, Codex `sandbox_mode`, projection), G64 (every spec declares it) |
| `agentLimits.readOnly` | `core/registry/harnesses.json`, one per harness | How a `read-only` spec is limited on that harness. `native`: `apply` holds the keys the adapter writes into the agent file (Claude `disallowedTools`; Copilot and Kiro a `tools` list; OpenCode a `permission` block with `edit` and `bash` set to `deny`). `unenforced`: a `reason` says why (Codex, Pi, Antigravity). A `write` spec gets no keys. Each `evidence` entry must be a non-blank `http(s)` URL (an `unenforced` entry may list none only with the reason "no agent surface"). | G63 (declared everywhere; an `unenforced` limit needs a reason; a projection carries the keys for read-only and, for a `write` spec, only its tool-scope keys), `test/registry/agent-limits.test.ts` |
| `agentLimits.toolScope` | `core/registry/harnesses.json`, one per harness | How a spec's `tool-scope` (categories `read`, `edit`, `shell`, `web`, `agents`, `mcp`) is written. `native`: `field`, `form` (`comma`, `list` or `closed`), a `map` from each built-in category to the harness's tool names (`read` includes the skill-loading and todo tools where the vendor names them, never an ask-the-user tool), and an `mcpSelector` template that writes `mcp` once per MCP server DoFlow's registry names (`mcp.json` servers plus the `mcp.server` of `capabilities.json` providers: context7, graphify, semble, sequential-thinking). Claude gets a `tools` line (`mcp__{server}__*`), Copilot and Kiro a `tools` list (`{server}/*`, `@{server}`), and OpenCode a closed `permission` block: `"*": deny`, then `allow` for each declared tool and `{server}_*`. The read-only limit can narrow the built-in tools, never widen them, and keeps the MCP selectors (all registry servers are retrieval servers). `unenforced`: a `reason`, and no tool field is projected (Codex, Pi, Antigravity). `doflow doctor` (`[Agent Tool Scope]`) and `capabilities` list each harness's row. | G77, `test/registry/agent-limits.test.ts`, `test/runtime/doctor-reach.test.ts` |
| Orchestration `nest` | `core/registry/harnesses.json`, in each harness's `orchestration` block, the sixth operation after spawn, wait, status, cancel and resume | Whether a subagent can start a subagent. Claude, Copilot and Antigravity are `supported`; Pi `unsupported`; Codex, OpenCode and Kiro `unknown` (the vendor documentation is silent). Anything not `supported` falls back to `sequential`. No runtime code branches on it. | `src/registry/index.ts` (`ORCHESTRATION_OPS`), `test/registry/orchestration.test.ts` |

The `apply` keys and tool names for Copilot and Kiro and the `permission` keys for OpenCode were
read from vendor documentation (`checked: docs`) and have not been run against the live CLIs; treat them as
unverified until a live check records them. The docs check (reported by the second review seat, context7,
2026-10-23) found `bash` to be OpenCode's documented permission key, so the projected bytes match the docs
and FU-v4p417 stays open for the live check only; the Copilot names also read correct in the
docs. The Kiro `tools` categories `read`, `shell` and `web` are the Kiro CLI V3 vocabulary
(https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/v3/agent-config/); CLI 2.x named its tools `fs_read`, `execute_bash` and so on,
and Kiro's migration table lists `read` and `shell` as names a 2.x CLI also accepts, so only `web` is a
V3-only tag: on a 2.x CLI the projected `reviewer` `tools` list may be only partly recognised (`web`), not
wholly unrecognised (inference from the two documentation pages). Open followups, all the user's live
checks: FU-cqmhg8, FU-v4p417 and FU-q2pwes.

Two limits are recorded and not enforced. The `delivery-lead` rule "no product code" and the `quality-guardian`
rule "tests only" are stated in their specs; both agents are `access: write`, which cannot express a path
limit, so the rules hold by discipline. Because the four non-reviewer specs (`spec-analyst`,
`system-architect`, `quality-guardian`, `research-writer`) are `write`, OpenCode projects no `edit` or `bash`
deny for them and their Codex TOMLs carry no `sandbox_mode`; only `reviewer` is limited.

**MCP tools on the read-only agent.** The `reviewer` `tools` allowlist on Copilot and Kiro names
built-in tools only, so the reviewer runs there without MCP tools (context7, semble). DoFlow projects no MCP
selector in 118. The vendors document per-server selectors: Copilot `<server>/*`
and Kiro `@<server>` (read from the Kiro CLI migration guide; not run live). The
servers DoFlow installs itself are listed in `core/registry/mcp.json` (`context7`, `sequential-thinking`), so
their names are known. They are not projected because the global wildcards (Kiro `@mcp`) would
also open the write-capable tools of any MCP server; restoring per-server selectors for DoFlow's own servers
is followup FU-83yxjr. A user who knows a server is read-only can extend the projected `reviewer` file by
hand with the vendor's selector: Copilot `<server>/*` or `<server>/<tool>`
(https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/custom-agents-configuration); Kiro `@mcp`
(https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/v3/agent-config/).

Agent specs are standalone: neither a shared spec nor a Codex agent TOML names a DoFlow runtime verb, a
`doflow <verb>` command, a state path, a record, a trailer or `DoFlow-Row` line, or a chain artifact name
(G64 derives the verb list from the CLI and fails on a hit). Each spec carries one line that lets a workflow's
stage brief override its defaults; `core-implementer` alone keeps five optional lines about chain artifacts, an
exact-line exception that fails when a line goes stale. The DoFlow
procedure for a stage lives in the stage's skill. The roster is capped at seven specs, each description at
160 characters and all descriptions together at 1,000 (G64).

## Runtime & Diagnostics Commands

These are `doflow` CLI commands, plus five `doflow-run` shell verbs (`render-diagrams`, `render-puml`, `render-page`, `render-drawio`, `task-brief`) and the
`bench` harness rows; none is a slash-command skill. Installation and lifecycle commands
(`install`, `update`, `status`, `rollback`, `remove`) are documented in [Setup](setup.md).
When `doflow-run` selects a CLI from the caller's checkout, it warns on stderr if that checkout's HEAD is behind its local `develop` branch.

### Command index

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED:command-index -->
| Command \(doflow\) | Summary |
|---|---|
| `install` | Install configs to target tools \(use --dry-run to preview\) |
| `update` | Incremental update of changed files only |
| `reconcile` | Converge observed state onto the doflow.lock pin \(drift report + heal\) |
| `status` | Show resolved context + installed state from manifest \(--json for scripting\) |
| `rollback` | Restore from a backup \(interactive pick if id omitted\) |
| `remove` | Remove only lifecycle-owned native resources |
| `list-backups` | List available backups |
| `self-update` | git pull + reinstall |
| `tools` | List external tools and whether each is present; install, update or uninstall one |
| `ui` | Run the DoFlow Console, a separately installed package \(--port N, --lan, --action pair\|devices\|revoke; exit 2 when it is not installed\) |
| `capabilities` | Show registered abstract capabilities and resolved providers |
| `enforcement` | Report lifecycle policy enforcement coverage \(--harness, --policy\) |
| `doctor` | System health and capability smoke check diagnostics |
| `readiness` | Evaluate task readiness contract \(--task-class, --task-id, both required\) |
| `evidence` | Record a stage's evidence batch, or inspect what is recorded \(--task-id\) |
| `claim` | Record a claim, link evidence to it, or list them \(--task-id, --action\) |
| `context-pack` | Compile a task's evidence and claims into a context block \(--task-id\) |
| `context-emit` | Decide and render one launch, prompt or delta emission \(--event, --cwd\) |
| `context-grant` | Grant one compacted summary to a launched run and lineage \(--summary, --to-run, --to-lineage\) |
| `retrieval-plan` | Declare a stage's information needs, attempt retrieval, then report every declared item |
| `outcome` | Record a task's terminal state with its basis, or show it \(--task-id\) |
| `classify` | Validate a proposed task class and return its workflow \(--task-class \[--view brief\|full\]\) |
| `workflow` | Resolve a task class to its stages, gates and readiness templates |
| `orchestrate` | Start or resume a run: complete stages, decide gates \(--action\) |
| `research-request` | Open, list or resolve a feature-stage research question \(--task-id\) |
| `task-attempt` | Claim, finish, release, run controls, resume, inspect, reopen or report usage on a plan's task attempts \(--action, --slug\) |
| `worker` | Discover, probe, describe, select, run, status or cancel an external headless worker on a claimed task, or start a main session \(--action\) |
| `dispatch` | Run a feature's task graph: start, step, run, submit, spawn, pause, inspect, cancel, takeover or resume \(--action\) |
| `retrieve` | BM25 guidance search by default; use --scope code for scoped project retrieval \(--query\) |
| `model-role` | Resolve a model role to ranked provider candidates \(--role, --exclude-family\) |
| `advise` | Route decision questions to the configured local model \(--action\) |
| `brief-tools` | Tier, tools and report-format block for a dispatch brief \(--owner\) |
| `route` | Resolve an information need to a healthy provider \(--intent\) |
| `verify` | Compile the verification contract and report against it \(--task-id\) |
| `recover` | Classify a verification failure and plan the bounded retry \(--error\) |
| `trace` | Trajectory of the current or most recent workflow \(run ledger\). |
| `stats` | Aggregate local run-ledger usage |
| `indicators` | Per-stage and per-gate timings from the orchestration record |
| `discover` | Missed capability opportunities in recorded runs |
| `inventory` | Shadowed copies, drift and unmanaged files across both install scopes |
| `storage` | Report stored data and retention bounds \(--action report\|prune\|upkeep\) |
| `config` | Show each setting's effective value and where it came from, print the settings schema, or validate, set or unset one \(exit 0 ok, 1 refused, 2 usage\). |
| `scaffold` | Emit the reviewable code scaffold the active feature's artifacts imply |
| `decision` | Register, list or compact the active feature's decisions \(--action init\|add\|list\|compact\) |
| `review-pack` | Write the feature's review pack from its records: review-pack --task-id &lt;slug&gt; \[--json\] |
| `intake` | Sort, preview, undo, list or file prompt text for the active feature's additional-context folder \(--action sort\|preview\|undo\|list\|file-prompt\) |
| `followup` | Record, list, take, settle, promote or report what a feature left unfinished \(--action add\|list\|take\|settle\|promote\|report; list: \[--view brief\|full\] \[--cursor &lt;c&gt;\] \[--limit &lt;n&gt;\] \[--ids &lt;FU-id,...&gt;\]\) |
| `lifecycle` | Open follow-ups, feature tracking, releases and status for the project \(--action overview\|init\|release\|status\|merged; overview: \[--view brief\|full\]\) |
| `goal` | Add, check, link, list or close a goal and its checklist \(--action add\|item\|check\|link\|list\|done\) |
| `failure` | List, settle or switch off the failures DoFlow captured on this machine \(--action list\|settle\|capture\) |
| `roadmap` | Session-driven autopilot runner \(--action render\|status\|next\|audit\|report\|init-policy \[--check\] \[--row &lt;id&gt;\] \[--run &lt;id&gt;\]\). |
| `plan-check` | Check a plan's task lines the way the task-attempt ledger reads them: plan-check &lt;plan path&gt; \[--json\] |
| `review` | Validate a feature's review/findings.json; print errors, advisories and counts \(exit 0 valid, 1 invalid, 2 usage\). |
| `protocol` | Print the review and debate protocol presets, or the one a run would use \(read-only, writes nothing\). |
| `rules` | Print the values the runtime owns for this project, each with its source \(read-only, writes nothing\). |
| `primary` | Resolve which harness and model the main session runs \(read-only\). |
| `fleet` | List the projects this machine knows \(read-only\). |
| `transcript` | Read an agent's recent events, or the project's traffic \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `events` | Follow live sections of the read verbs as NDJSON envelopes on stdout \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `worker-log` | Print one masked page of a worker run's out or err log by byte cursor \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `worker-result` | Print the result fields of one worker run: phase, end, report status, checks, offered result \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `worker-runs` | List one feature's worker runs, ended and child runs included, newest 100 \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `advice-picks` | List advice picks per UTC day over the last 90 days with each day's coverage, and the newest 50 picks \(read-only, run in a project\). |
| `worker-matrix` | Print every harness's worker and orchestration cells from the install's registry, evidence masked and capped \(read-only\). |
| `sessions-history` | List finished workflow sessions within retention window \(read-only\). |
| `worktree` | Remove a merged feature's worktree and branch, never by force \(--action cleanup\). |
| `attendance` | Say whether a person is watching a stage \(read-only\). |
| `package` | List the contribution points a package may fill, check a package directory, show the packages, add and update a local package, or turn it on and off. |
| `slot` | Named machine-wide queue, one holder at a time \(--action run\|list\|peek\|release\). |
| `models` | Synchronize, list, or inspect observed models across workers \(--action sync\|list\|describe\|bind\|unbind\) |
| `leak-scan` | Report DoFlow-internal identifiers in shipped files \(--path, repeatable\) |

<!-- END GENERATED:command-index -->

| Command | Description |
|---|---|
| `doflow doctor [--json]` | Health check: harness adapters, capability providers, index freshness, and the project's detected build and test commands. Health means a provider **answered a probe**, not that its binary is on `PATH` — an installed provider that cannot answer reports `UNHEALTHY`, and one that declares no probe reports `UNVERIFIED`. Its `[Runtime Reach]` section lists, per installed harness and scope, whether the skills' resolver finds a dispatcher and runtime (`REACHED`, `NO-REACH` with the install command that fixes it, or `N/A`; in `--json`, `harnesses[].reach[]`). Reach is a static check of the installed files: it does not consider the other ways the dispatcher finds the CLI (`DOFLOW_CLI`, a `node_modules` install, a `doflow` on `PATH`). Two further sections report on MCP servers and never enter `[Warnings]` or `[Findings]` (in `--json`, `mcp.servers` and `mcp.consultations`). `[MCP Servers]` lists each server a shipped stage names and, per installed harness and scope, whether it is `selected` (in DoFlow's catalog selection), `configured` (present in the harness's MCP config, whoever wrote it), `not selected`, `not taken` (the harness takes no catalog servers; both only where DoFlow read every config the harness merges) or `unknown` (DoFlow cannot see every config the harness merges, as for Claude Code, or a config it reads could not be parsed, which prints `fix <file> first`), with the command that adds it where it is missing. `[MCP Consultations]` reads this project's orchestration journals (`.doflow/state/orchestration/`) and, per stage skill, counts the servers' outcomes over the 20 most recent recorded stage runs: `consulted, used`, `consulted, nothing usable`, `skipped`, `unavailable` and `no outcome recorded`, each in its own column, and, per server and skill, lists the latest 3 runs with no outcome after their count. Only runs that recorded MCP outcomes are counted, so a stage completed before this existed never shows as missing one. Finished orchestration journals are pruned 30 days after their work finished (see the `task-orchestration` row below), so the window counts only the runs still kept. `[Decision Model]` reports the optional local decision model; `[Advice Agreement]` summarizes recorded advisory picks and outcomes. These sections do not change the exit status. Doctor starts no MCP server: its MCP sections only read files, while provider probes run each declared probe command and an enabled decision model's endpoint is queried. When this doctor runs with `DOFLOW_UPKEEP=off`, it prints `note: DOFLOW_UPKEEP=off in this environment: upkeep and lifecycle retention are skipped`; `--json` adds `upkeepSwitch: "off"`, and neither appears otherwise. The adapter line of the text output reads `adapter PASS`; the JSON gains additive fields only. Exits 1 when a provider is installed but does not answer, or a harness has `NO-REACH`; neither MCP section changes the exit status The `[Warnings]` section (the `warnings` array in `--json`) also names a Node below the patched release of its line, 24.22.1 or 26.12.1 (CVE-2026-111497; kind `node-security-release`); it is a warning only, never a finding, and a line with no known patched release, such as Node 25 or 27, gets none. It also warns (kind `claude-permissions-user-array`, never a finding) when a Claude `settings.json` that the install ledger records as projected by DoFlow (hooks on or off) holds your own `permissions.allow`, `deny` or `ask` array that lacks DoFlow's entries for that list: an array you wrote wins whole and DoFlow never merges into it, so add the entries yourself if you want them. The shipped `ask` list (`git push` and `git tag`, with an option before the subcommand and in the bare form) prompts before a push or a tag that the blanket `Bash(git *:*)` allow would approve; it matches command text only, so it does not catch an alias (`git p`, `git -c alias.x=push x`), another command that moves refs (`git send-pack`, `git update-ref refs/tags/...`), or a push run through a wrapper script. It also warns (kind `lifecycle-migration-pending`, never a finding, no change to the exit code) when the lifecycle events still sit only in `agent-docs/lifecycle/events` and have not been copied to `.doflow/state/lifecycle`; the line gives the recovery (run any followup, lifecycle or goal command once without `DOFLOW_UPKEEP=off`). The check is read-only: it takes no lock, writes nothing and runs no upkeep, and it applies under `DOFLOW_UPKEEP=off` too. Its `[External Tools]` section lists each absent external tool with its role and the one command that installs it for this machine, or says that every tool is on `PATH`; an absent tool is never a finding (in `--json`, `externalTools[]` carries `role` and, for an absent tool, `install`; a present tool has no `install` key). Its `[Worker Pool & Models]` section prints one line per harness, `<id> binary: <installed\|missing\|unknown> login: <cell> mode: <..> billing: <..>`, then one line per provider a login recipe checked. In `--json`, `workerPool.harnesses[]` carries `login` (the harness's login answer, see [Login state](#login-state)) and `synced` (true when the models catalogue holds the harness; its binary reads `unknown` when it does not) in place of the older login fields; a harness with no catalogue entry still gets its answer, which doctor reads from the `harness-login` store without running a login check. |
| `doflow tools [--tool <ids>] [--action status\|install\|update\|uninstall] [--json] [--dry-run]` | List the external tools in `core/registry/external-tools.json`, required first, then optional: present or absent, what each unlocks and, for an absent one, the install command chosen for this machine (the first route of the platform, the managed one; other routes are listed as alternatives and never run) with the `doflow tools --tool <id> --action install` form. `status` is the default, read-only, and works without a terminal; with no `--tool` it lists every tool. `install`, `update` and `uninstall` need `--tool` outside an interactive terminal, show and separately confirm every command (also a step that follows an install, such as agent-browser's browser download), and reject `--force`. A route or a step that needs administrator rights is shown (DoFlow runs only a command that starts with the tool's own program or its route's installer (`brew`, `npm`, `cargo`, `uv`, `pipx`, `apt-get`) and names no `sudo`, `doas`, `su`, `pkexec` or `run0`, and a shell, `env` or an interpreter given inline code is run only as `<program> --version`; any other command is not run, its result is `refused` and the exit code is 1) (a `manual` step: "run it yourself") and never run. A status check that gives no answer in 5 s is listed as `timeout` with the tool's own status command to run, and nothing it started is left running (`inspection-timeout` in `--json`, with `timeout.statusCommand` and `timeout.message`); the verb still exits 0, `update` and `uninstall` give a `skipped` result for that tool (exit 0), and running the list again is the remedy. `update` and `uninstall` with the installer missing are also `skipped`, with `missing installer: needs <installer> on PATH`. Where no official page gives a command, the list says so and names the page. `--json` carries the same data (`results[].role`, `unlocks`, `state`, `install` with `alternatives` and `manual`) |
| `doflow capabilities [--json] [--check]` | Which provider currently backs each abstract capability on this machine. `--check` adds a smoke check (each provider's probe command) after the presence check passes |
| `doflow enforcement --harness <id> --policy <id> [--profile <id>] [--json]` | Read the lifecycle coverage matrix and report whether the selected policy is verified to block before a tool runs. Only `mcp-tool-guard` rows are blocking; the implement gate and `stop-check` rows are advisory, so they report not enforced (exit 1). JSON includes the evidence rows, prerequisites and known gaps. Exits 0 when enforced, 1 otherwise, or 2 for usage and unknown ids |
| `doflow readiness --task-class <class> --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--verification-plan <text>] [--scope <text>] [--invariants <text>] [--user-decision-pending] [--json]` | Evaluate a task's readiness contract. Classes: `bug`, `feature`, `refactor`, `trivial-edit`, `dependency-change`. Returns one of four states — `READY`, `NEEDS_EVIDENCE`, `NEEDS_USER_DECISION`, `BLOCKED` — never a number. The four trailing flags are inputs the **caller states** rather than evidence the gate measured, so they are reported straight back as `callerAsserted` (JSON) and `Caller-stated:` (text), and a requirement satisfied that way links no evidence. Every call records its evaluation, whatever its state, as the task's latest record under `.doflow/state/readiness/` (in a `<slug>/` subdirectory under the same rule as evidence, described below) and prints `Recorded:` with the path; `--json` adds `record` and `declaredScope`. A `--scope` that is a comma-separated list of repository-relative paths is also kept as the task's declared scope, which `verify` bounds a change by (`Declared scope:`); any other wording stays a statement. An input a call does not pass (`--scope`, `--verification-plan`, `--invariants`) is taken from the task's last record for the same template, so a bare re-check keeps a READY verdict reached with them (`Carried:` line, `carriedFromLastRecord` in JSON); passing an input replaces it, and an input can be replaced but not dropped. A previous record that cannot be read is moved to `<record>.unreadable` and a `Replaced:` line says so; a record written by a newer DoFlow is never replaced. The call exits 1 when it could not record the evaluation |
| `doflow evidence --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--action list\|add\|supersede\|retire] [--stale] [--kind <k>] [--provenance extracted\|inferred\|asserted] [--provider <p>] [--capability <c>] [--locator <file[:line]\|uri>] [--content <text>] [--need <intent>] [--absence] [--batch <file>] [--json]` | List a task's recorded evidence, or record it with `--action add`: one item from the flags, or a whole stage batch from `--batch` (a JSON array, or an object whose only key is `evidence`; `--batch -` or `--batch=-` reads stdin). Per item `kind`, `provenance` and `source` (`--provider` + `--capability`) are required and none is defaulted; `extracted` additionally requires a locator unless the item carries an observation (`--observed-command` and `--observed-exit`), `inferred` and `asserted` require content, and `generated-analysis`/`user-statement` may never be `extracted`. `--need <intent>` names exactly one declared intent and stamps `source.queryRef` from the task's current plan; without it the item remains unbound. `source.attemptRef` is read from the active task attempt. `--absence` requires `--need`, takes no locator, requires `extracted` provenance, writes `result: EMPTY`, and measures freshness from the provider's index at write time. Caller-supplied `queryRef` is refused with the fix to re-run using `--need`; `attemptRef` is refused with the fix to claim the task attempt and re-run without it; `result` is refused with the fix to use `--absence --need`. Absence without `--need` asks you to name the need, a locator asks you to drop it or record a positive item, and non-extracted provenance asks you to use `extracted`. A locator that names a directory is refused for every provenance (exit 2, naming the locator). Freshness is measured at the write and never accepted from the caller. `--action retire --stale` retires every item the ledger currently classes stale, judged as readiness judges them, with no replacement and the reason kept on each item; it prints the count and ids and leaves fresh items untouched. A batch is validated whole, so one rejected item writes nothing. `--confidence`, `--score`, `--relevance` and every other score-shaped flag are refused by name: relevance is a property of a search, not of a fact. **Two scopes:** a list reads the feature's `<slug>/` record; while that file does not exist it reads the flat record of the same task instead, but only when that record parses (a flat file that does not parse, or is not a regular file of at most 1 MiB, is never read) and names this feature, or names none and the feature has no `decisions/register.json`, and the first write goes to the namespace, starting from the flat one, after which only the namespaced record is read. A flat record is never read for a feature that has its own file, a task id equal to the slug has only the flat record, and a namespaced record is never read by a call that resolves no slug (the main checkout on `develop`, say), so such a call can list nothing or only part of what a feature recorded: pass `--slug <name>` there (see [Where a task's records live](#where-a-tasks-records-live)). |
| `doflow-run render-diagrams [--slug=<slug>] [--artifact=<path>] [--format=png|svg] [--json]` (`render-diagrams.sh`) | Render a chain artifact's fenced Mermaid blocks to image files, styled by the shipped theme. A **faithful** render: same nodes, same edges, same labels, and the committed Markdown is never modified. Output lands in `<feature dir>/design/diagrams/` and is excluded from version control by an ignore rule the verb writes on first run. Requires `@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli` on `PATH`, an optional dependency DoFlow never installs: when it is absent the verb reports how to obtain it and exits 0, because it sits outside the chain and must never make a missing optional capability read as a broken run. Runs only when a user invokes it, never as part of a stage, which is why no skill names it and `test/guards/verb-reachability.test.ts` allowlists the verb |
| `doflow-run render-puml [--slug=<slug>] [--check] [--json]` (`render-puml.sh`) | Render a feature's authored PlantUML sources under `design/c4/` to committed images, and project one of them (`container.puml`, else `context.puml`) into one delimited Mermaid block of the design artifact, from the same read of that source. `--check` writes nothing and reports blocks whose source changed since they were written (it compares the source hash, so a block written by an older projector counts as current until the verb runs again). No active feature, no `design/c4/` directory and no renderer are each a note with exit 0 and nothing written; an unknown argument exits 2. It writes only into `design/c4/` and that one region. |
| `doflow-run render-page [--slug=<slug>] [--out=<file>] [--diagrams=auto\|inline\|cdn\|source] [--json]` (`render-page.sh`) | Write one self-contained HTML page from a feature's chain documents: requirement, design, interface contracts, data model, plan, decisions, state and audit, whichever exist, in that order, with a contents list. The Markdown is never modified, and document text reaches the page escaped, so a document cannot put markup or script on it. Output is `<feature dir>/page.html` unless `--out` names a file. Diagrams: `inline` renders each Mermaid block with `@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli` and the shipped theme and embeds it, so the page displays with nothing else; `source` shows each block as text; `cdn` makes the page load Mermaid 11.17.2 from jsDelivr when it is opened, as one file checked against a pinned hash; the page then needs the network and Mermaid reads document text in the browser, so this mode is never chosen for you; `auto` (the default) is `inline` when the renderer is on `PATH` and `source` otherwise, with a line saying how to get rendered diagrams, and `inline` without the renderer does the same. User-invoked and outside the chain: no active feature or no document is reported and exits 0. A relative link in a document is rewritten from the document's folder to the page's. A local image a document shows with `![alt](path)` is read and embedded in the page as a data URI when its type is `.svg` (a `.drawio.svg` is one), `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif` or `.webp` and the file it resolves to, links followed, is inside the checkout; the type is judged on that real file, not on the name in the document. An image is left out, with an `image-missing` span on the page and a line in the result's `note`, when it is of another type, resolves outside the checkout, is not a file, cannot be found, has a path with a control character, a space or a backslash, or uses a scheme other than `http(s)`. A file over 5 MB is left out, and so is any image that would take the page's embedded images past 20 MB, counted once per reference since each reference repeats the data; a file shown more than once is read once. An `http(s)` image is not fetched: it becomes a link, so the page makes no request when opened. An SVG is embedded as an `<img>`, so a browser runs no script from it. The `--json` result gains `images`: `{ "embedded": <count of references embedded>, "skipped": <count left out> }`. Exit 1 means the renderer ran and failed on a block (the page is still written, with that block as source) or the projector itself failed (nothing written). `page.html` is a deliverable like the documents it is built from: DoFlow adds no ignore rule for it, so commit it or list it in your own `.gitignore`. |
| `doflow-run render-drawio [--slug=<slug>] [--level=<name>] [--icons=<dir>] [--force] [--timeout=<seconds>] [--json]` (`render-drawio.sh`) | Write an editable draw.io draft of a feature's C4 sources, for a presentation: each `design/c4/<level>.puml` becomes `design/presentation/<level>.drawio` (or only `--level`). It is a first draft that a person then arranges by hand: inside a container, elements joined by relations sit in one column per step of the chain, left to right, and the rest in a grid below; a relation that passes over a column, or joins elements of different containers, has its whole path written so it runs through no shape; a relation between neighbours in one container, and one between an element and a container around it, are left to draw.io's router, and so is a relation that passes over more columns than are left of the 2000 one diagram may route. Written paths are set apart where the layout has room for it: a gap between columns is as wide as the relations turning in it need, and the space under a container's title as tall as the relations leaving it need. The space beside a shape and above a grid row is fixed, so in a crowded container two relations can still lie on one line for part of their way, level or upright; parting them is for the person arranging the draft. A direction hint in the source (`Rel_U`, `Rel_D`, `Rel_L`, `Rel_R`, `Rel_Neighbor`) is read as a plain relation: the layout follows the order of the chain, not the hint. The `.puml` stays the source of truth and Mermaid and PlantUML stay the working diagrams. An element is a box (a database a cylinder, a person an actor, an external one grey) labelled with its name, `[technology]` and description; a boundary is a container holding its children; a relation is an edge. An unrecognized macro refuses the source, naming macro, file and line, as `render-puml` does. **Icons**: DoFlow ships none, because logos are trademarks with mixed licences. Put your own `.svg` or `.png` files in `<feature dir>/design/presentation/icons/` (or the folder `--icons` names, which must resolve inside the checkout), named for what they stand for: the element's `$sprite` value, else its technology, normalised to lower case with every character other than a letter or digit removed (`Apache Kafka` is tried as `apachekafka.svg`, `apachekafka.png`, then `kafka.svg`, `kafka.png`). The first match wins, `.svg` before `.png`, and an icon over 256 KB is skipped and named. The verb prints, for each element that found none, the file names it looked for. [Simple Icons](https://simpleicons.org) and [Devicon](https://devicon.dev) are two places to get them; check each icon's terms before you use it. A `.drawio` edited after it was generated is never overwritten without `--force` (the sha of the generated text is kept in `<level>.drawio.sha` beside it, since draw.io keeps nothing it does not know when it saves); a refused overwrite exits 1. When a `drawio` binary is on `PATH` (or `DOFLOW_DRAWIO` names one) `<level>.drawio.svg` is also exported beside it, with `drawio -x -f svg -e --embed-svg-images`, under a `--timeout` (default 60 seconds); `render-page` embeds that file when a document references it as an image. Without the binary, or if it fails, the `.drawio` is still written, the export is reported as skipped with the command to run, and the exit is 0. Needs `node` and `jq`; like `render-puml`, a feature without `design/c4/` is reported and exits 0 |
| `doflow trace [--days N] [--json]` | Trajectory of the current or most recent workflow, read from the run ledger |
| `doflow trace spans [--run <key|id>] [--json] [<project>]` | Project spans from ledger, telemetry (`runs/telemetry`), task-attempts, orchestration, worker-runs, and dispatch stores without a dedicated span store. Trace segments are scoped to task-attempt slugs rather than session run keys alone. Line placement follows tiered precedence: explicit identifiers beat time windows, an ID-less line matching multiple segments joins if it fits exactly one activity window, and lines held by a single segment join by `(run, lineage)` and window; overlapping windows remain ambiguous gaps. Emits trace spans (recorded vs derived durations) and located gaps (`unmatched`, `ambiguous`, `unparseable`). A `stage` span names the context packs that stage was handed (`pack_count`, and `pack_task` and `pack_digest` of the latest), read from its `complete-stage` entry's `packs` (recorded when `context-pack` delivered each pack); a stage with none has no `pack_*` attribute |
| `doflow trace analyze [--run <key|id>] [--json] [--emit evidence\|followups] [--task-id <id>] [-g] [<project>]` | Its detectors (exit 1 on a finding). `--run <key|id>`: keep the traces of one run key or run id. `--emit evidence\|followups`: print only the batch for evidence --action add --batch -, or for followup --action add --batch <file> (with --task-id, cites its evidence) |
| `doflow trace export --otlp-http [--run <key|id>] [--json] [--dry-run] [-g] [<project>]` | Export spans via OTLP/HTTP. `--otlp-http`: export spans to an OpenTelemetry collector via OTLP/HTTP JSON. `--run <key|id>`: keep the traces of one run key or run id. `-n, --dry-run`: print the request bodies as one JSON array and send nothing (no endpoint needed). Exit 0 all accepted, 1 any failure or rejected span, 2 usage. Never prints the endpoint, a header or a response body; an empty OTEL_* variable counts as unset; `--otlp-http` always sends HTTP/JSON (OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL is not read) |
| `bench perf measure --paired-env <NAME=VALUE> [--surface <ids>] [--runs N] [--seed S] [--json]` | Measure per-surface latency (p50/p95) under an environment pair (`NAME=VALUE` vs the variable unset, e.g. `DOFLOW_TELEMETRY=off` vs unset), in a seeded shuffle of the surfaces each round to limit drift; paired mode measures no context budgets. Refuses `--write` (exit 2) and never modifies `budgets.json`. Requires `NAME=VALUE` format with `NAME` matching `^[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*$`. Emits paired comparison records and delta statistics (`delta`) |
| `bench verify-baseline --snapshot <dir> [--json] [--out <dir>]` | Verification baseline computation over a recorded snapshot directory containing `manifest.json`. Validates manifest schema and runs deterministic baseline computation across recorded cases. Outputs byte-equal analysis reports (`report.json`, `report.md`); a manifest mismatch exits 1, and a missing or malformed snapshot/manifest exits 2 |
| `bench noise --iterations <a,b[,c..]> [--out <file>] [--json]` | Measure eval noise floor over k baseline repeats. Validates that iterations share the same model, commit, and case set, re-runs programmatic grading twice in memory for grader stability, and computes per-repeat means, noise, baseline mean, headroom, and a noise-check verdict. Outputs text or JSON; `--out` writes the noise artifact to the supplied path and has no default. `report` reads the baseline directory's `noise.json` by default (today this is bench/baseline/noise.json). Candidate stamps need `model` and `sourceAt`; the artifact model must match the candidate and baseline models and its case set must match `baseline.json`. With-skill runs whose skill source is not `verified` are excluded as `source-unverified` |
| `bench agentic <plan\|execute\|grade\|noise\|report> [flags]` | Agentic retrieval baseline: coding-agent file localization with and without `retrieve`. Only `execute` spawns the harness (paid) and it refuses without `--cap`; `plan`, `grade`, `noise` and `report` are offline. Protocol, paid stages and what each result triggers: `bench/agentic/README.md` |
| `bench impact-replay [--rev <ref>] [--commits <n>] [--json] [--out <path>]` | Offline replay that measures whether choosing tests from a change's imports would still catch what the full co-changed tests catch. For each of the last `--commits` non-merge commits reachable from `--rev` (`--rev` defaults to `HEAD`; `--commits` defaults to the `impactReplay.commits` value in `bench/config.json`), it exports the commit into a scratch directory with `git archive`, runs the commit's co-changed tests, restores the changed non-test files to their parent versions, runs the tests again to find the detecting set (a lower bound: only the co-changed tests are tried), and compares it with what `src/runtime/verification/impact.ts` would select. Makes no model call and touches no checkout. Prints one verdict: `insufficient-data` (fewer evaluated commits than the configured minimum), `keep-off` (a detecting test was missed, or the median selected fraction reached the ceiling), or `selection-candidate`. **The verdict enables nothing:** `verify` does not use the selector, and only this command reads `impact.ts`. `--out` sets the report path; the default is `bench/runs/impact-replay/<UTC stamp>.json`; `--json` prints the report |
| `bench advice-gate --kind <id> [--arm choice\|ladder\|both] [--model <name>] [--write] [--json]`, `bench advice-gate --freeze` | Score one decision kind against its frozen corpus with the configured local model and print a gate verdict: the number of cases, the valid share, the quality difference against the static answer with a 95% interval, and p50 and p95 latency. `--write` stores the verdict in `core/registry/advice-verdicts.json`; without it nothing is written. `--freeze` refreshes the corpus digests of every choice and yes/no kind and takes no `--kind` or `--model`. `--arm` applies to `--kind rerank` only (default `both`). Exit 0 when a verdict was produced, pass or fail; 1 for a setup or write failure (advice off, endpoint not reached, model missing, a corpus changed without a higher `corpusVersion`); 2 for usage. Details in [Decision model: report, evidence and shadow kinds](#decision-model-report-evidence-and-shadow-kinds). |
| `DOFLOW_TELEMETRY` | Telemetry switch (default `on`). Set to `off` to disable telemetry emission across all hook front doors, policies, and runtime components. When `off`, writers write nothing and create no files or directories under `runs/telemetry` |
| `DOFLOW_UPKEEP` | Exact, case-sensitive `off` skips every upkeep moment: after-verb upkeep (ignore files such as `.gitignore`, upkeep markers, prune passes and the run-ledger line), the prune `verify` runs on verification logs and reuse records, install-time upkeep (the ignore file and the recovery prune), lifecycle retention and the lifecycle marker write. It does not stop a write verb writing its own record or failure capture (`DOFLOW_FAILURE_CAPTURE`). If the one-time lifecycle store copy is still due, `followup`, `lifecycle`, `goal` and `failure --action settle --as imported` refuse with `store-migration-pending` (exit 1; nothing written). Doctor reports the switch only for its own environment |
| `DOFLOW_VERIFY_V0` / `<config>/verify-v0.off` | Verification V0 optimization flag (default `on`). Read once per verify invocation. When set to `off` (or when the `<config>/verify-v0.off` sentinel file exists; stat fault counts as absent/on), disables failed-first test execution and in-flight deduplication, recording `verify.run.v0="off"` |
| `DOFLOW_VERIFY_REUSE` | Can only turn verification reuse off. `off`, `0`, `false` or `no` disables reuse for the invocation even when the `verify.reuse` setting is `on` (see [Settings keys](#settings-keys)); any other value, or none, leaves the setting in charge, so the variable never turns reuse on. Reuse ships off; see [Reuse of a recorded PASS](#reuse-of-a-recorded-pass) |
| `doflow stats [--days N] [--json]` | Aggregate local run-ledger usage: runs per verb, failures, duration percentiles |
| `doflow indicators [--json]` | Per-stage and per-gate timings from the orchestration record, grouped by task class |
| `doflow discover [--days N] [--json]` | Missed capability opportunities in recorded runs. Exits 1 when there is a finding; an analysis it cannot settle from the recorded metadata reports `UNKNOWN` rather than "clear" |
| `doflow storage [--action report|prune|upkeep] [--json] [-g]` | Report declared stores, retention bounds and cleanup candidates, or run cleanup |
| `doflow config [list] [--show-origin] [--json] [-g] [<project path>]` | Read-only effective settings and their sources. Exits 0 when no security-relevant setting is invalid (other invalid settings are reported), 1 when a security-relevant setting is invalid, and 2 on a usage error or unavailable settings registry |
| `doflow config schema [--json]` | Read-only. Every settings key with its type, allowed values, bounds, resolved default, scope, combine rule and `writable` (false for a security-relevant key), then the locked `invariant.*` entries and the review and debate presets. Exit 0; 2 when a registry is unusable |
| `doflow config validate <key> <json-value> [--layer user\|project\|local] [--json]` | Checks a JSON value for a key and writes nothing. Without `--layer` it checks the key and the value only; with `--layer` it also applies the scope and narrowing rules of that layer. Exit 0 valid, 1 refused (one entry per failing key: `key`, `layer`, `code`, `message`, `allowed`), 2 usage |
| `doflow config validate --prepared-run <file> [--json]` | Checks a prepared-run file `{ schema: 1, taskClass, primary, workers, settings, presets: { review, debate } }` with no harness, model call, git or write, and returns one entry per fault keyed by JSON path (`taskClass`, `primary.<field>`, `workers.enabled`, `workers.models.<worker>.<tier>`, `settings.<key>`, `presets.review`, `presets.debate`, or an unknown top-level name). The task class must be one of the workflow registry's; each `settings` entry is judged as `config validate <key> <value> --layer project`; `workers.enabled` is judged as a project-layer narrowing and `workers.models` against worker and tier value rules, including an unevidenced model flag and, for a slot that is an object, the same part checks `models --action bind` makes: `provider-unsupported` and `effort-unsupported` when the worker's descriptor entry is not `supported`, and `effort-unknown-level` when it lists levels and the effort is not one (each keyed `workers.models.<worker>.<tier>`; the message names no model); `primary` takes `harness` and `model`, each judged as `config validate primary.<field> <value> --layer project`. Presets are judged as before: neither may be looser than the preset the settings give, and `presets.review` must meet the class's gate-b seat floor. The risk-policy step of the protocol resolution needs the diff and is not applied. A path, a file that cannot be read, a key or value, or `--layer` given with it exits 2; a file that is not a JSON object is one entry with key `$`. Exit 0 valid, 1 any entry (all reported) |
| `doflow config set <key> <json-value> --layer user\|project\|local [--json]` | Validates as above, then writes that layer's file atomically (`<config home>/settings.json`, `.doflow/settings.json` or `.doflow/settings.local.json`) and prints the resolved value and its origin. Quote a string value (`'"on"'`). Refuses a security-relevant key (`autopilot.enabled`, `verify.reuse`, `workers.billing`, every `advice.*`; a person writes those in the file), an `invariant.*` key (locked), a key the layer may not set, a value wider than the layers below for a narrow-only key, and a `review.rounds` below the `rounds.min` of the effective `review.preset` (`not-narrower`; under `auto` a note, `notes` in `--json`). The write holds `<file>.lock` over its read-merge-rename (a held lock is waited on for 2 s, then refused as `locked`), so a key set meanwhile is kept; a file that changed after the check is checked again, three tries in all, then refused as `changed`; a file that would pass the reader's 64 KiB limit is refused as `too-large`. A write that changes an existing file first keeps the replaced bytes in one `<file>.config.bak`. A symlinked settings file is written through to its target and stays a link; a project or local file linked outside the project is refused as `write-failed` naming the file, as is a file holding a number with more than 15 digits that JSON cannot write back exactly (the message names the key). Nothing is written when it refuses. Exit 0 written, 1 refused, 2 usage |
| `doflow config unset <key> --layer user\|project\|local [--json]` | Removes the key from that layer's file; a key or file that is absent is not an error and creates nothing. Refuses the same locked, unknown and security-relevant keys, and writes under the same lock with the same `locked` and `changed` refusals and `.config.bak`. Exit 0, 1 refused, 2 usage |
| `doflow inventory [path] [-t <list>] [--json]` | Read-only cross-scope inventory: reads the global and the project ledger in **one** invocation and reports, per logical asset, every scope holding a copy, that copy's currency (`matches`, `diverged`, or `indeterminable` — the third means this scope holds no basis for judging the copy at all, whether because no fingerprint is recorded for it, because the scope's plan would remove it rather than rewrite it, because no plan was produced for that harness, or because the recorded row names no harness or no ownership identity — the two components the plan lookup is keyed by, so no planned change could ever be found for it; each copy states which, and none of the four is a weaker `diverged`), the precedence verdict naming the scope whose copy the harness loads, and any unmanaged file sharing a directory the harness loads wholesale. The verdict is derived from the harness's recorded order **and** resolution mode together (`core/registry/harnesses.json`), never from either alone, and is **withheld with its reason** where the mode is unestablished rather than defaulted to a guess. It states its own repair coverage: only a divergence is reachable by `doflow reconcile`, and a shadow, a withheld verdict, a copy nothing judged and an unmanaged file are each reported as having no remedy from it. A scope with nothing recorded is reported as holding nothing — the ordinary state of a repository never installed into — and is never an error. Takes no scope flag: reading both scopes is the whole point, so `-g` is refused rather than silently narrowing the answer. Exits 1 when there is any finding, including a withheld verdict or a copy this scope holds no basis for judging, because the caller cannot conclude the install is sound from either; 2 when `-g` is given or when the registry, a ledger or a named harness cannot be resolved. Writes, moves and deletes nothing — the remedy on each entry is text to run, and the verb never runs it |
| `doflow classify --task-class <id> [--calling-skill <skill-id>] [--rationale <text>] [--proposed-by <who>] [--request <text>\|--request-file <path>] [--view brief\|full] [--json]` | Validate a proposed task class against the workflow registry and return its workflow. A class the registry does not declare is **rejected** with the valid set and a suggestion — never coerced to `feature`. With `--calling-skill`, it also checks that the class's workflow has a stage the caller can occupy, and rejects it when it does not; without it, fit is reported as `NOT_EVALUATED` rather than assumed. Optional `--request` or `--request-file` asks the configured local decision model for an advisory second opinion; the verdict and exit status are determined before that call and never change because of its answer. Under shadow (an `advice.kinds.task-class` of `shadow`, an `advice.kinds.task-class` left unset while a decision model is configured, an `act` that fell back to shadow, or a chosen rollout profile whose advisor is `shadow` or `staged`) the opinion is recorded but not shown: `secondOpinion.status` is `shadow`, its `reason` the `BENCH_BAR_UNMET: …` warning or null. `--view full` is the default and returns the whole answer; `--view brief` keeps the outcome, the reason or message, `validClasses`, `suggestions`, the fit state, reason and `hostingClasses`, `secondOpinion` and the workflow's stage and gate skeleton, and adds a `Full view` next line. Exits 1 on a rejection, 2 when no class was proposed |
| `doflow workflow --task-class <id> [--json]` | Resolve a class to its ordered stages, their gates, which stages mutate source, and which readiness templates gate them. Exits 2 on an unknown class |
| `doflow orchestrate --action start\|status\|list\|timeline\|catch-up\|handoff\|complete-stage\|skip-stage\|decide-gate\|annotate\|close --task-id <id> [--task-class <c>] [--stage <id>[,<id>…]] [--gate <id>] [--node <id>] [--decision approve\|reject] [--policy <gate>] [--expect-revision <n>] [--via console\|device.d-<12 hex>] [--note <text>] [--reason <text>] [--forced] [--calling-skill <skill>] [--result passed\|failed\|unverified] [--mcp-outcome <server>=<outcome>]… [--verification-plan <text>] [--scope <text>] [--invariants <text>] [--json]` | Deterministic run-state machine over a class's stage list. `start` compiles the program; `catch-up` positions the run toward one or more comma-separated candidate stage ids, backfilling the non-mutating stages it walks past (marked `backfilled: true`) and stopping at the first gate, source-mutating stage or candidate (it never walks past a pending gate; `handoff` requires `--calling-skill`, and `--result` records the outcome of `complete-stage` and `handoff`; `complete-stage` without it records `unverified`) and refusing a candidate id the run's own compiled program doesn't contain (the class's workflow, when no run exists yet); each completed or skipped (optional-only) stage advances a persisted cursor under `.doflow/state/orchestration/`; `close --note <why>` ends a `RUNNING` or `AWAITING_GATE` run whose work ended outside the record (state `CLOSED`, journal action `close-run`; no stage or gate changes status, and a finished run is refused); gates pause the run (`AWAITING_GATE`) until approved or rejected, never auto-approved regardless of kind (the one machine decision is `decide-gate --policy`, below, which approves only on evaluated facts and records its decider as `autopilot`); `annotate` appends a history entry without touching cursor/state, for a stage re-run after its own handoff; `handoff` and `complete-stage` of a source-mutating gated stage (the `implementation` stage of `feature`, `bug`, `refactor`, `dependency-change` and `trivial-edit`) need a READY readiness record of the stage's template, made by `readiness` before the call (a `--task-class` that differs from the run's own is refused). Without one the call refuses before changing anything, names the `readiness` command that clears the refusal and says which harnesses also check at edit time; nothing is evaluated by the call, so `--verification-plan`, `--scope` and `--invariants` are not read and a note says so. `handoff`, `complete-stage` and a `catch-up` that backfills the `do-plan` stage (row 134) read the feature's `plan.md` §2 "Constitution Check" table (`| Principle | Tier | Result | How |` with its `|---|` separator row; a table in a fence or an HTML comment does not count) against both constitution tiers: the base this runtime ships (its `core/shared` copy, else the install's `guidance/references/` beside `.doflow/runtime`) and `agent-docs/constitution.md` of the checkout holding the plan, a tier-2 principle replacing the base one with its id, each MUST unless its body says `**Tier:** SHOULD`. A MUST row marked FAIL, a MUST principle with no PASS/FAIL row, a header with no separator row (`constitution-unparseable`), a missing plan, or a constitution that cannot be read, has no `## Principles` section with a principle, or declares one id twice refuses the call before anything is written, naming each principle and the unblock (revise the plan, or a person amends the constitution with `/do-constitution`; there is no waiver); a SHOULD row marked FAIL, and a base MUST the overlay makes SHOULD (naming the overlay and its `**Version:**`), each print one `orchestrate: note:` line on stderr. A `catch-up` that would import the stage is refused the same way, and the roadmap loop records the refusal as `denied: plan-exit-refused`. The record and the run are looked for in this checkout, else in exactly one other checkout of the repository for the same feature, and a run found there is updated where it is. `--forced` requires `--note` and is refused on any action but `decide-gate`/`annotate`. `--expect-revision <n>` and `--via <label>` (row 162) are taken by `decide-gate` without `--policy` and by `close`, and refused as no-effect usage (exit 2) on any other action or with `--policy`. `--expect-revision` refuses the call as `stale-revision` (exit 1, nothing written) unless the run's `revision` is `n`, so a decision a person made while looking at one state is never applied to another; the check holds up to the write, which itself refuses a record that changed since it was read. `--via` takes `console` or `device.d-<12 hex>` (a bad value is exit 2) and is recorded as the entry's `by` in place of `manual` (the default); it names the channel the decision came through. The label is an unauthenticated assertion by whoever runs the command at the machine: the Console sets it from the authenticated actor (the loopback session or the paired device), the CLI trusts its caller; from a worker session (`DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE` set and not `main`, the guard of `worker --operator`) `--via` is refused, exit 1, under `--json` `{v:1,ok:false,error:'worker-session',current:null}`, and nothing is written. A `close` entry carries `by` as well (`manual` when no `--via`). Under `--json`, a `decide-gate` or `close` refused by state also prints `{v:1,ok:false,error,current}` on stdout, with `error` one of `no-run`, `not-awaiting-gate`, `gate-mismatch`, `run-terminal`, `concurrent-update`, `lock-lost`, `ambiguous-run`, `other-feature` or `stale-revision` and `current` the run's snapshot as it stands after the refusal, read again from its record (`null` when there is no run or it cannot be read); the stderr line and exit 1 are as before. Every transition is journaled — runs survive process death. The `current` object that `start`, `status` and `handoff` print carries the stage's `agent` (the archetype that works it, bound in `core/registry/workflows.json`); it is `null` for a gate, and for a run journalled before the binding existed until that run is restarted. Transition refusals exit 1 naming the expected node. `--mcp-outcome` (handoff only, repeatable, one server per value; any other action refuses it with exit 2) records what the stage did with an MCP server it names: `<server>=used`, `nothing-usable`, `unavailable` or `skipped:<reason>` (a reason of 1 to 120 characters, no comma). The handoff then adds `consultations` (the stored record: the servers the stage names, one outcome per server and where it came from; `null` for a deferred handoff) and `mcpWarnings` (an array of `{kind, server?, taskId?, text}`) to its `--json` snapshot. A named server with no outcome from the flag, from the `Tools used:` line of a task report, from evidence attributed to that server or from a brief record that expected it unreachable prints `[WARN] mcp: <skill> recorded no outcome for <server>; pass --mcp-outcome <server>=used\|nothing-usable\|skipped:<reason>\|unavailable` on stderr; an `unavailable` outcome prints `[INFO] mcp: <server> unavailable to <skill>; add it: <command>` on stdout; a malformed value is ignored with a warning. A standalone handoff prints the same lines and stores nothing. None of these changes the exit status. `decide-gate --policy <gate>` lets the runtime decide instead of a person: `--gate` must name the same gate, and `--decision`, `--forced` and any caller-named decider (`--by`) are refused with exit 2 before the run is loaded or changed (the run file lookup, and from a linked worktree the record sweep, happen first), as is the reserved decision channel `policy` on every other verb. The policy is read from develop's committed head, never from the feature branch (`core/registry/gates.json`, narrowed at most by `.doflow/gates.override.json` in the main checkout), and the facts each predicate names are gathered with a locator and sha256. When every predicate passes the gate is approved, and the decider is recorded as `autopilot` (a manual `decide-gate` records `manual`: a direct command, typed by a person or by an attended agent for them) with the policy id and digest and the inputs in the run history and as a `policy`-channel entry in the feature's decision register. A deny changes nothing: the run stays `AWAITING_GATE`, autopilot never rejects, and the answer carries `decision: "deny"` with `reason` and `reasons`, plus `rule` when the policy loaded and was evaluated (a policy-load or seed-status deny has no `rule`); the call still exits 0, so callers branch on `decision`, not on the exit code. With `autopilot.enabled` off on any layer the call denies `autopilot-disabled` before reading any fact. From a linked worktree the run is created and read in the main checkout's `.doflow/state` (see [Where a task's records live](#where-a-tasks-records-live)).  `list` takes no filter: it needs no `--task-id` and refuses every flag another action reads (`--task-id`, `--task-class`, `--gate`, `--decision`, `--policy`, `--forced`, `--note`, `--reason`, `--stage`, `--node`, `--verification-plan`, `--scope`, `--invariants`, `--result`, `--mcp-outcome`, `--calling-skill`, `--slug`, `--dry-run`) with exit 2. It reads every run in this checkout’s store and, when Git can list them, the other checkouts’ stores; sandbox and non-Git directories list the current store only. It shows one row per run, the copy `status` reads: a run’s `<slug>/<id>.json` wins over a flat `<id>.json` that names that feature, and this store’s copy wins over another checkout’s, so a shadowed copy is not listed. A flat `<id>.json` here that names no feature, or that is a link, is the file `status` stops at for any feature without its own `<slug>/<id>.json` here (a link it refuses, below), so it is its own row and hides every other checkout’s copy of that id. When this store has no copy and more than one other checkout has one, each of them is a row (same `featureSlug`, `taskId` and `origin`), and `status` refuses that run as ambiguous. `status` with no `--slug`, when no run matches the feature the checkout resolves, opens the one run of that id under any feature namespace, this checkout’s store first and then the other checkouts’, which is the run `list` shows for it; when more than one feature holds the id it exits 1 naming the candidate files and each `--slug`, and `--slug` picks one. `timeline`, `annotate` and `close` look across features the same way, so the task id `list` prints is enough for them. `annotate` and `close` also count this checkout's flat `<id>.json` run among the candidates (a flat run that records a feature is addressed by that feature's `--slug`; one that records none has no `--slug`, so the refusal says so), and refuse a run of another feature than the one this checkout's branch resolves, naming that feature and the `--slug` to pass, because `CLOSED` is terminal; where the checkout resolves no feature (for example on `develop`) the one run is acted on. Both print the feature the run belongs to (`Feature: <slug>`, and `featureSlug` with `--json`); the actions that move a run forward (`complete-stage`, `skip-stage`, `decide-gate`, `catch-up`, `handoff`) still need the feature to resolve. A run file over 1 MiB is unreadable (`io`). It never follows a symbolic link: a linked run file is listed as unreadable (`io`) and a linked feature folder is not entered. Every action that reads a run holds the same line: a run file or feature folder that is a link, or a run file that is not a regular file (a FIFO, a device) or is over 1 MiB, is refused with exit 1 (`Run file '<path>' is not a regular file inside the run store`) before anything is read from it. From a linked worktree, `list` and `status` read the main checkout’s store and move nothing; a record still left in the worktree is shown with origin `other` until the next write verb moves it. With `--json` it prints `{v:1,runs,unreadable}`. Each run row has `taskId`, `featureSlug`, `taskClass`, `workflowName`, `state`, `current`, `awaitingGate` (with `gateId` and `name`, without `prompt`), `progress` (`done` and `total`), `terminalStage` (`id`, `skill` and `kind` only), `revision`, `startedAt`, `updatedAt`, `origin` (`current` or `other`) and `runKey` (the record's `<worktreeKey>/<run>/<lineage>` run key, or `null` when the record has none, as with telemetry off or a solo lineage); `current` has `type`, `id`, `kind`, `mutatesSource` and `readinessTemplate`. Missing or invalid times are `null`. Unreadable rows have `taskId`, `featureSlug`, `origin` and `reason`, one of `unparseable`, `schema-version`, `shape`, `id-mismatch` or `io`; a record whose feature slug or current stage id is not a plain id (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`) is unreadable, so no record text reaches the text form. Runs order by `updatedAt` descending with null last; ties use `featureSlug` then `taskId` in bytewise ascending order, null slug first. Text prints `<state> <featureSlug|-> <taskId> <current.id|-> rev <n>` per run, then `unreadable: <n>` when non-zero. Exit 0 even for no runs or unreadable entries; exit 1 only for `cannot-list-checkouts`; exit 2 for usage. It writes nothing of its own. `timeline` (093 IC-012) reads one run's history for the Console's run view: it needs `--task-id` (`timeline requires --task-id`, exit 2) and takes `--slug` to pick the feature, resolves the run as `status` does, and refuses every other flag with exit 2 (`'<flag>' has no effect on action 'timeline'`). It writes nothing and never moves a record out of a linked worktree, as `list` and `status` do not. `--json` prints `{v:1,taskId,featureSlug,revision,startedAt,events,otherEvents}` with the events in journal order. An event is `at` (an ISO instant or null), `action` (`start`, `complete-stage`, `skip-stage`, `skip-gate`, `decide-gate`, `annotate`, `complete-run` or `close-run`; any other journal action is counted in `otherEvents`, not listed), `node` (an id or null), `outcome` (`passed`, `failed` or `unverified`, on `complete-stage` only), `decision` (`approve` or `reject`, on `decide-gate` only), `by` (on `decide-gate`: `autopilot` for a `--policy` decision, `manual` for a manual one, `console` or `device.d-<12 hex>` for a decision relayed by `--via`; on `close-run`: `manual`, `console` or `device.d-<12 hex>`; `null` for another action or a record written before the decider was kept), `forced`, and `synthetic` (the entry was backfilled, or its execution status is not `completed`). Notes, detail text, policy inputs, consultations and trace ids are never read, nor is any decider other than `by`. The text form prints `Timeline <taskId> <slug|-> rev <n>`, then `<at> <action> <node|-> <outcome|decision|->` per event with ` forced` and ` synthetic` appended, then `other events: <n>` when non-zero. A task with no run exits 1 (`No workflow run for task '<id>'`). Every action that prints a snapshot includes `revision`, 0 for journals written before revisions existed. |
| `doflow research-request --task-id <feature-id> [--slug <name>] [--action open\|list\|resolve] [--stage-id <stage-id>] [--question <text>] [--reason user-request\|detected-gap\|ideation] [--blocking true\|false] [--request-id <id>] [--outcome answered\|unresolved] [--claim-id <id>] [--evidence-id <id>] [--gap <text>] [--json]` | Stage-scoped external research on an existing feature run. `open` requires the current stage, a question and reason (detected gaps block by default); `list` is the default; `resolve` requires an open request and either a supported same-task claim with linked evidence for `answered` (extracted, fresh, not superseded, supporting that claim, and located by an http(s) URI or a resolvable file), or an explicit gap for `unresolved`. A blocking open/unresolved request refuses completion of its stage without moving the workflow cursor; research never approves a gate. The `ideation` reason is valid only on a feature discovery or design stage and is non-blocking by default, but an OPEN ideation request refuses completion of its stage until it is resolved. Web and Context7 retrieval remain in the harness, not this runtime. Requests, and the run and claims they are checked against, are read and written in the same root: the main checkout's `.doflow/state` from a linked worktree. |
| `doflow task-attempt --action claim\|finish\|release\|status\|start\|pause\|cancel\|resume\|inspect\|reopen\|report-usage --slug <name> [--task <id>] [--owner <name>] [--mode manual\|sequential\|subagent] [--owner-override <reason>] [--epoch <n>] [--reserve-tokens <n>] [--exec-worktree <path>] [--token <n>] [--result COMPLETED\|BLOCKED\|ABANDONED\|INCONCLUSIVE\|FAILED] [--evidence-id <id>] [--reason <text>] [--tokens <n> --tokens-basis measured\|estimated] [--by <name>] [--budget-attempts <n>] [--budget-tokens <n>] [--adopt-unowned] [--ack sha256:<hex>] [--json]` | The attempt ledger for a feature's plan tasks, one record per clone shared by its linked worktrees (`.doflow/state/task-attempts/<slug>.json` under the main worktree). `claim` grants one active attempt per task with a monotonic fencing token and refuses with `active-attempt`, `already-done`, `unknown-task`, `unmet-phase` (a task in an earlier phase has no COMPLETED attempt), `unmet-dependency`, `dependency-held`, `budget-exhausted`, `cancelled`, `run-paused`, `run-cancelled`, `reservation-required`, `aggregate-unaccounted` or `aggregate-exhausted` in every plan; concurrent claims yield exactly one `claimed`. `finish` and `release` apply only to the active token and otherwise return `stale`; `finish` also refuses with `plan-changed` when the task's `plan.md` line changed since the claim, and `finish --result COMPLETED` needs `--evidence-id` naming an active, non-stale `test-result` whose observation exited 0, is bound to the feature slug or the task id, and was observed no earlier than the attempt started. `status` reports `complete` only when every plan task has a COMPLETED attempt; a task ticked `[x]` without one is listed in `unclaimedChecked`. There is no lease or heartbeat: a crashed claimer is recovered by an explicit `release`. `orchestrate` refuses to complete the `do-execute-plan` stage while a ledger exists and is incomplete, and records the stage as `unverified` when the feature has no ledger. With a `**Task graph:** v1` plan, `claim` also refuses `owner-mismatch` (unless `--owner-override <reason>`), `resume-required` (an `--epoch` other than the run's) and `worker-session`; `--reserve-tokens` holds tokens against the run's budget and `--exec-worktree` (an existing directory, else exit 2 `usage`; 080 DEC-023) names where the attempt runs. `finish` and `release` take `--tokens <n> --tokens-basis measured\|estimated` together, as the caller reports them, and `finish --result FAILED` needs `--reason`. `report-usage --task --token --tokens --tokens-basis` sets the usage of an ended attempt that has none and refreshes its run's totals, in any plan; it is fenced by the token alone, so a worker may report, and it is the remedy for `aggregate-unaccounted` (080 DEC-018). It returns `usage-recorded`, and refuses `unknown-task`, `stale no-attempt` (no attempt has that token), `active-attempt` (finish it instead), `stale attempt-cancelled` (its reservation already counts as estimated) or `usage-present` (it never overwrites). The run controls act on the caller's run, each needs `--by <name>`, and each refuses `worker-session` in every plan and `identity-solo`; `resume` refuses `identity-solo`, and `worker-session` in a graph plan only (080 DEC-022): `start` (optional `--budget-attempts`, `--budget-tokens`, each at least 1; refuses `run-exists`), `pause --reason` (claims refuse until an acknowledged resume; active attempts still finish; refuses `control-unchanged`, `run-cancelled`), `cancel --reason` (one attempt with `--task --token`, else the whole run, with `--adopt-unowned` to take attempts that carry no run; marks attempts and ends no process; one attempt refuses `no-active-attempt`, `token-mismatch` or `foreign-run`, an already-cancelled run `control-unchanged`) and `reopen --task --token --reason` (the done attempt becomes superseded; refuses `not-done`, `token-mismatch`, `run-cancelled`). `resume` without `--ack` writes nothing and returns `ack-required` with the report and its digest; `resume --ack <digest> --by <name> [--reason]` resumes with a new epoch, or refuses `report-changed` with the fresh report when the digest no longer matches. `inspect` is read-only: the graph, every task's state and budget, and the caller's run with recomputed totals. Each entry of `tasks[]` in the `--json` body ends with `attempts` (093 IC-013), the task's attempts in ledger order as `token`, `owner`, `mode`, `state` (`active`, `finished`, `released`, `cancelled` or `superseded`), `startedAt`, `endedAt` (the finish time, else the release time, else the cancel time, else null) and `result` (one of the five `--result` values, else null), followed by `worktreeKey`, `run` and `lineage` (093 IC-018 c), the three parts of the attempt's stored run key `<worktreeKey>/<run>/<lineage>`: the 16-hex hash of the checkout that claimed it, the run id and the lineage. All three are null when the record has no run key of that shape. Reason text, evidence ids, the execution worktree, usage and trace data are not in `attempts`, and the other keys of an entry keep their names and order. The Console's run and Sessions views read it. A flag added in 080 given to an action that does not take it is exit 2 `usage`; a pre-080 flag (`--task`, `--token`, `--reason`, `--owner`, `--mode`, `--result`, `--evidence-id`) given to `claim`, `finish`, `release` or `status` is not checked against the action, so one that action does not take is ignored (the ones it takes are used), while on every other action one it does not take is exit 2 `usage` (`--task` and `--token` are taken by `cancel`, `reopen` and `report-usage`, `--reason` by `pause`, `cancel`, `reopen` and `resume`); a non-numeric `--token` is exit 2 `usage` on every action; and an integer flag is digits only (`5abc` and `-1` are usage; `--epoch` and the budgets also refuse 0). Exits 0 applied (`claimed`, `applied`, `released`, `started`, `paused`, `cancelled`, `resumed`, `reopened`, `usage-recorded`) or a `status`/`inspect` read; 1 `refused`, `stale` or `ack-required`, with the disposition in the JSON on stdout; 2 malformed input or an unreadable store or plan (a torn or future-version file is left unchanged). Exit 1 with no JSON on stdout is a parser usage error (for example `--reason` with no value, or a flag a pre-080 runtime does not know): nothing ran. Callers branch on the JSON `disposition` or `error`, never on the exit code alone |
