| `doflow worker --action discover\|probe\|describe\|select\|run\|status\|cancel\|evidence\|start-session\|start-options\|send\|steer\|stop [--slug <name>] [--harness <id>] [--need <id>[,<id>…]]… [--model-check <id> [--budget-tokens <n>]] [--edit] [--task <id> --token <n>] [--child-of <w-id> --request <d-id>.c<n> --exec-worktree <path>] [--prompt-file <path>] [--timeout-seconds <n>] [--wait] [--worker-run <id>] [--checks <path>] [--out <path>] [--docs-root <dir>] [--by <name>] [--operator --via console\|device.d-<12 hex>] [--reason <text>] [--project-key <16 hex> --worktree-key <16 hex> [--model <id>] [--provider <name>] [--effort <level>] --entry do\|none --request-id <id> --stdin] [--json]` | The harness execution adapter: a one-shot, read-only external headless worker run on a task claimed through `task-attempt`, and, since row 165, the start of a main session (`start-session`, `start-options`: see [Started sessions](#started-sessions)). `--action` is required. `discover` and `probe` report each registered harness's binary and readiness (`probe` takes optional `--harness`, `--need` and `--model-check <id>` with `--budget-tokens <n>`, at least 1, and writes `.doflow/state/worker-probes/<harness>.json`); `describe --harness` (required) prints one harness's declared worker capabilities; `select` picks an eligible harness for `--need` (candidate gaps include `worker-disabled` when omitted from `workers.enabled`, `binding:invalid` when tier model binding is invalid, and `model-flag:unevidenced` when the worker model flag is unevidenced; when `workers.enabled` resolves invalid, every candidate is held as `workers-enabled-invalid`); `run --harness --task --token --prompt-file [--timeout-seconds 1-14400] [--wait]` starts a supervised run recorded in `.doflow/state/worker-runs/<slug>.json` (refusing `worker-disabled` with exit 1 when the harness is not enabled), its output masked into `.doflow/state/worker-output/<runId>.out.log` and `.err.log`; `status` and `cancel` name the run by `--worker-run w-<12 hex>` or by `--task --token`, and `cancel` needs `--by` and `--reason`. `cancel --operator --worker-run <id> --via <label> --reason <text>` (row 162) is the one way to cancel a worker run that another run or session started: it skips only the foreign-run check, takes `--via` (`console` or `device.d-<12 hex>`) in place of `--by` and `--task`/`--token` (either is exit 2), is refused `operator-in-run` when the caller is a worker session or inside a workflow run, and records `cancel.source: operator` with `cancel.by` set to the `--via` label; `unknown-run`, `run-ended` and `cancel-in-progress` are unchanged. The flag is for a person at the machine, or a paired `control` device acting through the Console (owner ruling DEC-007). `status` shows `cancelBy` (`{by, source, requestedAt}` or `null`) in every phase. `evidence --harness --out` either runs a declared free `--checks` file and records the version and matched help lines, or records an ended `--worker-run` (with optional `--slug`); a run is refused unless it has stdout and a terminal result event. The evidence JSON omits prompt and summary text. A checks file may also hold doc checks, read from `--docs-root <dir>` (see [Doc checks in worker evidence](#doc-checks-in-worker-evidence)); `--docs-root` is accepted only by `evidence`, and only with `--checks`. `--edit` (describe, select, run) uses the edit baseline: `run --edit` needs the claimed attempt's execution worktree to be a dispatch attempt worktree on its `doflow-attempt/<slug>/<d-id>` branch, and the run record gains `edit`. `run --child-of <w-id> --request <d-id>.c<n> --exec-worktree <path>` starts a read-only child of a live run on the same `--task --token`, in that run's child worktree; it refuses `depth-exceeded`, `granter-mismatch`, `exec-worktree-missing`, `exec-worktree-not-child`, `request-mismatch` (`--request` is not the child worktree's name) or `unknown-request` (with a dispatch run, `--request` names no `requested` spawn request of the granter's dispatch), and cancelling a child never cancels the attempt. `--slug` (run, status, cancel) is resolved from the branch when absent. No action prints an output file's content; status returns its path. `start-session` and `start-options` start and read a session, not a worker on a claimed task: their flags, checks, refusals and limits are under [Started sessions](#started-sessions). `send`, `steer` and `stop` (row 170) put a message to a running started session: see [Talking to a started session](#talking-to-a-started-session). `login-state` reads each harness's login answer (see the next row). `run` and `dispatch` refuse the slug `started-sessions` (the started sessions' run store) with `slug-reserved`. A flag given to an action that does not take it is exit 2 `usage`. Exits 0 `discovered`, `probed`, `described`, `selected`, `started`, `cancelled`, `status` or `recorded` (`send`, `steer` and `stop` answer `{ok, ...}`: 0 for `ok: true`, 1 for `ok: false`); 1 `refused` or `none-eligible`, with the disposition in the JSON on stdout; 2 `{ ok: false, error }` with `usage`, `store-unreadable` or `registry-invalid`. Exit 1 with no JSON on stdout is a parser usage error: nothing ran. Callers branch on the JSON `disposition` or `error`, never on the exit code alone |
| `doflow worker --action conform --harness <id> [--json]` | Check one worker descriptor against a fake harness program, and name each part that fails (row 171; the last step of [Adding a harness](adding-a-harness.md)). `--harness` is required: a built-in id, or an active package worker as `<package>.<local>`. Ten parts are checked in order: `binary`, `one-shot`, `prompt-transport`, `model-flag`, `provider`, `effort`, `main-session`, `control-channel`, `stream` and `login`. `provider` and `effort` bind a sample value (`conform-provider-1`, or the effort entry's first level) with the model, and check that the fake received the composed argv and that the value reached it once: a flag transport's flag once, an id transport's composed id once. The `control-channel` part (row 170) starts the channel argv under the fake with stdin kept open and writes one follow-up, one steer (when the descriptor has a steer record) and one stop, each carrying a fixed text with quotes, a backslash, `$`, a line feed and non-ASCII characters; it fails unless the fake received exactly the bytes the core frames for each record, the turn reads `working` after the follow-up and the steer and `waiting` after the stop, every answer (when `answer` is declared) carries its record's id and reads accepted, and the fake exits within `close.graceSeconds` after stdin is closed (a fake that stays alive fails the part). A part is `unchecked` when its descriptor entry is not `supported` (`not-declared`) or when a part it needs failed (`depends-on <part>`). The `stream` part builds the smallest event for each rule of the worker's field map and checks the status and reason the parser gives, a rule hidden by an earlier rule included; a worker on a code parser leaves `stream` `unchecked` (`code-parser`). The `login` part runs the login recipe against the fake: it refuses a widening or secret-printing flag in `argv` or `providers.argv`, feeds each answer rule the smallest output that meets its `when` and exit, and checks the state and reason the runner gives (a shadowed rule is named), an exit no rule names reads `unknown exit <n>`, a fake that sleeps past the bound reads `unknown timeout`, and each expiry sign, with `{provider}` set, must be found by the expiry matcher in a fake failure summary. Its passed reason is `<n> answers, <m> signs, providers <yes\|no>`. Safety: the fake is the only executable on `PATH`, spawned by its absolute path with no shell, for at most 10 seconds each time, with an environment built from nothing (`PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG` and conform's own variables), so no caller variable reaches the child. The real binary and a package's own program never run. The scratch directory `doflow-conform-*` is made in the OS temp directory and removed afterwards. It writes no telemetry and no store. Output: one line per part, `<part>  <passed\|failed\|unchecked>  <reason>`, then `conform: <id> ok` or `conform: <id> failed: <part>, …`; `--json` prints `{ok, harness, origin: "builtin"\|"package", parser: {id, kind: "code"\|"field-map"\|null}, parts: [{part, result, reason}], failed}`, at most 4 KiB. Exit 0 when every part that was checked passed, 1 when at least one part failed (each is named), 2 for a usage error, `unknown-harness`, `platform-unsupported` (win32) or `scratch-failed` |
| `doflow worker --action login-state [--harness <id>] [--json]` | Read each harness's login answer (row 172; see [Login state](#login-state)). Read-only: it reads the registry, the active packages and the `harness-login` store, starts no process and writes nothing. `--harness` names one built-in id or `<package>.<local>`; without it every worker in the catalogue's order is answered, at most 32. `--json` prints `{harnesses: LoginAnswer[]}`; the text form is one line per harness, `<id>  <cell>`, where the cell is the one doctor prints (for example `unknown (no login check: <why>)`). Exits 0, or 2 for a usage fault or a `--harness` the catalogue does not list (the error names the known ids). |
| `doflow models --action sync\|list\|describe\|bind\|unbind [--harness <id>] [--tier light\|standard\|frontier\|orchestrator] [--model <id>] [--provider <name>] [--effort <level>] [--allow-unlisted] [--json]` | Model catalogue and per-tier model binding across the 7 AI coding harnesses (`claude`, `codex`, `opencode`, `pi`, `copilot`, `kiro`, `antigravity`). `sync` probes harnesses, writes `.doflow/state/models/catalogue.json`, runs each harness's declared login recipe (see [Login state](#login-state)) and records the answer in the `harness-login` store, and records detected mode (`subscription` vs `api-key`) and derived billing (`free` vs `metered`) from that answer's billing (a harness with no recipe, or with no logged-in answer, reads `unverified` and `unknown`) and each harness's offered models, read from the harness's own listing command (`worker.modelList` in `core/registry/harnesses.json`: `agy models`, `opencode models`, `pi --list-models`; claude, codex, copilot and kiro record none, so their `offeredModels` is `[]` with a `modelList.reason`). A listing keeps what the recipe says its output holds: with `modelList.columns` (pi's table) each model carries its `provider`, `contextTokens`, `maxOutputTokens`, `reasoning`, `imageInput` and `effortLevels`, as the columns give them; with `modelList.providerInId` (opencode's `<provider>/<model>` lines) the provider is split from the id at the descriptor's provider separator. A model is one entry per provider and id; a column that is missing or unreadable leaves the field out, and `modelList.columnFaults` counts the unreadable cells. A failed listing keeps the previous list with `modelList.stale: true` and `sync` still exits 0. `list` prints one row per installed harness (or any harness with a binding): `light`, `standard`, `frontier`, `orchestrator` (each a binding cell, or `null` for the harness default or an `unresolved:<reason>` string when `workers.models` cannot be read; the cell is `{model, provider, effort, check: {model, effort}}` plus `effortMayLower: true` when an effort is bound and the descriptor says the harness may lower it, plus `refused: <reason>` (`bindingGap`'s reason, such as `effort:unsupported`) when dispatch would refuse the binding, and the checks are computed from the catalogue each time it is read: `check.model` is `listed`, `not-listed`, `several-providers` (several providers list the id and none is bound) or `unchecked` (no list), `check.provider` is `unchecked` only when a provider is bound and the listing carries no provider for the id (the model then matches on the id alone), `check.effort` is `null` with no effort, else `listed`, `not-offered` or `unchecked`; the text form writes the cell as `<model> (provider <p>, effort <e>) [notes]` (a refused cell ends its notes with `dispatch refuses it (<reason>)`), `harness default`, or `unresolved`, one function for every place a binding is shown) and `orchestratorSource` (`binding`, `main-session`, `frontier-binding` or `harness-default`), the main session's `primary`, then the observed models with enablement against `models.enabled` (an entry matches a model by its bare id or, on a harness that puts the provider in the id (`id-prefix`), by the composed `provider/id` the user would type, in `list`, `describe` and `doctor` alike) (`--json` is `{ primary, bindings, harnesses, models }`; the text table has the columns `HARNESS`, `MODEL`, `PROVIDER`, `CONTEXT`, `MAX OUT`, `REASONING`, `IMAGES`, `LOGIN` and `STATUS`, and `-` is a field the harness did not list, which the footer line says). `describe --model <id> [--provider <p>]` inspects metadata and offering harnesses for a model, with each offering's listed columns; `--provider` keeps only the offerings under that provider. `bind --harness <id> --tier <t> --model <id> [--provider <p>] [--effort <e>]` writes `workers.models.<harness>.<tier>` in the user settings file through the settings writer (lock, `<file>.config.bak`): the plain id when neither part is given, else `{"model": <id>, "provider": <p>, "effort": <e>}` with only the parts given. Every refusal exits 1 and writes nothing, with `{ok: false, error, message}` in `--json`: `provider-unsupported` and `effort-unsupported` (the harness's descriptor `provider` or `effort` entry is not `supported`: `unknown`, `unsupported` and absent are refused alike, and the message gives the entry's `reason`), `effort-unknown-level` (the descriptor lists its levels and the effort is not one; `levels` is in the JSON), `ambiguous-model` (the id is listed by several providers and no `--provider` was given; `providers` is in the JSON), `unlisted-model` (the id, with the given provider, is outside the harness's catalogue list; a listing with no provider for the id matches on the id and the bind is written with `provider unchecked`: the three closest ids in `closest`, unless `--allow-unlisted`) and `effort-not-offered` (the listed model's `effortLevels` lack the effort, or it lists `reasoning` as no and the effort is not the descriptor's `offLevel`). A provider or effort that breaks its pattern (a letter or digit first, then letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`; at most 64 and 32 characters) exits 2. A harness with no list accepts the id and says it was not checked; an effort with no list for the model is accepted and reads `effort unchecked`. `--json` is `{ok, action, harness, tier, model, provider, effort, checked, check, changed, file}` (`checked` is `listed`, `unchecked` or `unlisted-allowed`). A slot that already holds a plain id several providers list keeps running as typed and is shown with the `several providers list it` note; only a new bind is refused. Without `--model` on a terminal it syncs that harness and reads a number from a printed list (with `--provider`, only that provider's rows; the picked row's provider is written only where the harness takes one); without a terminal it exits 2. `unbind --harness <id> --tier <t>` removes the slot (the key itself when the map empties). Neither action runs a harness CLI other than the listing command. |
| `doflow dispatch --action start\|step\|run\|submit\|spawn\|pause\|inspect\|cancel\|takeover\|resume [--slug <name>] [--by <name>] [--reason <text>] [--concurrency <n>] [--mode auto\|sequential\|manual] [--fallback off\|ask\|auto] [--ladder <id>[,<id>…]] [--billing-cap free] [--verify-argv <json>] [--verify-seconds <n>] [--attempt-seconds <n>] [--reserve-tokens <n>] [--budget-attempts <n>] [--budget-tokens <n>] [--spawn-grant <json>] [--harness <id>] [--need <id>[,<id>…]]… [--max-seconds <n>] [--dispatch <d-id>] [--status success\|failure] [--summary <text>] [--blocker <text>]… [--tokens <n> --tokens-basis measured\|estimated] [--prompt-file <path>] [--request-id <d-id>.c<n>] [--task <id>] [--ack sha256:<hex>] [--json]` | The dispatch and integration loop: runs a feature's `**Task graph:** v1` plan through claimed attempts, each in its own attempt worktree under `.doflow/state/dispatch-worktrees/`, and integrates verified attempts onto an integration branch the user merges; it never writes the caller's checkout or branches, merges or pushes. The run is recorded in `.doflow/state/dispatch/<slug>.json`, handoff and result envelopes in `.doflow/state/dispatch-envelopes/`, and verification output in `.doflow/state/worker-output/`. `--action` is required; `--slug` is resolved from the branch when absent (a worker in an attempt worktree passes it). `start --by` (with optional `--concurrency` 1 to 4, default 2; `--mode`, default `auto`; `--verify-argv` a JSON array of 1 to 64 strings; `--verify-seconds` 1 to 14400, default 600; `--attempt-seconds` 1 to 14280; `--reserve-tokens`; `--budget-attempts`; `--budget-tokens`, which needs `--reserve-tokens` of at least 1; `--spawn-grant`; `--harness`; `--need`; `--billing-cap free`) creates the integration worktree and the 080 run (`start` with `--harness` or `--ladder` naming a disabled worker refuses `worker-disabled` with exit 1 `refused`; `--mode sequential` without `primary` in `workers.enabled` refuses `no-enabled-worker`). `--fallback off|ask|auto` on `start` sets the run mode (default from `dispatch.fallback`); `--ladder <id>[,<id>…]` takes registry harness ids once each, at most 8, and cannot accompany `--harness`; absent `--ladder`, registry order applies. `resume --fallback auto` accepts the pending quota proposals once: it clears each `quota` hold and that task steps once, only to the rung that was proposed (if that rung is no longer the next eligible one the task is held again with a new proposal), while the run's mode stays unchanged, so a later quota holds again; `resume --fallback off|ask` sets the run's mode and withdraws accepted proposals. A quota failure (class `quota`) is recognized only when a failed run's masked summary contains a registry-declared `quotaSignals` value; codex signals are `usage limit` (`basis: transcript`, backed by `core/worker-evidence/codex/2026-11-05-quota-transcript.json`, a session-rollout capture rather than `exec --json` output) and `rate limit`, `quota exceeded`, `too many requests` and `resource_exhausted` (listed in `unverifiedSignals`, always `basis: unverified`), so fallback lines say `signals unverified` when an unverified signal matched. Quota releases the 080 attempt instead of finishing it, so it never counts toward the repeat-to-BLOCKED rule, but still spends one attempt from the task's budget; two quota releases exhaust a two-attempt budget. Every other failure class is unchanged. The harness that reports quota is exhausted for every task for the rest of the run. In `ask` (the default), a task is held with the next rung proposed; in `off`, it is held without a proposal; in `auto`, light and standard work moves to the next rung. These rules apply to every move off a quota-ed rung, including one that became eligible again. A task whose plan line says `tier: frontier` (the task-line tier wins over the owner's, as for briefs), a frontier-tier owner (`delivery-lead` and `reviewer` are `frontier` in `models.json` `dispatchTiers.byOwner`), `reviewer` (the review hold: `REVIEW_OWNERS` in `src/runtime/dispatch/loop.ts` is `reviewer`), and owners absent from `models.json` `dispatchTiers.byOwner` (case-insensitive) are held `frontier-no-step` in every mode. A `quality-guardian` task at tier `standard` (its `byOwner` tier) may step; at tier `frontier` it is held, and `plan-check` warns about it (the `review-owner` warning). With no rung left, a task is held `ladder-exhausted`, never moved to `sequential`. Holds include `no-enabled-worker` (when `inSessionGate` is false and no eligible worker is enabled) and `workers-enabled-invalid` (when `workers.enabled` resolves invalid); candidate gaps include `worker-disabled`, `binding:invalid`, `model-flag:unevidenced`, and `protected-path:<path> (<file>)`, which a task gets when a declared `files:` entry starts, at the workspace root, with a path in the harness's `worker.isolatedEdit.protectedPaths` (codex: `.git`, `.agents`, `.codex`, `.aws`, from a manual probe on codex-cli 0.164.0 that tried 9 paths, so the list may be incomplete; a nested `sub/.git` does not match): that harness is skipped for the task, which runs on the next eligible rung or sequentially instead of failing `worker-denied`. Holds do not wait for input or admit the task again in the run; they appear as decide-phase gaps with cause `quota`, and the run ends `blocked` when nothing else can progress. `frontier-no-step` and `ladder-exhausted` holds persist within the run and stop blocking completion once their task succeeds outside dispatch through `task-attempt`. Each quota, step and hold prints `fallback <kind> <task> <from> -> <to|none> (<reason>; mode <mode>; signals <basis>)` on `step` and `run`, is kept in `fallback.events` (newest 200; older events counted in `eventsDropped`) and quota or step events are copied to the dispatch record. Text `inspect` prints `hold <task> <reason> from <from> proposed <to|none>` per hold; `inspect --json` includes `fallback`. `--billing-cap` takes only `free` (anything else is exit 2 `usage`), is stored in the run and applies to every later selection. The cap judges each worker by its effective billing, read once per selection pass from the registry value (`worker.billing`; every shipped harness declares `unknown`, G58) and the `workers.billing` record, a global-only setting the owner writes in `<config home>/settings.json` (see [Settings keys](#settings-keys); `config` never writes it). A record `free` lifts only a registry `unknown`; a record `metered` narrows a registry `free` or `unknown` to `metered`; a registry `metered` is never lifted; a worker with no entry keeps its registry value. A worker whose effective billing is not `free` fails the static gate with requirement `billing`, result `failed`, reason `billing:<value>` and a `source` field (`registry` or `user-record`) saying where the value came from, and with no eligible worker the run falls back to `sequential` and the selection records `reason: no-eligible-worker`. A candidate that clears the gate carries `billing: { value: "free", source, asserted }` (`asserted` only when the source is `user-record`). A record that is malformed, unreadable, or names an id that is not a registry harness is invalid as a whole: every worker then fails with reason `billing:record-invalid`, source `user-record` and a `recordReason` (for example `unknown-worker:<id>`). `config list` exits 1 for a shape or value fault in the record but not for an id that is not a registry harness, which only dispatch checks. A project or local `workers.billing` is ignored. An uncapped run never reads the record. `start --billing-cap free` adds `billingRecord: { state: "absent"|"set"|"invalid", reason, workers: [{ harness, billing, asserted, basis? }] }` to its JSON and, in text, one line `billing: <id> <free|metered> (user record, asserted <date>)` per recorded worker, or `billing: user record invalid (<reason>): no worker counts as free`; an absent record prints no line. Quota is unaffected and stays with the fallback ladder. `step` runs one pass and `run [--max-seconds 1-14400]` repeats passes; both take optional `--by`. `submit --dispatch --status --summary` (`--summary` is required; with repeatable `--blocker`, `--tokens --tokens-basis`) records a worker's result. `spawn --prompt-file --reserve-tokens` requests a child run within the dispatch's grant, and `spawn --request-id` reads one request and writes nothing. `pause --by --reason`, `cancel --by --reason`, `takeover --by --reason --task` and `inspect` act on the caller's run; `resume` without `--ack` writes nothing and returns `ack-required` with the report and its digest, and `resume --ack <digest> --by` resumes. Every action but `spawn` first refuses `identity-solo` or `worker-session`, and every action but `start` refuses `no-dispatch-run` or `foreign-run`. A flag given to an action that does not take it is exit 2 `usage`. Exits 0 `started`, `stepped`, `ran`, `submitted`, `spawn-requested`, `spawn-status`, `paused`, `inspected`, `cancelled`, `taken-over` or `resumed` (`step` and `run` exit 0 whenever the pass ran, the loop's outcome is in the JSON, and `run` interrupted by SIGINT or SIGTERM exits 130 or 143); 1 `refused`, `stale` or `ack-required`, with the disposition in the JSON on stdout; 2 `{ ok: false, error, message }` with `usage`, `store-unreadable`, `plan-unreadable`, `plan-malformed` or `registry-invalid`. Exit 1 with no JSON on stdout is a parser usage error: nothing ran. Without `--json` each action prints one summary line (`inspect` prints one line per dispatch, then the run, verification and measurement lines and one line per fallback hold; `step` and `run` add one line per fallback event) and never envelope, output or argv content. Callers branch on the JSON `disposition`, `outcome` or `error`, never on the exit code alone |
| `doflow retrieve --query <text> [-k N] [--json]` | BM25 search over the installed guidance tree: `$DOFLOW_CONFIG_DIR/guidance/` when that is set, else the nearest `.doflow/guidance/` in the working directory or a directory above it, else `~/.doflow/guidance/` (a project `.doflow/` with no `guidance/` is skipped). It is served from an auto-refreshing content-addressed index under `index/guidance/` beside the chosen tree — only files whose sha256 changed since the last build are re-chunked. Returns ranked path, section title, score, and a snippet; `-k` caps results (default 5, max 25). Lexical-only by design until a dense provider is declared in models.json: an absent embedding provider degrades to lexical, never to a silent half-answer. Exits 1 when no guidance tree is installed |
| `doflow retrieve --scope code --query <text> [--profile lexical-only\|hybrid] [--root <project>] [--model-path <local-path>] [--task-id <id> --need <intent> --slug <name>] [--json]` | Searches the project code corpus. The default profile is `lexical-only`; `hybrid` requests Semble 0.7.0 for conceptual queries, with lexical fallback on provider/index faults. Exact queries stay lexical. The bounded corpus is admitted once and returned spans are validated against live source before snippets or evidence are delivered. Task-bound retrieval requires `--task-id` and one declared `--need` together; the active attempt and query reference are read from the existing stores. An ad hoc search writes no evidence. Empty results mean no match in the attempted scope, never repository-wide absence. |
| `doflow retrieve --scope code --index-action build\|refresh [--root <project>] --model-path <local-path> [--json]` | Explicitly builds the project's initial local Semble index or refreshes a compatible index. `--root` defaults to the current directory. A verified absolute model directory is required: `--model-path` must name it for `build` and `refresh` (`SEMBLE_MODEL_PATH` is read only by hybrid queries). Python packages and model files must already exist locally. Calls run with offline model-library flags and project-local bounded cache/statistics paths; DoFlow never downloads a model. Failed lifecycle output includes a repair instruction. Correct the cause, then run an explicit bounded `build` to repair an incomplete or incompatible index; queries never rebuild or refresh it. The default remains lexical-only while hybrid promotion is incomplete and T-005/T-006 have no separate paid evidence. |
| `doflow advise --action ask\|outcome\|configure\|report [--kind <id>] [--json] [-g]` | `ask` (IC-008) sends a named decision question or a questions file to the configured local model and returns its advisory pick; `outcome` (IC-009) records the caller's observed outcome for a retained pick and works locally even when advice is off; `configure` (IC-021) enables or disables the user setting, or disables a project setting / chooses its model after the user has enabled advice; `report` prints counts, agreement and latency from the advice records, with the n of every figure, and makes no model call and no write (see [Decision model: report, evidence and shadow kinds](#decision-model-report-evidence-and-shadow-kinds)). `act` takes effect for a kind only when its model has a matching passing verdict in `core/registry/advice-verdicts.json`. The shipped file holds none, so a kind set to `act` runs as shadow. `ask` with `--kind` goes through the one dispatcher path and runs in the mode `advice.kinds.<kind>` and the rollout profile's cap give it: `off` makes no call and prints `status: off`; `shadow` records the pick through a detached child and prints `status: shadow`, never the answer; `act` prints the answer only for a kind past the bench bar. A kind with no `advice.kinds.<kind>` setting records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise. A question with no `--kind` has no kind setting: the profile cap alone decides between off, shadow and the direct ask, which `timeoutMs` bounds. |
| `doflow model-role --role <id> [--exclude <id,id>…] [--exclude-family <name[,name…]>…] [--json]` | Resolve an abstract model role (`core/registry/models.json`: `triage`, `reasoning`, `review`) to ranked provider candidates. Ordering: installed backend first, then preferred-tier match (`tiers` on the provider), then fallback tier; `--exclude` drops provider ids. `--exclude-family` accepts repeated flags or comma-separated lowercase identifiers declared in the registry's `families`; an empty list or a name absent from the registry exits 2 and lists known families. The `unknown` family cannot be excluded because it represents missing knowledge. A provider is dropped only when every declared family is excluded; a known overlap with an excluded family is labelled `overlaps-author`, even when the provider also declares an unknown family. A provider with unknown family data and no known overlap is kept and labelled `unverified`; a provider with no overlap or unknown family is labelled `cross-family`. JSON candidates carry this as `familyRelation`, with `familyVerified` true only for `cross-family`. Same-family providers are listed as excluded, not candidates. In JSON family mode, `familyExcluded` lists the provider ids dropped by family filtering. Availability means the provider's backend CLI resolved on PATH; the verb always probes, so each candidate is `yes` or `no`, and a provider with no local CLI is `no`. Advisory only: DoFlow binds no model for the harness's own work; binding stays with each harness's native selection. When the user configures a local decision model, the runtime calls it for advisory picks only, through `advise`, `classify --request` and `brief-tools` |
| `doflow route --intent <id> [--query <text>] [--harness <id>] [--check] [--json]` | Resolve an information need to a provider that is healthy on this machine, with the concrete command or MCP tool to run. `mcpTool` is the Claude form `mcp__<server>__<tool>` by default. With `--harness`, it is that harness's `capabilities.mcp.toolName` template from `core/registry/harnesses.json`; a harness with no verified template gets `mcp: {server, tool}` and no `mcpTool`. An unknown harness id exits 2. Exits 1 when no provider can answer |
| `doflow-run task-brief --task=<id> [--harness=<id>] [--slug=<slug>] [--json]` / `--group=<phase>:<owner> --tasks=<id,…>` (`do-task-brief.sh`) | Compose the brief a dispatched implementer reads from the plan, requirement and design (the single-task and the group form). `--harness` names the harness the brief is for, so tool names follow what that harness registers. The brief ends with the tier, tools and report-format block that `brief-tools` prints (`## Tier and tools` and `## Report format` after `## Verification bar` for a task; `### Tier and tools` and `### Report format` closing `## Shared context` for a group), and the report is asked to carry a `Tools used:` line just before its final `Status:` line. Beside the brief it writes a `*-brief.json` record (`task-<id>-brief.json`, `group-<phase>-<owner>-brief.json`) holding the tier, the expected MCP servers and whether each is reachable, and the tools shown; a later handoff reads the reports it names. `--json` gains `record` (the record path, or `null`). If `brief-tools` is missing, exits non-zero or prints something unparseable, the brief says `Not generated: <reason>. Name the tier and tools in the dispatch.` in that place, carries the static report-format block, lists `brief-tools: <reason>` under `missing`, writes no record, and the exit status is unchanged |
| `doflow brief-tools --owner <archetype> [--tier <light\|standard\|frontier>] [--harness <id>] [--heading-level 2\|3] [--slug <slug>] [--json]` | Prints the rules, tier, tools and report-format block of a dispatch brief. The rules block ("Rules that apply to you") is the `mustCarry` key of `core/registry/rules.json`, generated from the RULES.md lines marked `<!-- must-carry -->` (G78); `do-task-brief.sh` calls it, no skill does. The tier is the task line's `--tier` when valid, else the archetype's entry in `models.json` `dispatchTiers`, else its default. Tools are one row per `routes.json` intent naming the provider that is healthy on this machine, or the fallback, or that none exists; each is shown as the target harness's MCP tool name where the harness has that server, else as the command line. It also lists the MCP servers the run's current stage expects, and states any the target harness cannot reach. The block ends with the `Tools used:` line the report is asked to carry just before its final `Status: <status>` line. `--json` prints `{markdown, record}`. Writes nothing, except that with `--tasks <ids>` and the local decision model on it records the advisory tier pick, or a miss, in the advice store (under shadow, as for `classify`, which is also what an unset `advice.kinds.tier` gives while a decision model is configured, the pick is recorded by a detached child the verb does not wait for and is not printed, and `record.advice` is `{kind: tier, status: shadow, reason}`); the brief generator falls back to a static block when the verb fails. Exits 0 on every outcome except a usage error (missing `--owner`, `--heading-level` other than 2 or 3), which exits 2, and an unexpected failure, which exits 1 |
| `doflow claim --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--action list\|add\|link\|retract\|supersede] [--statement <text>] [--claim-id <id>] [--evidence-id <id>] [--replaced-by <id>] [--relation supports\|contradicts] [--json]` | Record a proposition, link evidence to it, retire it, or list what is recorded. A new claim starts as a `hypothesis` and can only become `supported` through linked evidence — there is deliberately no way to assert one supported. Linking an evidence id the ledger does not hold is refused (exit 2) rather than graded, so record the evidence first. `--action retract` and `--action supersede` move a claim to a terminal state so an obsolete conclusion stops blocking the readiness gate without anyone hand-editing state JSON. Nothing is deleted: the statement and its evidence links survive, and a terminal claim is never re-derived from its evidence on a later read. `supersede` requires `--replaced-by` to name a claim the store already holds, because a forward pointer to nothing is worse than no pointer. **Two scopes:** a list reads the feature's `<slug>/` record; while that file does not exist it reads the flat record of the same task instead, but only when that record parses (a flat file that does not parse, or is not a regular file of at most 1 MiB, is never read) and names this feature, or names none and the feature has no `decisions/register.json`, and the first write goes to the namespace, starting from the flat one, after which only the namespaced record is read. A flat record is never read for a feature that has its own file, a task id equal to the slug has only the flat record, and a namespaced record is never read by a call that resolves no slug (the main checkout on `develop`, say), so such a call can list nothing or only part of what a feature recorded: pass `--slug <name>` there (see [Where a task's records live](#where-a-tasks-records-live)). |
| `doflow context-pack [<project>] --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--task-class <class>] [--objective <text>] [--json] [--harness <id>] [--max-bytes <n>] [--mandatory-file <path|->] [--since <digest>] [--expand <pointerId>] [--reason <text>] [--bare] [--no-record] [-g]` | Compile a task's recorded evidence, claims and mandatory requirements into the context pack a stage is handed, partitioned per the [classification table](https://github.com/khoavu882/do-flow/blob/main/specs/orchestrator-context-modes.md#classification-table). Mandatory content is always emitted whole. Exits 0 (`OK`) when a pack is emitted, whether or not its delivery is recorded (`--no-record`, or a failed record write, sets `delivery.recorded: false`), 1 (`EMPTY`) when nothing is recorded for the task (no evidence of any freshness, claims, research requests or live decisions), 2 (`USAGE`) on an IC-001 usage error, or 3 (`OVERFLOW`) when mandatory content plus omission records exceed the cap (the whole pack is still emitted; act on `overflowAction.message`). Precedence is 2, then 3, then 1, then 0. A recorded delivery is also named on the stage active at that moment in the task's run, or its feature's run (`--slug`, else the feature pointer), as that node's `packs`; with no active stage nothing is written |
| `doflow context-emit --event launch\|prompt\|delta --cwd <dir> [--session <id>] [--harness <id>] [--nudge nudge\|suppress\|none] [--max-bytes <n>] [--task-id <id>] [--slug <slug>] [--peek] [--json]` | The launcher's entry to one emission (078 IC-005), also reached as `doflow-run context-emit`, a thin wrapper over `emitContext()`; no skill invokes it. Identity comes only from the environment (`DOFLOW_RUN_ID`, `DOFLOW_LINEAGE_ID`, `DOFLOW_ATTEMPT`, `DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE`, `DOFLOW_WORKTREE`), and no flag overrides it. Without `--json` it prints the text alone, or nothing when silent; `--json` prints `{"v":1,"event","identity","decision","text","pack","committed"}`. `--peek` decides and renders but takes no claim and writes no ledger line. Exits 0 when it emits or is silent, 2 on usage (including `launch` without a launched identity, or an invalid identity value), and 3 when refused (`refused:mandatory-over-budget`) |
| `doflow context-grant --summary <summaryId\|pending> --to-run <id> --to-lineage <id> [--from-worktree <dir>] [--from-run <id>] [--from-lineage <id>] [--max-bytes <n>] [--json]` | Grant one compacted summary to a launched `(run, lineage)` (078 IC-013), also reached as `doflow-run context-grant`; no skill invokes it. The source scope defaults to the caller's identity (worktree `DOFLOW_WORKTREE` or the working directory, run `DOFLOW_RUN_ID` or `local`, lineage `DOFLOW_LINEAGE_ID` or `main`). `pending` names the scope's current summary. The body is copied to `grants/<to-run>/<to-lineage>/<summaryId>.md` atomically; the owner's summary is untouched. Exits 0 when created, 1 when the summary is not found, has no `summary_id`, or the grant already exists, 2 on usage, and 3 when the grant header plus the body exceeds `--max-bytes` (default 16384), refused before any write |
| `doflow retrieval-plan --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--action declare\|attempt\|report] [--need <intent>[,<intent>]…] [--provider <id>] [--status EMPTY\|UNREACHED\|UNVERIFIED] [--reason <code>] [--note <text>] [--stage <stage-id>] [--json]` | `--action declare` records needs and their resolved providers before retrieval, mints a `planId`, and assigns each need a `queryRef` (`<planId>#<intent>`). `--action attempt` appends a caller attempt for one need and provider; it requires `--need`, `--provider`, `--status` and `--reason`, with optional `--note`. `RETRIEVED` is derived from bound evidence and cannot be entered by hand. Reports include the declared `planId`, per-need `queryRef` and `attempts[]` (caller and derived), plus `unbound: { count, ids, causes }` for evidence not linked to a current need. Outcome precedence is `RETRIEVED` > fresh `EMPTY` > stale `EMPTY` > `UNVERIFIED` > `UNREACHED`; ties go to the lowest attempt number. A need with no attempt is `UNREACHED`; report exits 1 if any need is `UNREACHED` or `UNVERIFIED`. Legacy plans without a `planId` retain provider matching and do not gain attempts or query binding. |
| `doflow outcome --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--action record\|show] [--state COMPLETED\|BLOCKED\|ABANDONED\|INCONCLUSIVE] [--task-class <c>] [--stage <stage-id>] [--readiness <state>] [--verification <verdict>] [--json]` | `--action record` writes the task's terminal state from a closed four-state vocabulary — `COMPLETED`, `BLOCKED`, `ABANDONED`, `INCONCLUSIVE` — together with the basis it rests on; a state outside that set is refused with the valid one. `INCONCLUSIVE` carries verification's meaning: a verdict over zero evidence is not a pass, so `COMPLETED` is refused when the task's evidence ledger holds no records. `--task-class` is required on a write because the workflow's **terminal stage** is what records an outcome, and that stage is read from the workflow engine rather than decided here; a `--stage` that is not the terminal one is refused, naming the one that is. This verb never re-evaluates readiness and never re-runs verification: `--readiness` and `--verification` are the verdicts the run states it saw, validated against `readiness`'s and `verify`'s own vocabularies and reported back under `statedByCaller`; omit either and the basis records `NOT_RECORDED`, which is never read as a pass. `basis.evidenceCount` is a count of records and never a score, `basis.unreached[]` carries the declared needs the retrieval plan reports as `UNREACHED` plus verification's own gap, and `recordedAt` is stamped by the runtime and never accepted from the caller. The default `--action show` emits the recorded outcome and exits 1 only when none exists — a recorded `BLOCKED` exits 0, since "no outcome was recorded" and "the outcome was not a completion" are different answers |
| `doflow verify --task-id <id> [--slug <name>] [--action contract\|report] [--risk <level>] [--plan-path <path>] [--scope <path>[,<path>…]] [--verification-input <file>] [--verification-phase postimplementation] [--baseline-ref <ref>] [--json]` | `--action contract` compiles the verification contract before implementation; the default runs it and reports. Exits 1 on `FAIL` **and** on `INCONCLUSIVE` — a verdict over zero evidence is not a pass. `--plan-path` points at a feature's `plan.md` whose `doflow-verification` block overrides manifest detection, which is what lets a specs-and-scripts repository with no `package.json`, `go.mod` or `pom.xml` declare its own build and test commands instead of leaving every tier unresolved. For a feature with a decision register, the change-scope tier is bounded by the `files:` lists of the plan's tasks plus the feature folder, and measured from the merge base with the integration ref (the working tree alone, and the tier says so, when there is none); every file outside the bound is listed and the tier fails; paths under `.doflow/` never count as changes. The plan comes from `--plan-path`, else the feature folder in this checkout, else, when run in a linked worktree, the same folder in the main checkout; the first that exists is used, and a `--plan-path` that does not exist stops the search. `--scope <path>[,<path>…]` (repository-relative paths, a trailing `/` for a directory) bounds a change that has no plan and, given with a plan, is added to its bound without narrowing it; a scope stated on `readiness --scope` is used the same way, and a `--scope` that differs from it is refused. A `bound:` line under the tier names where the bound came from. The tier stays unresolved without a register, without a plan, or when the plan names no task files, unless a scope is declared, and then it names where it looked and the command that bounds the change. A report that would be `PASS` is `INCONCLUSIVE` while the task's run has its gated stage pending and no READY readiness record of that stage's template exists. `--verification-input <file>` supplies caller-defined `structuralChecks` and `requirements`. `--verification-phase postimplementation` truthfully records `compiledBeforeImplementation: false` (default unknown records `null`). `--baseline-ref <ref>` pins an ancestor commit from which committed, staged, unstaged and untracked changes are measured, leaving change-scope unresolved if non-ancestor. With the `verify.reuse` setting on (off by default), L1 and L2 checks can be answered from a recorded PASS: see [Reuse of a recorded PASS](#reuse-of-a-recorded-pass) |
| `doflow recover --error <message> [--failed-check <name>]… [--iteration N] [--agent <name>] [--json]` | Classify a verification failure into one of eleven classes and return the targeted action for it. Exits 0 when a bounded retry is available, 1 when the loop must stop |
| `doflow scaffold [--slug <name>] [--json]` | Emit the reviewable code scaffold the active feature's `requirement.md`, `design.md` and `plan.md` imply, under that feature's own `scaffold/` directory. Signatures, types and stubs only — never a write into the source tree. Exits 1 when the scaffold is incomplete or blocked, so a partial result is never reported as success. This is what `/do-execute-plan --scaffold` runs |
| `doflow review-pack --task-id <slug> [--json]` | Write `review-pack.md` in the feature's folder from its records and print its path; `/do-flow` runs it as its last step so a person reviews once, at the end, what an unattended run decided. No model call: every line is something a record states, and a section with nothing recorded says `none recorded`. The sections, in order: (1) Result: task class, branch, head commit, merge commit when the roadmap seed has one, stage reached; (2) Decisions taken for you: each register decision decided by the agent or autopilot, with stage, topic, decision, reason and `undo` (decisions by the user are a count only); (3) Gates: each gate in the run journal, who decided (the policy, or `not by policy (actor not recorded)`, since the journal names no person), when, and for a policy approval the policy digest, the number of inputs and the inputs digest from the register; (4) Verification: each verify run on record with its verdict, risk, commit and tree state, and whether the latest ran on the head commit (the record keeps no command or contract, which section 7 says); (5) Review: seats and rounds from `review/findings.json`, findings by severity and the ones the person accepted without a fix; (6) Open items: follow-ups filed for the feature, holds on its roadmap row and unresolved `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` markers; (7) Not verified: what the records cannot show. The same records give the same bytes except the `Generated at` line. `--json` prints `{v,slug,path,counts}`. A record that exists but cannot be read (or a review record that is not valid) is named in section 7 with its file, apart from one that is absent. Exits 2 when `--task-id` is missing or names no feature folder |
| `doflow decision --action init\|add\|list\|compact [--by <user\|agent\|autopilot[,…]>] [--slug <name>] [--json]` | Keep one decision register per feature under `decisions/`, resolved the way `scaffold` resolves the feature (working directory, or `--slug`). `init` creates `decisions/register.json` and both generated views for a feature that has none; a folder that already holds `requirement.md`, `design.md` or `plan.md` is refused (exit 1, `predates-register`) so older features stay untouched, and a rerun on an existing register writes nothing. `add` takes `--topic <key> --statement <one line, at most 280 chars> --channel question\|gate\|prompt\|default\|resolution --stage discovery\|design\|planning\|implementation\|verification\|review --rationale <text> [--supersedes DEC-###[,…]] [--refs <id>[,…]] [--source <locator>] [--undo <one line>]`, or `--batch <file.json>` carrying an array of the same fields; `--undo` says how a person reverses the decision, is stored in the register and shown in `decisions/archive.md` beside the rationale, and is required (exit 2 otherwise) when `--channel default` carries a rationale starting `unattended:`; any invalid item refuses the whole batch (exit 2) and nothing is written. Who decided follows from the channel (`question`, `gate`, `prompt` are the user; `default`, `resolution` are the agent) and cannot be passed. A topic holds one live decision: a new one on a live topic must name it in `--supersedes`, otherwise exit 1 (`topic-conflict`) and nothing is written. `list` returns live decisions by topic, `--all` every decision by id, `--by user|agent|autopilot[,…]` only those whose decider is named (any other value is exit 2 and names the valid set); `status` is a synonym. `compact` moves the lines of each chain artifact's `History` section into `decisions/history/<artifact>.md`, leaves its comments and one `Earlier entries:` pointer, and reports `unchanged` on a second run; every artifact is attempted, and a failure exits 1 (`compaction-failed`) with status `partial`, a `failed[]` list carrying each artifact's message, and the failed artifacts untouched; `moved` still reports the artifacts that succeeded, each by its feature-relative path such as `intention/requirement.md`. `decisions.md` and `decisions/archive.md` are generated from the register and never hand-edited. Exits 1 on `no-register`, `predates-register`, `topic-conflict`, `compaction-failed` and `register-locked` (the register lock timed out; retry), 2 on a usage error or an unresolvable feature |
| `doflow followup --action add\|list\|take\|settle\|promote\|report [--slug <name>] [--json]` | Record and manage what a feature left unfinished, in the project's own store: one write-once JSON event per change under `.doflow/state/lifecycle/events/` at the repository root (the first non-bare worktree, so every linked worktree shares one store), local to this machine and not shared. DoFlow never stages, commits or pushes that folder; `.doflow/state/.gitignore` keeps it out of `git add -A` (see [Storage and retention](#storage-and-retention)). The first `followup`, `lifecycle`, `goal` or `failure --action settle --as imported` command copies an existing `agent-docs/lifecycle/events` once and leaves it untouched; a marker file, `.doflow/state/lifecycle/migrated.json`, records that the store is in place, and from then on the old folder is never read again, even when the store later empties. A store that already holds events gets the marker without a copy. While the old folder exists, every one of those commands prints one stderr line saying it can be deleted. Settled follow-ups and goals (never open, taken or unfinished ones) are pruned once their newest event is older than the retention window. `DOFLOW_RETENTION_HOURS` set to a positive whole number of hours decides the window, and a command that removes event files prints one stderr count line (`retention: removed N event files older than H h`, `1 event file` for one); an invalid value warns and removes nothing. Unset or empty, the window is 180 days (4320 hours) while the project's `prune` setting is `on`, and nothing is removed while it is `off`. `add` takes `--stage discovery\|design\|planning\|implementation\|verification\|review\|release\|maintain --statement <one line, at most 280 chars>` and records the feature from `--slug` or the branch; with no feature it needs `--source run\|release\|manual` (`run` also `--task-class <c> --task-id <id>`, `release` also requires `--release <tag>`); `--batch <file.json>` carries an array of `{statement, stage, source}` objects and any invalid item refuses the whole batch (exit 2). `list [--state open\|taken\|done\|dismissed\|all] [--view brief\|full] [--cursor <c>] [--limit <n>] [--ids <FU-a,FU-b>]` defaults to open, and a taken item shows `done` while its feature derives finished. It answers brief by default: each item keeps `id`, `state` and `statement`, 10 items to a page (`--limit` takes up to 100), with `view`, `shown`, `nextCursor` and `next` beside them; `--cursor` takes the `nextCursor` of the page before it, and text mode pages the same way, printing `next:` lines whenever the page is not the whole list (the last page of a cursor walk included, where only the full-view line remains). In a JSON answer the `Full view of this page` line ends with `--json`, so it prints the full payload as written. `--ids` filters to the named items: an unknown id is refused (exit 1), and without `--state` every state is searched. `--view full` with no cursor and no limit returns the whole previous object, the flag to add when a script reads fields the brief drops; a paged full walk keeps `--view full` on its next-page line. `--view`, `--cursor` and `--limit` on any other action are a usage error (exit 2). `take --ids <FU-a,FU-b> --slug <name>` needs open items and a tracked feature. `settle --ids <FU-a> --as kept\|dismissed\|fix\|done` needs `--reason` for `dismissed` and `fix`, and for `kept` when it releases a taken item or reopens a dismissed one, and `--evidence` for `done`. `promote --ids <FU-a,FU-b> --title <intent title>` creates a new `agent-docs/intent/<title>.md` and never touches an existing one. `report --statement <one line>` files a product problem as an open follow-up (source `report`, optionally `--release <tag>` and `--feature <slug>`); its body comes from exactly one of `--file <path>` (`-` reads stdin), `--stdin` or `--text <body>`, is masked, capped at 1 MiB and kept only on this machine under `<config home>/reports/`, while the project store holds the id, a masked 2 KB excerpt and the size. Free text is masked for secrets before it is stored, on a best-effort basis, never as a guarantee. `--channel question\|gate\|prompt\|default` records whether the user or the agent acted. Exits 1 on `unknown-id`, `illegal-transition`, `untracked-feature`, `intent-exists`, `reason-required`, `body-not-written`, `id-collision`, `store-locked`, `store-migration-failed` and `store-migration-pending`, 2 on a usage error or `-g` |
| `doflow lifecycle [--action overview\|init\|status\|release\|merged] [--slug <name>] [--view brief\|full] [--json]` | The project-level view of the same store. `overview` (the default with no `--action`) lists the open follow-ups with their sources, promoted intents, goals, and feature statuses in four buckets (`finished`, `awaitingRelease`, `inProgress`, `unknown`), plus ready-to-run `next` lines. `--view full` is the default; `--view brief` drops `features` and `featuresShown`, reduces each follow-up to `id`, `statement` and `source` (plus `pending` with `--maintain`), reduces each goal to `goal`, `outcome`, `items`, `proposeDone`, `nudges` and `conflicts`, and in JSON lists 10 follow-ups (`followups.shown` says how many; `--maintain` keeps its 50), at most 10 intents with at most 10 ids each, and at most 10 nudges per goal (a nudge names at most the 10 most recent finished features, then `(+N more)`, in both views); what the brief cuts is counted in a brief-only `omitted` object (`intents`, `intentItems`, `nudges`, `finished`, each with `total` and `more`; absent when nothing was cut) and a `Full view:` line in `next` names the complete answer; the text answer is the same in both views. In both views the `next` line for the rest reads `Page through all <open> open follow-ups: doflow-run followup --action list`. `--view` on any other action is a usage error (exit 2). It writes no event; like every lifecycle command it first runs the one-time copy and the retention removal of expired settled files, and when neither has anything to do it creates no folder. `--maintain [--since <ISO time>]` shows up to 50 items, a `pending` count and, inside the DoFlow repository, the new and regressed failure entries. `init --slug <slug> [--take <FU-a,FU-b>] [--intent <path>] [--goal <goal>]` tracks a feature whose folder exists under the repository root, optionally taking follow-ups or every open one promoted to that intent, in one all-or-nothing write; a folder that exists only in a linked worktree is refused (`no-feature-folder`). `status --slug <slug>` derives `finished`, `awaiting-release`, `in-progress` or `unknown` from git (merge evidence into the integration ref, the first that exists of `develop`, `origin/develop`, `main`, `origin/main`, `master`, `origin/master` and `origin/HEAD`) and never writes it; `status` with no `--slug` reads `.doflow/state/feature/<id>.json`, the feature the runtime recorded for this checkout (written by `init` and by a `feature` run's `orchestrate --action start` or the `catch-up` that begins it, removed by `merged`, a confirmed `release` and a run reaching COMPLETED), and compares it with the branch: if they disagree it reports both with `slug: null`, finding `feature-mismatch`, and exits 1. A feature the pointer names but `lifecycle` does not track answers `<slug> is tracked by its run only; lifecycle --action init to track it`. `release --tag <vX.Y.Z> [--confirm] [--feature <slug>]… [--exclude <slug>]…` previews the tracked features a release ships and writes nothing; with `--confirm` the tag must exist (`tag-missing`) and one release record names what shipped, which finishes those features and the follow-ups they took; `--feature` adds a feature git could not detect, `--exclude` removes one, and `notDetected` lists tracked features with no evidence (a squash, rebase, fast-forward or cherry-pick merge). `merged --slug <slug> --reason <line>` records a merge git cannot show (`already-merged` when git already shows it). Reads git only; no network; nothing is committed or pushed. Exits 1 on a refusal (`no-feature-folder`, `untracked-feature`, `unknown-id`, `unknown-goal`, `goal-already-linked`, `illegal-transition`, `no-integration-ref`, `tag-missing`, `already-merged`, `store-locked`, `store-migration-failed`, `store-migration-pending`), 2 on a usage error or `-g` |
| `doflow roadmap --action render\|status\|next\|audit\|report\|init-policy [--check] [--row <id>] [--run <id>] [--commit] [--json]` | Session-driven autopilot runner for the feature roadmap. Every call does at most one step and exits; nothing stays resident while a model works. `--action` is required (missing or unknown: exit 2). `--check` belongs to `render`, `--row` to `status` and `next`, `--run` to `next` and `audit` (shape `rl-<8 digits>-<4 lowercase Crockford base-32 characters>`, which excludes `i`, `l`, `o` and `u`, else exit 2). The seed is `agent-docs/roadmap/rows.json` in the main checkout; it declares identity, `needs`, class, `writeSet` and `requires` per row, and a `status`, `phase` or `evidence` key is rejected (`seed-typed-status`). A row may carry `hold`, a non-empty single-line string of at most 120 characters: the picker skips the row with reason `row-held`, the loop does not advance a held row already in flight, `render` prints `held: <text>` in the Next column and `status` reports it as `holdReason`; the derived status does not change (a held row that has a merge is still merged). A row may carry `proposed: true` (any other value is `seed-invalid`): `seed apply` and `amend` write it while autopilot is not verifiably off, its derived status is `proposed` until it has a branch or a merge, the picker skips it with reason `row-proposed`, the loop does not advance it at `intent` or `built`, and a policy gate denies it `row-proposed`; `doflow roadmap promote` removes it. `render` writes `agent-docs/roadmap/ROADMAP.md` by temp file and rename, only when its bytes change; `render --check` is read-only and exits 1 with findings (`render-drift`, `duplicate-merge`, `trailer-unknown-row`, `commit-claimed-twice`, `trailered-and-seeded`, `pointer-not-merge`, `needs-unresolved`, `duplicate-id`, `slug-id-mismatch`, `seed-invalid-json`, `seed-invalid`, `seed-unknown-key`, `class-unknown`, `needs-cycle`, or the seed parser's own code such as `seed-typed-status`), else 0. `status` derives each row as `merged` (a `DoFlow-Row: <id>` trailer on a first-parent two-parent merge of develop, or a seeded `mergeCommit` that is such a merge), `in-flight` (a local `feat/<slug>` branch exists; no claim record is read) or `not-started`, and shows `merged-unverified` while an open merge intent has that status; it also lists open merge intents. Beside the git-derived status it prints what git cannot show: the holds and pauses the newest open run record keeps per row (`Run hold <row>: held|paused (<kind>): <reason>`; `--json`: `run`, `holds`) and the lease holder (`Lease: pid <pid> on <host> for <runId>` or `Lease: none`; `--json`: `lease`). A rejected seed is exit 2; an unreadable fact source (git facts, merge intents, a run record or the lease file; `--json`: `error: cannot-read-facts` with `sources`) or an unknown `--row` is exit 1, never an empty answer. Scheduling and `status` cover the seeded rows only: a `feat/*` branch, worktree or run for no seeded row counts toward neither the work-in-progress cap nor the write-set overlap check, and `status` does not report it. `next` prints a summary line, then the `perform` envelope as one JSON line when present and one line per skipped row (`--json`: one object with `schema`, `runId`, `n`, `outcome`, `step`, `row`, `reason`, `reasons`, `rule`, `evidence`, `checkpoint`, and where present `annotations`, `perform`, `skips`). The outcome is one of `acted` (`step` is `pick`, `gate`, `merge`, `render` or `cleanup`), `perform` (an envelope the session carries out, then calls `next` again), `idle` (`skips` names every row's reason; the reasons are listed below), `denied`, `blocked` or `stopped`. Exit 0 for `acted`, `perform` and `idle`; 1 for `denied`, `blocked` and `stopped`, including `lease-held`; 2 for usage and runtime errors. A `denied` reason is a member of the closed `denyReasons` list in `core/registry/gates.json` (a `:<x>` suffix is a parameter, not a new reason). Besides the gate predicates' reasons, `denied` carries: `lease-held` (the lease file names a holder not proven dead, or another reclaimer not proven dead holds `lease.reclaim`), `lease-lost` (at a step boundary or before a write the lease file no longer holds this call's own bytes; the call writes nothing more), `facts-unreadable`, `stage-not-performed`, `plan-exit-refused` (the planning envelope's plan was written but its handoff was refused by the constitution exit check; `annotations.refusal` holds the refusal and the row stays at planning), `report-changed`, `pause-not-machine`, `seed-typed-status`, `no-develop-worktree`, and from the merge step `head-moved` (the feature branch is not the head gate-b approved), `develop-moved`, `develop-dirty`, `develop-wrong-branch`, `merge-conflict`, `git-too-old`, `ff-running` (a fast-forward child of an earlier call is still alive), `policy-never` (develop's current policy has gate-b `never` for the row's class, checked again before the first intent is written; otherwise an approval is not retroactive) and `plan-changed` (the feature plan differs from the one gate-b approved). Gate and policy reasons new with the review: `user-accepted-needs-person:<id>` (a policy evaluation denies every `user-accepted` blocker whatever its decision channel, because any session can write the register entry that would back it, so only a person approving gate-b gets past it; a `user-accepted` note or should-fix is accepted when its decision is on the `question`, `gate`, `prompt` or `default` channel, where `default` is the agent's own record, so an unattended session can record what it decided), `protected-path:plan-verification` (the predicate `plan-verification-absent` of gate-a and gate-b: a `doflow-verification` block in the feature `plan.md` replaces the post-merge verify commands, so a plan that carries one is denied), `policy-override-invalid` (`.doflow/gates.override.json` is unreadable or not a JSON object) and `policy-override-widens:<key>`. A `develop-moved` or `plan-changed` deny on an open merge intent is held per row (see `held:<reason>` below), so other rows still advance. Before the `review` stage's envelope is issued, the loop applies the verify rule of gate-b (`verify-missing`, `verify-not-pass`, `verify-flaky`) to the row's feature head; a failing row is `denied` with `step: review` and rule `review/<reason>`, held per row on unchanged inputs, with no question and no review seats spent. `skips` (in `idle` and, per row, in the text output) holds one reason per row. The picker gives `row-held`, `row-proposed`, `class-unsupported`, `need-unmerged`, `exclusive-row-in-flight`, `needs-exclusive`, `write-set-overlap:<id>` and `wip-full`. The step loop gives `row-unknown` (an open intent names no seeded row), `row-proposed` (a seed `proposed` row, which a person promotes), `row-held` (a seed `hold`: it also stops an intent at `intent` or `built` before the fast-forward; from `ff-started` on the row settles like an off switch), `class-unsupported`, `no-worktree` (an in-flight row whose `feat/<slug>` branch has no worktree), `run-rejected` (the orchestration run is `REJECTED`), `gate-b-not-by-policy` (the run's gate-b was approved by a person, so there is no intent for the loop to create), `held:<reason>` (the row's last deny or block repeated on unchanged inputs; it is logged once and the row is skipped, spending no budget, until its inputs change), `cleanup-kept`, `intent-abandoned` (a person archived the row's intent as `abandoned`; the loop neither resumes nor replaces it), and for any other row `merged`, `in-flight` or `not-in-flight`. `blocked` carries `budget` (the run reached `loop.maxSteps`), `stuck` (a dispatch run is blocked, cancelled or repeated a failure class) or `quota` (the dispatch pause names a quota); `stopped` has no reason and names the switch layer in `annotations.switch` (`env`, `sentinel` or `setting`). The kill switch is the setting `autopilot.enabled` (see [Settings keys](#settings-keys)), read at the start of `next` and again immediately before every mutating act (claiming a row, starting an orchestration run, deciding a gate, landing a dispatch, each step of the merge, the render and, again, the cleanup); a switch turned off mid-call stops the next act, and a sentinel file that cannot be read counts as set. With the switch off an intent at `ff-started` is only settled (a develop already at the commit records `ff-done`, a killed partial that is proven dead is repaired) and no new fast-forward starts. `status`, `render` and `audit` do not depend on it. The merge step is `git merge-tree --write-tree`, a `commit-tree` with the identity of the develop worktree and the trailers `DoFlow-Row` and `DoFlow-Policy`, then `merge --ff-only`, recorded as an intent whose phases are `intent`, `built`, `ff-started`, `ff-done`, `rendered`, `cleaned`; a killed call resumes from the intent and yields the same commit. The fast-forward call reports `acted` step `merge` with `annotations.pending` `post-merge-verification`; the next `next` call yields a `perform` envelope (stage `verification`, `purpose` `post-merge`) that runs `verify --action report --baseline-ref <develop head before the merge> --plan-path <the feature plan>` in the develop worktree. The envelope is answered by a verify run record, not by a named set of guards or suites: the loop reads `.doflow/state/verification/runs/<slug>.jsonl` in the main checkout, where the verb writes it, and applies gate-b's own rule (`verifyRecordReasons`). Only lines for the row's task id (the slug) on the new develop head from a clean tree count; none yields the envelope, and a set whose last line is not a PASS, with a line that is not schema 1, or with a FAIL that a later PASS would hide (`verify-flaky`) is `merged-unverified`. The loop records the feature plan's sha256 at gate-b; if the plan differs when the post-merge step runs, the call denies `plan-changed` before the envelope. The feature's own verification envelope runs `verify --action report --baseline-ref <merge base of the feature branch and local develop>`, so an unpushed local merge never widens the next row's change scope. The implementation envelope runs `dispatch start --billing-cap free --verify-argv <json>` (the row's seed `verifyArgv`, an array of 1 to 8 non-empty strings, else `["npm","test"]` when the feature worktree's `package.json` has a test script, else `blocked: stuck` with `annotations.dispatch` `no-verify-argv`); when the dispatch completes, the loop fast-forwards `feat/<slug>` to the dispatch's integration branch (`merge --ff-only` in the feature worktree), and a branch that is not a fast-forward is `blocked: stuck` with `annotations.dispatch` `not-fast-forward` and nothing changed. The S8 tail then runs inside the merge call: the render of `ROADMAP.md` and the non-force cleanup (`worktree remove`, `branch -d`) follow in the same `next`, which reports step `cleanup` with `annotations.rendered`, so the next call is already the pick. The cleanup first proves the branch is safe to drop: its tip must be the head the merge recorded and an ancestor of `refs/heads/develop`, and its worktree must be on that branch and clean. Otherwise (a dirty worktree, or a branch someone kept committing on) both are kept (`annotations.cleanup` is `cleanup-kept`, the row stays merged) and the row is skipped as `cleanup-kept` until a person resolves it. A row whose merge carries a `DoFlow-Row` trailer and whose branch or worktree is left over gets the same render and non-force cleanup; a row merged only by a seeded `mergeCommit` is never cleaned by the loop. A failed post-merge check keeps the merge, worktree and branch, reports `merged-unverified` and trips the sentinel. The loop spawns only `git` (G53, G54) and has no push, fetch, tag, publish or install step. `audit` lists every logged step of every run, or of one `--run`, oldest run first. A run record keeps its last 500 log entries and is pruned after 30 days of finished work; the gate, merge, render and cleanup entries are also appended, with their input locators and sha256 hashes, to the `roadmap-audit` store (`.doflow/state/roadmap/audit.jsonl`, the last 10000 entries kept) and `audit` merges both, showing an entry present in both once. `--json` gives `{schema, action, entries}`; an unreadable run record or audit file is exit 1. `init-policy` writes the installed DoFlow's `core/registry/gates.json` to `<main checkout>/core/registry/gates.json` and prints "commit this on develop"; it refuses to overwrite an existing file (exit 1, the file untouched), stages and commits nothing, and runs no command (`--json`: `{schema, action, written, root}`). `init-policy --commit` (valid only with `init-policy`, else exit 2) instead commits the file onto the local `develop` with git plumbing in a temporary index, so the index, the working tree and the checked-out branch stay as they are: the bytes are the main checkout's `core/registry/gates.json` when it exists (a reviewed or narrowed copy wins), else the packaged policy, and invalid bytes are refused (exit 1, the reason). One commit touches only that file (message `chore(roadmap): add the autopilot gate policy`, author from the repository's git config, no signing), and the ref moves with compare-and-swap. It exits 1 and changes nothing when develop already has the file, when there is no local develop, when develop moved meanwhile, or when develop is checked out in any worktree (the ref is not moved; the `mkdir`, `cp`, `git add` and `git commit` commands to run in that worktree are printed). On success it prints `develop <old> -> <new>`; an identical working copy is then removed (it is tracked on develop now), a differing one or one tracked in the checked-out branch is kept and reported (`--json`: `{schema, action, committed, commit, previous, file, root, workingCopy}` with `workingCopy` `none`, `removed` or `kept`). It never stages, pushes, tags or publishes. The loop never reads the working copy: the policy is read only from develop's committed head. A repository whose develop has no committed `core/registry/gates.json` is denied `no-policy` (the reason id is unchanged) with `annotations.hint` (`next`, `--json` and as a text line) and, for `orchestrate --action decide-gate --policy`, the same `annotations.hint`: write the file with `init-policy`, review it and commit it on develop; `agent-docs/` may stay gitignored `report` writes one table for a roadmap run (`--run`, default the newest run record) to `agent-docs/roadmap/roadmap-report.md` beside `ROADMAP.md` (one file, overwritten by each report, with the run id in its heading) and prints it: per row of the run, its title, class and status, whether it is held and why, how many decisions the agent or autopilot took, how many open items it left, and the path of its `review-pack.md`, which the report regenerates for every row it lists. It reads records only and makes no model call. Exit 1 when no run record exists (or the named run has none); exit 2 on a usage error such as a malformed `--run` or a positional argument |
| `doflow roadmap seed [<project>] --action check\|apply\|amend --draft <path> [--json]` | Validate a draft of roadmap rows against the seed `agent-docs/roadmap/rows.json` and, for `apply` and `amend`, write it. `seed` is the first word after `roadmap`; `<project>` is an optional project root (default the current directory) used to find the store and `ROADMAP.md`, which are the main checkout's, found as `render` finds them; a relative `--draft` always resolves against the current directory, never against `<project>`. `roadmap --action render seed` routes to this verb, and `--row`, `--run` and `--check` are ignored under `seed`. `--action` (one of `check`, `apply`, `amend`) and `--draft` are required. `check` writes nothing; `apply` appends rows whose id is not in the store; `amend` replaces, whole and in place, rows whose id is in the store (no partial patch). `apply` and `amend` write the store and then `ROADMAP.md`, each by temp file and rename, only when the judgement taken under the lease has no finding. A failed rename removes its temp file. A stop between the two renames leaves the new store and the old table: `render --check` reports `render-drift` and `roadmap --action render` repairs it. `check` also prints `land`, the command the PERSON runs to land a clean draft (`--json` key `land`, last key; text line 2 `land (person only): ...`; a string only for `check` with `ok` true and every row sharing one `change`, `apply` for adds and `amend` for amends; otherwise null; `land` is also null, with no `land` line, when the root or draft path holds a control character: U+0000-U+001F, U+007F, U+2028 or U+2029); a missing `agent-docs/roadmap/rows.json` (ENOENT only) is read as an empty store on check/apply/amend (digest null, text line 1 ends `, store absent`), `apply` creates the directory, store and table, `amend` reports `row-not-found`; any other read error still exits 2. Exit 0: `check` clean, or a write done. Exit 1: any finding, a refusal (below) or unreadable git facts (`--json`: `error: cannot-read-facts` with `sources`). Exit 2: usage, meaning `--action` missing or not one of the three, `--draft` missing, or the draft file, or the store file (other than a missing one, which is read as an empty store), unreadable as a file. A draft file that is not valid JSON is the finding `seed-invalid-json` (exit 1), not usage. The draft is `{"schema":1,"rows":[...]}`: the top-level keys are exactly `schema` (the number 1) and `rows` (a non-empty array); any other top-level key is `seed-unknown-key`, and a top-level `status`, `phase` or `evidence` is `seed-typed-status`. A draft row may carry `id`, `slug`, `title`, `class`, `needs`, `writeSet`, `requires`, `hold`, `verifyArgv`, `intent` and `acceptance`; a `proposed` key is `seed-invalid` (only `apply` and `amend` write it); any other key is `seed-unknown-key`, and `status`, `phase`, `evidence` or `mergeCommit` is `seed-typed-status` (a merge pointer is status evidence, so a draft cannot carry one). Each `writeSet` entry is a repo-relative path: no leading `/` or drive letter, no backslash, and no empty, `.` or `..` segment; one trailing `/` marks a directory; anything else is `seed-invalid`. The store must carry `schema` 1, else `seed-invalid`. `--json` prints one line with these keys in this order: `schema`, `action` (`seed`), `op`, `ok`, `draft` (`path`, `sha256`), `seed` (`path`, `sha256`), `rows` (per draft row in draft order: `id`, `change` as `add` or `amend`, and for `amend` the derived `status`), `findings` (`rule`, `message`, and `rowId` or `rowIds` when the rule concerns rows), `overlaps` (`rows` and `paths`: pairs of rows not merged, at least one in the draft, whose write sets overlap), `alone` (draft rows not merged that declare no `writeSet`, which run alone), `reason`, `switch`, `writes`, `land`. `reason` is `null` or a refusal; `switch` is `null` in every case the verb produces; `writes` is the number of files written (0, 1 or 2); `ok` is true only with no finding and no refusal. A refusal prints `rows: []` (the draft is not judged). A row whose `id` is not a string carries findings without `rowId`. The text form is capped at 12 lines: a first line `roadmap seed <op>: <ok\|N findings\|refused: <reason>>, <a> add, <m> amend, <o> overlaps, <l> alone` (plus `, <w> files written` on a clean apply or amend, including `0 files written` when nothing changed), then at most 10 finding and overlap lines, then `  ... <k> more (--json)` when lines were cut; findings and refusals print on stderr when the exit is non-zero. The finding codes are the closed list `SEED_RULES`, in this order: `seed-invalid-json`, `seed-invalid`, `seed-unknown-key`, `seed-typed-status`, `slug-id-mismatch`, `duplicate-id`, `class-unknown`, `needs-unresolved`, `needs-cycle`, `id-exists`, `row-not-found`, `row-started`, `row-not-proposed`. The first nine are the store rules that `render --check` reports with the same codes; the last four belong to the operation: `id-exists` (`apply` on a row already in the store), `row-not-found` (`amend` on a row not in the store), `row-started` (`check` or `amend` on a row derived `merged` or `in-flight`; the message names the status), `row-not-proposed` (`roadmap promote` on a row that is not proposed). Findings are ordered document-level first, then by row position in the candidate store, then by `SEED_RULES` position; a cycle is reported once, on its first row, with `rowIds` in path order. Activation, not the write, is gated (maint-thin, 2026-10-26). `apply` and `amend` run whether or not autopilot is on and are never refused for it. Rows land ready only when stdin is an interactive terminal and either autopilot is verifiably off for the config home, or a person answers `y` or `yes` to a `[y/N]` prompt on the controlling terminal (`/dev/tty`, the same prompt `roadmap promote` uses; it lists the draft's row ids and titles and is asked only for a draft that judges clean, before the roadmap lease is taken). No flag or environment variable skips the prompt. Otherwise (no terminal, no `/dev/tty`, an answer other than `y` or `yes`) every row they add or replace lands with `proposed: true` (`--json` key `proposed`, the ids; text line 1 adds `, <n> proposed`), and autopilot never picks, steps or gate-approves such a row. Autopilot is verifiably off only with the sentinel `<config home>/autopilot/off` present or `autopilot.enabled` resolved to `off`; `on`, settings that cannot be read or parsed, or no config home all count as not off.
