The verb does not read the variable `DOFLOW_AUTOPILOT_ENABLED`. The terminal check is there because a model with a shell controls the environment: pointing `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` at an empty config home reads as off, so the switch alone cannot tell a person. The prompt is there so that a seed does not land ready rows while autopilot is on just because stdin looks like a terminal: it stops a scripted or non-terminal stdin and any environment variable or flag, and it needs an answer read from `/dev/tty`. It does not stop a process that can allocate a pseudo-terminal and write `y` to it; such a process can still answer it. The prompt waits 60 seconds; with no answer by then the rows land `proposed` and one line on stderr says the prompt timed out (`roadmap promote` has no time limit). A model can still draft and write rows; they wait for a person's `roadmap promote`. An `amend` of a row that is live and `proposed` keeps it proposed whatever the checks say; only `roadmap promote` clears the mark. The terminal check and the prompt are a speed bump, not proof that a person ran the command: a process can allocate a pseudo-terminal, answer the prompt and pick an empty config home, so the harness permission layer, not these checks, is the real boundary. With the terminal check and one of the two other checks met the rows land ready, with no `proposed` key. The one refusal, exit 1 and only for `apply` and `amend`, is `lease-held` (a loop step holds the roadmap lease; the text names the holder). A static guard (G54) keeps every autopilot code path away from `seed.ts`. No wrapper script (such as a `doflow-seed.sh` that turns autopilot off around the call) is needed. Git is read through read verbs only; no model, network or process other than `git` is used |
| `doflow roadmap promote <id...> [--json]` | Make proposed roadmap rows ready: removes `proposed` from each named row of `agent-docs/roadmap/rows.json` in the main checkout of the current directory, then rewrites `ROADMAP.md`, each by temp file and rename. Person-only, by two checks, and it does not read or change the autopilot switch: without an interactive terminal on stdin it is refused `needs-person-tty` (exit 1, nothing written), because a model with a shell controls the environment; then it validates the named rows, prints their ids and titles and asks `Promote <n> row(s): <ids>? [y/N]` on the controlling terminal (`/dev/tty`, not stdin, an argument or the environment). No `/dev/tty`, or an answer other than `y` or `yes`, is refused `needs-person-confirm` (exit 1, nothing written). There is no `--yes` flag or environment variable that skips the prompt. The prompt comes before the roadmap lease, so Ctrl-C leaves no lease behind; under the lease the store is read again, and if it changed since the rows were shown the verb is refused `needs-person-confirm` with nothing written. While a loop step holds the lease it is refused `lease-held`. The person-only check is a terminal check: it stops a caller without a controlling terminal, but a process with one (a pseudo-terminal) can still answer (a signed token is planned for 2.1). All or nothing: an id not in the store (`row-not-found`), a row that is not proposed (`row-not-proposed`) or an id named twice (`duplicate-id`) is a finding, exit 1, reported before any prompt, and nothing is written. No ids, or an id that is not a row id, is usage (exit 2). Text: `roadmap promote: ok, <n> promoted: <ids>`; `--json`: one line with `schema`, `action` (`promote`), `ok`, `ids`, `promoted`, `findings`, `reason`, `writes`. |
| `doflow plan-check <plan path> [--json]` | Check a plan's task lines the way the task-attempt ledger reads them. It prints the one plan verdict that `task-attempt` and the roadmap facts also take, so the three cannot disagree about a plan's task graph, plus content `findings` (row 134). Read-only: it reads the file, resolved against the working directory, and the `requirement.md` beside it (`intention/requirement.md` first, as the path resolver finds it), and writes nothing. The text form is one line, `plan-check: <path>: ok, <n> tasks` or `plan-check: <path>: <k> problem(s): <kind> <ids>; ...`, cut to 200 characters; `--json` prints `{ok, tasks, problems:[{kind, ids, value?, via?}], warnings, findings:[{rule, severity, path, line, message, id?}], file}`. `findings` never change `ok` or the exit code: `placeholder` (severity `fix`) is a plan line outside code spans, fences (closed only by the same character, at least as long) and HTML comments holding `TBD`, `TODO`, `FIXME`, "to be decided", "fill in later" or a table cell that is only `later`; `coverage` (`fix`) is a user story (`**US<n>`) or a `FR-`/`NFR-` index row of the requirement, not `Superseded` and above its History section, that the plan's visible text never names, located at its requirement line; `constitution-must-fail` and `constitution-must-missing` (`refuses-exit`), `constitution-should-fail` and `constitution-downgrade` (`advisory`), `constitution-unparseable` and `constitution-unreadable` (`refuses-exit`) are the planning stage exit's own check (see `orchestrate`), the constitution read from the checkout holding the plan (the nearest `.git` above it, else the working directory). The text form appends `; findings: <rule> <path>:<line>, ...` before the 200-character cut. `warnings` is a list of strings and does not change the exit code. The text form also lists them, appending `; warnings: <w>; ...` to the line (before the 200-character cut), for an ok plan and for one with problems (a plan that is not ok prints its warnings too). One warning is `review-owner <id> 'quality-guardian'`: the plan has a task owned by `quality-guardian` (case-insensitive; one trailing parenthetical such as `quality-guardian (tests)` is ignored for this warning only, while claim matching stays exact) at tier `frontier`, which the dispatch loop holds as `frontier-no-step`; review work is owned by `reviewer`. The only other warning is `near-miss-dependency`. A claim with the declared owner still succeeds. A plan with no task lines is the problem `no-tasks`. Exits 0 when the plan is ok, 1 when it has problems, 2 on a usage error (no path, a second path or an unknown flag) or an unreadable file (`plan-unreadable`). `validate` calls it for every `plan.md` and reports each problem under the rule `task-graph`; `/do-plan`'s self-review step calls it for its `findings` |
| `doflow review --action check [--slug <name> \| --path <findings.json>] [--json]` | Validate schema 1 `review/findings.json` with the shared `validateFindings` gate-b uses (`src/runtime/review/findings.ts`, re-exported by `gates.ts`), including optional `rounds`, `depth` and `risk`, `origin`/`preExistingAt`/`escapedFrom`, `verification`/`verifiedBy`, `bugClass`/`sweep` and `acceptedAs`. `--path` selects the file; otherwise it reads the active or `--slug` feature's `review/findings.json` in the checkout that holds the feature folder (this checkout when it has the folder, else the main checkout); `--slug` and `--path` together are a usage error. A `preExistingAt` commit outside `reviewedHead` ancestry is an error; when git cannot decide, `ancestry-unchecked` is advisory. `depth` must be the `depth` label of a review preset in `core/registry/protocols.json` (`single` or `deep`; error `depth`) and `risk` one of that file's risk levels (error `risk`); a record without either stays valid. A record's `protocol` is held to its preset: `rounds` below the preset's `rounds.min` is error `protocol-rounds` and fewer seats than the preset's is error `protocol-seats`; roadmap gate-b also denies (`findings-invalid`) a `protocol.id` looser than the project's `review.preset` when that is not `auto`. When the effective `review.preset` is not `auto`, a record without `protocol` is error `protocol-missing` (and its rounds and seats are held to that preset); under `auto` or unset it stays valid; an unreadable preset is error `review-preset-setting-unread`. Advisories include `unverified-blocker`, `round-cap` at 3, `bug-class-open` and `depth-unmet:<depth>` (the record names a `depth` but has fewer seats than that preset's `seats`; an advisory never changes the exit code). An optional `author` (`{agent, session, family}`, `session` a string or null, `family` a lower-case id or `unknown`; error `author`) and optional `agent`/`session`/`family` on a seat (error `seat-identity:<seatId>`) declare who wrote the change and who sat each seat; with an `author`, a seat with the author's `agent`, or with the same non-null `session` as the author's, is error `independence:<seatId>`, and a seat of the author's known `family` is advisory `same-family:<seatId>` (error under `review.sameFamily` `refuse`, read for the current project; error `same-family-setting-unread` when settings cannot be read or the project or local file holds an invalid `review.sameFamily`; roadmap gate-b applies the project's value and `review.preset` too, through its `review-settings` fact, and denies when the settings cannot be read, a project or local `review.*` value is invalid, or `review.preset` is unknown or invalid in any layer including the user file; a malformed user `settings.json` (bad JSON or another schema) therefore refuses review start, review check and gate-b in every project until it is fixed), while an unknown or missing family is `family-unverified:<seatId>`. The verb also compares `author.family` with the head's `Co-Authored-By` trailers (`author-trailer-contradiction`) and `author.agent` with the owners in the slug's task-attempt store (`author-attempt-contradiction`); an unreadable source is `author-unchecked:trailers` or `author-unchecked:attempts`. The author is a claim: these catch mistakes and contradictions, not a forged record. `--base` is a usage error with `--action check`. JSON is `{schema, path, valid, errors, advisories, counts}`; exits 0 valid, 1 invalid, missing or unreadable, 2 usage. The verb runs no upkeep and writes only one counts-only `review.run` telemetry line (`task` is null for an invalid record; `depth`, `risk` and `head`, the record's `reviewedHead`, are written from a valid record and are null for an invalid one or when the record omits `depth` or `risk`); through `doflow-run`, the seam also appends its pre-existing metadata-only run-ledger line (timestamp, verb, exit code, duration, argument count, run identity), as for every verb (`plan-check` is the precedent). `/do-code-review` runs it before handoff; `--action start` below runs first |
| `doflow review --action start [--slug <name>] [--base <ref>] [--json] [--preset <id>]` | Check the verify precondition on `HEAD` and resolve the review protocol (and with it the depth) in one call. `/do-code-review` runs it first and copies `depth` into `review/findings.json`, and `risk` only when `riskSource` is `record`. `--slug` defaults to the active feature and must be a plain feature name (`[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*`, at most 200 characters); `--base <ref>` is the commit or ref the changed paths are measured from and must be the review's own base (the commit the branch was cut from): a nearer commit measures only the commits after it, so a source change before it is not seen and a docs-only tail can give `single`; `/do-code-review` does not pass it, so the integration-base merge base is used; `--preset <id>` runs the review with that review preset (`review-deep`, `review-single`); it must be a plain preset id (lower-case letters, digits and hyphens), and a malformed or empty value, or `--preset` with `--action check`, is a usage error. A preset looser than the protocol the change needs is refused after the preconditions (exit 1, `error: preset-looser`), as is an id that is not a review preset (`preset-unknown`) and a project or local `review.*` setting that is invalid, or a `review.preset` that is unknown or invalid in any layer (user, project or local) (`settings-unavailable`, the message naming the key and file: the resolver would fall back to the default without a word); a stricter or equal one is taken (`raisedBy: run`). `--path` is a usage error, as is an empty flag value. The verb never prompts and reads no stdin. It writes no review record and no telemetry line; its only write is one advice pick or miss line, and only when a decision model is configured and `advice.kinds.review-depth` is not `off` (below), so a headless caller gets the same output. **Not applicable** (exit 0, `applicable: false`, `ok: true`, `reasons: []`; `notApplicable` is one of `no-feature` for no slug when the resolver answers `no-active-feature` (any other resolver error, or a feature slug that is not a plain name, is `feature-unresolved` and exit 1), `no-run` for a slug that has no orchestration run in either record layout (flat `orchestration/<slug>.json`, or `orchestration/<slug>/<taskId>.json`), `class-exempt` for a class whose registry stages have no `review` stage directly after a non-optional `verification` stage). From `core/registry/workflows.json` that is `feature`, `bug`, `refactor` and `dependency-change` applicable; `review`, `documentation`, `research`, `operations` and `trivial-edit` exempt. A not-applicable result still carries `depth`, decided at the default risk. **Refused** (exit 1, `ok: false`; `depth`, `seats`, `protocol`, `protocolDigest` and `depthBasis` are null): `reasons`, sorted and unique, hold `verify-missing`, `verify-not-pass` or `verify-flaky` (the rule gate-b's `verify-pass-on-head` applies to the run records of the slug on `HEAD` from a clean tree) and `tree-dirty` (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` is not empty or git cannot report). A run-record line that does not parse, or a record file that is not a regular file (a FIFO, a link, a device) or is over 8 MiB, exits 1 with `error: records-unreadable` and a `message`. The records read are those of the run's own `taskId` (the slug when the run names none). An uncertain applicability exits 1 and is never read as not applicable, and every `error` refusal prints `applicable: null`, not `false`: `error` is `run-ambiguous` (more than one run belongs to the slug), `run-unreadable` (the run file does not parse, is not a regular file or is over 1 MiB, names no `taskClass`, or names a `taskId` that is not a plain name; the orchestration store or the `<slug>/` folder cannot be listed for any reason other than being absent; or, when no run names the slug, a flat `<id>.json` of the store cannot be read or is not a regular file and so cannot be ruled out as the slug's run, the `message` naming the file), `class-unknown` (the run's `taskClass` is not in the workflows data), `feature-unresolved` (the active-feature resolver threw, returned an error other than `no-active-feature`, or returned a slug that is not a plain name) or `primary-refused` (when `resolvePrimary` returns `refused`, e.g. when session observation contradicts declared model under `primary.onContradiction: refuse`); each carries a `message`, `depth` and `seats` null, and the text form is `review start: <error>: <message>`. The text form of a refusal ends with the next command, `doflow verify --action report --task-id <slug>` (plus `--baseline-ref <ref>` when `--base` was given). **Depth** (exit 0): `single` (preset `review-single`) only when every changed path is covered by `reviewDepth.lightPaths` (a `/`-ending entry covers its subtree, any other entry that exact path) and the risk is in `reviewDepth.singleRisks`; otherwise `deep` (preset `review-deep`). The risk is the `risk` of the last clean matching run record (`riskSource: record`), else `policy.defaultRiskLevel` (`default`). An unresolved base, an unreadable or empty diff (the base resolves to `HEAD`, as on the integration branch itself) or an invalid risk gives `deep` (`depthBasis.scope` is `unknown`). `seats` is the seat count of the resolved protocol, whose seats are the preset's in `core/registry/protocols.json`; when the class's gate-b `minSeats` in `core/registry/gates.json` (narrowed by `.doflow/gates.override.json`) is larger, the next stricter preset with enough seats is taken, or the strictest preset gains copies of its last seat named `floor-<position>` (`raisedBy: gate-floor`; today `feature`, `bug`, `refactor`, `documentation` and `trivial-edit` have a floor, 2). The changed paths are `git diff --no-renames --name-only <base>` (so a rename lists both paths), with `<base>` from `--base` or else the integration-base merge base. The `review-depth` advice kind is asked with the decided depth as the static answer, in the mode `advice.kinds.review-depth` and the rollout profile give it. Only a kind set to `act` that has passed the bench bar can raise the depth: a `deep` pick over a decided `single` then gives `deep` (`raisedBy: advice`), and a `single` pick, an unavailable or off answer changes nothing. Nothing ships a passing verdict, so the depth is not raised today. With the kind set to `shadow`, to `act` that fell back to shadow, or left unset while a decision model is configured, the depth is never raised: the model is asked in a detached call the command does not wait for, and one pick or miss line is appended to the advice record when the call ends (a digest of the state, no text). With `advice.kinds.review-depth` set to `off`, or a chosen rollout profile whose advisor is `off`, nothing is asked; a profile whose advisor is `shadow` or `staged` holds the kind at shadow. A `--preset` wins over that raise: the run preset is compared with the pick before advice, so `--preset review-single` is taken (`raisedBy: run`) and the unapplied raise is listed in `protocol.disagreements` (`by: advice`, `reason: run-preset`), never a refusal. `--json` prints one object with these keys in this order: `schema` (1), `slug`, `taskClass`, `applicable`, `notApplicable`, `head`, `clean`, `ok`, `reasons`, `depth`, `seats`, `risk`, `riskSource`, `protocol` (the resolved protocol, folding the project's `review.*` settings: `id`, `kind`, `depth`, `seats` with their lenses, `rounds`, `crossAnswer`, `synthesizer`, `decider`, `lensesBySeat`, `blockerClauses`, `sameFamily`, `crossFamilyRereview`, `rereview`, `invariants`, `origins` naming where `id` and each other value came from as `registry`, `risk`, `advice`, `run`, `floor` or the settings layer that set it (`user`, `project`, `local`), `disagreements` (what a setting or advice asked for and was not applied: `field`, `by`, `value`, `reason` as `looser`, `below-min`, `above-cap` or `run-preset`) and `enforcement`; `seats` above equals its seat count), `protocolDigest` (the sha256 hex of the protocol's canonical JSON, the digest a findings record's `protocol.digest` carries; null when `protocol` is), `depthBasis` (`scope` as `light`, `code` or `unknown`, `files`, `base`, `floor`, `raisedBy` as `policy`, `gate-floor`, `advice`, `run`, `setting` or null), `advice` (`status` as `answered`, `shadow`, `unavailable` or `off`, and `pick`; `shadow` also carries `reason`: the `BENCH_BAR_UNMET: …` warning when an `act` setting fell back to shadow, else null), `authors` (authoring identities: kind, harness, model, channel) and `forbidden` (`(harness, model)` pairs forbidden by INV-8 from reviewing the change). The text form prints one line per key that is not null; `protocol` prints as four lines (`protocol:`, `protocol.seats:`, `protocol.origins:`, `protocol.invariants:`), for a review also a `protocol.rules:` line with the `sameFamily` and `crossFamilyRereview` values and one `protocol.blocker:` line per project blocker clause (control characters escaped), one `protocol.enforcement: <agent> native=<harnesses> unenforced=<harnesses>` line per read-only seat agent (the review seats are the `reviewer` agent; `enforcement` in the JSON lists, per harness, whether its read-only limit is enforced, and codex, pi and antigravity are `unenforced`; a seat agent's access is read from `core/registry/workflows.json` `agents.access`, and an agent it does not declare keeps its seat and gets rows of `status: unknown` with a reason, printed as a trailing `unknown=<harnesses>`, never a refusal and never treated as `write`), `authors:` and `forbidden:` lines, and `protocol.disagreements:` when there are any. `depth-unmet` (from `review --action check`) is advisory: nothing ties a non-feature record's seat count or `depth` to this verb's result, an accepted limit. Exit 0 applicable and ok, or not applicable; 1 refused, `records-unreadable`, `run-ambiguous`, `run-unreadable`, `class-unknown` or `feature-unresolved`; 2 usage |
| `doflow protocol --action list [--kind review\|debate] [--json]` | Print the protocol presets of `core/registry/protocols.json` in the registry's own strictest-first order (`--kind` keeps one kind): per preset its id, kind, `depth` for a review preset, seats with their lenses, rounds (`default` and `min`), `crossAnswer`, `synthesizer` and `decider`, then the invariants (`INV-1` to `INV-7`) with the verb that enforces each; an invariant applies to a review unless its `kinds` list in the registry also names `debate` (`INV-6` and `INV-7` do), and a resolved debate protocol lists only those. `--json` prints `{schema, presets, invariants}`. `--task-class`, `--risk` and `--preset` are a usage error with `list`. Read-only: the verb writes no file and no telemetry line and runs no upkeep. Exits 0, or 2 usage |
| `doflow protocol --action resolve --kind review\|debate [--task-class <id>] [--risk LOW\|MEDIUM\|HIGH\|CRITICAL] [--preset <id>] [--json]` | Print the protocol a run would use, resolved as `review --action start` resolves it (`resolveProtocol`) for the project in the current directory, reading that project's settings (`review.*`, `debate.preset`), so a review or debate brief can embed the text verbatim. The text form is four lines (`protocol:`, `protocol.seats:`, `protocol.origins:`, `protocol.invariants:`) plus, for a review, a `protocol.rules:` line (`sameFamily`, `crossFamilyRereview`), one `protocol.blocker:` line per project blocker clause and a `protocol.enforcement:` line per read-only seat agent (a debate has none of these); a project or local `review.*` setting that is invalid, or a `review.preset` or `debate.preset` that is unknown or invalid in any layer (user, project or local), is refused with `settings-unavailable` naming the key and file; `--json` prints `{schema, ok, protocol}` with `origins` naming where `id` and each other value came from and `enforcement` rows `{agent, harness, status, reason}`, one per harness, saying whether its read-only limit is enforced. The verb has no diff, so the changed paths are unknown and a review is never `single`: it resolves to `review-deep`, and `--risk` (default `policy.defaultRiskLevel`) cannot lower that. A debate starts at `debate-2`. `--task-class` (a class in `core/registry/workflows.json`) sets the gate-b seat floor, read with `.doflow/gates.override.json` as `review --action start` reads it; without it the floor is 0. `--preset` must be a preset of the asked kind and at least as strict as the pick: a looser one is refused with `preset-looser`, a preset that does not exist for the kind with `preset-unknown`, an incomplete registry with `registry-incomplete` (text `protocol: <error>: <message>`, or `{ok: false, error, message}` with `--json`). Read-only, as `list`. Exits 0 resolved, 1 refused, 2 usage (unknown action, kind, class or risk, a missing or empty flag value, a malformed preset id) |
| `doflow rules [--task-class <id>] [-g] [--json] [<project>]` | Print the values the runtime owns for a project, each with its source, so guidance and policy text can point here instead of copying numbers. Four sections. **review**: for each task class of `core/registry/gates.json` (or the one named), the protocol `review --action start` resolves with no diff and no `--preset`, twice by the real `resolveProtocol`: *attended* (seat floor 0) and *unattended* (the class's gate-b `minSeats`, the floor that binds when autopilot is on). Each row has the preset id, seats (count and agent roles), rounds (`min`, `max` the registry's hard cap, `default`, `effective` with `review.rounds` applied), `crossAnswer`, `synthesizer`, `origin` (`registry`, `floor`, `risk`, or the settings layer `user`, `project`, `local`) and `source` (the file or `settings:<layer>`), and `disagreements` when a setting was ignored. **gates**: per class and gate `autopilot` and `minSeats` and the `loop` limits (`maxSteps`, `wip`), read from the packaged `core/registry/gates.json` (not the working tree), and `projectPolicy`: whether the project's local `develop` holds a committed `core/registry/gates.json` and whether it equals the packaged one (read with `git`, the only process the verb spawns). **tiers**: owner to tier from `core/registry/models.json` `dispatchTiers`, and for every installed or bound harness the binding per tier from the global-only `workers.models` setting (each a binding cell as `models --action list` shows it, `null` is the harness default; the text form and the brief's `Model:` line use the same function), the orchestrator's resolved model and where it came from, and the main session (`primary.harness`, `primary.model`). **settings**: value, layer and source of `review.preset`, `review.rounds`, `review.sameFamily` and `autopilot.enabled` (the last read from the global view). Anything the runtime cannot resolve (an invalid setting, an unreadable registry) is the string `unresolved:<reason>` for that field and for the rows that depend on it, never dropped and never guessed. `<project>` defaults to the current directory; `-g` reads the user layer only and takes no project; `--task-class` must be a class of the gates registry. The text form is one line per row (about 40 lines for all classes); `--json` prints `{schema: 1, review: {classes}, gates, tiers, settings}`. Read-only: writes no file and no telemetry and runs no upkeep. Exits 0, or 2 usage (unknown class or flag, a second project, `-g` with a project, a project that is not a directory) |
| `doflow package --action points [--json]` | List the contribution points of `core/registry/contribution-points.json` a package may fill, in file order: per point its id, supported versions, tier (`inert`, `instruction`, `executable`), status, entry kind (`file` or `dir`) and, for a reserved point, what it opens with. `review-preset`, `debate-preset`, `skill`, `agent`, `worker`, `capability-provider` and `intake-category` are `open`; no point is reserved today, and a contribution to a reserved point would be refused `point-reserved`. A package declares a control channel (`worker.controlChannel`, row 170) in the `worker` point, not in a point of its own. Read-only: it writes no file and no telemetry. `--json` prints `{ points: [...] }`. Exit 0 listed, 1 the registry file is unreadable or breaks its closed shape (the error names the field), 2 usage (a missing or unknown `--action`). Other `package` actions arrive with later tasks of the extension model. |
| `doflow package --action check --path <dir> [--json]` | Check a package directory without writing anything: the manifest `doflow-package.json` (closed key set, duplicate keys, [forbidden fields](#package-manifest-forbidden-fields), id grammar and reserved ids, host range, each contribution against its point and the point's validator, a preset's comparability with a core preset), the tree rules (a symlink, special file, path segment outside the grammar, a path leaving the package root, more than the file, byte, per-file or path-length limits of `contribution-points.json`) and, when there is no error, the `contentDigest` and `capabilityDigest`. One finding per line (`<severity> <code> <field>: <message>`); `--json` prints `{ path, ok, findings, contentDigest?, capabilityDigest? }` with each finding `{ package, field, code, severity, message, allowed }`. A running DoFlow whose version cannot be read (an install without `package.json`) fails closed: the manifest gets `host-incompatible`. Collisions with recorded packages are judged by the lifecycle verbs, not here. Read-only: it writes no file and no telemetry. Exit 0 clean, 1 findings of severity `error` or an unreadable point registry, 2 usage (no `--path`, or more than one). |
| `doflow package --action list [--id <id>] [--json]` | Print the effective state of every package, or of the one `--id`. It lists the bundled first-party package (`core/registry/contribution-points.json` `bundled`) and every third-party package recorded in the global lock (`add`, see the rows below), each with its state. A bundled package is `active` and `enabled` unless its directory or `doflow-package.json` is missing or breaks a rule of `check`, then `incompatible` with the finding codes as `reasons`. The bundled tree is not re-hashed at run time: `contentDigest` is the registry's value. Each package is judged alone, so a faulty one never changes another or any other command. Read-only and offline: it needs no harness binary and writes no file and no telemetry. `--json` prints `{ points, packages, findings, lockVersion }` (the Console contract): `points` as `package --action points`, each package `{ id, version, source, firstParty, tierMax, enabled, state, reasons, contributions, grant, contentDigest, capabilityDigest, storeDir }`, `findings` as `check` prints them, and `lockVersion` the version of the global lock on disk (1 when none). When that version is 2, text output ends with one line saying an older DoFlow cannot read this lock and refuses it; `--json` keeps its keys and states the fact as `lockVersion` 2. With a version 1 lock or none there is no such line. An unreadable point registry gives no packages and one `state-unreadable` finding. Text output also names, per package with a skill or agent, each harness of the global lock that cannot take one (`warning: <harness> cannot take <point> <qualified id>: <reason>`, NFR-003), or says that no harness is installed at global scope, so nothing is projected (a project-scope install never takes package skills or agents); `show` prints the same lines. Exit 0, 1 when a package is neither `active` nor `disabled`, any finding is an error, or `--id` names no package. |
| `doflow package --action show --id <id> [--json]` | Print the state of one package (`--json`: the one object). Exit 0, 1 for an unknown id, 2 without `--id`. |
| `doflow package --action add --source <spec> [--json]` | Plan adding a local directory, `git:<https url>[#<subdir>]@<ref>`, or `npm:<name>@<version or range>`. Unsupported schemes, URL credentials and unsafe specs are `bad-type`. Remote specs require a fetch grant naming the spec exactly before any git/npm call; without it, exit 1 `grant-missing` prints the exact line and the file a person appends it to. With the grant, git refs or npm ranges are resolved once, then fetched by full commit or exact version plus sha512 integrity. Text prints `pinned <url> <commit>` or `pinned <name>@<version>`. Remote files are retained in `~/.doflow/packages/fetched/<planId>/`; tool staging is removed afterwards. The checked directory produces `~/.doflow/packages/plans/<planId>.json` with the source (pinned for remote), inventory, digests, policy version, programs, projection, findings and any required trust line. Projection names skills/agents and listing bytes per globally installed harness, with warnings for unreachable contributions. A plan changes no record, installs nothing and activates nothing; fetch grants confer no trust. Checker errors write no plan. A local add writes only the plan, as before. Plans and fetched snapshots have a 24-hour retention bound. Exit 0 planned, 1 refused, 2 usage. |
| `doflow package --action add --index <file> --id <id> [--version <v>] [--json]` | Read a local IC-023 JSON index of at most 4 MiB: closed keys `{format:1,packages:[{id,versions:[{version,doflow,source}]}]}`. Each source is pinned git `{type,url,commit,path?}` or npm `{type,name,version,integrity}`. Any bad entry refuses the entire file with a finding naming its JSON path. An unpinned source is `source-unpinned`. Without `--version`, choose the highest non-prerelease version whose host range admits this DoFlow; no match is `host-incompatible`. An unknown id or exact version is `bad-id`, naming the choices. The index grants nothing. Before fetching, a grant must name its pinned git source as `{type:"git",url,ref:<commit>,path?}` or npm source as `{type:"npm",name,version:<exact>}`; no grant is `grant-missing` with the same fetch-line output as `--source`. Then pin output, planning, apply and trust follow the source flow. For `package`, `--version <v>` selects the index version; `--version` alone remains the CLI version flag. |
| `doflow package --action add --plan <planId> [--json]` | Apply a plan under the global run lock: re-snapshot the local source or retained `fetched/<planId>/` tree and recompute both digests. No network call occurs. A missing/changed fetched tree or differing digest is `digest-mismatch` (exit 1, nothing stored and the lock untouched). Copy to `~/.doflow/packages/store/.staging-<planId>/`, re-hash, rename to `store/<id>/<first 16 hex of content digest>/`, write the `.installed-from.json` marker beside the tree and write the global lock row last, carrying the pinned remote source when applicable and `enabled: false`. The package lands `disabled`; trust and enable remain separate. An already recorded id is `collision-package`. A failure after rename removes the new entry before touching the lock. Upkeep prunes plans, their retained snapshots and stale staging directories under the declared bounds. |
| `doflow package --action update --id <id> [--json]` | Plan an update from the recorded local source of `<id>`: the same plan file as `add --source`, with `action: "update"`. Remote recorded sources are refused with guidance to remove and use `add --source` or `add --index` with a new spec. Writes nothing else; an unknown id exits 1, neither `--id` nor `--plan` exits 2. |
| `doflow package --action update --plan <planId> [--json]` | Apply an update plan as `add --plan` does, replacing the lock row (the enable bit and `addedAt` are kept) and removing the previous store entry once the new row is written. When the content digest changed, the package's projected skills and agents are brought in line under the run lock (above `inert` the old grant no longer holds, so they are unprojected). A trust grant names the content digest it was given for, so a changed digest voids it by construction (no second mechanism): above the `inert` tier, applying an update whose content digest differs from the recorded one prints the `trust` line for the new digests and one sentence saying the package acts again only after a person adds that line to the grants path (appended next to any older trust line for the same package version, which no longer grants; `--json` adds `grantLines.trust`, `grantFile` and `note`). |
| `doflow package --action new --id <id> --points <point[:local],...> [--dir <path>] [--json]` | Scaffold an authored package, version `0.1.0`, from the open points' templates, validating the staged tree with `check` before publishing it. The default folder is `<project root>/.doflow/package/<id>/`; `--dir` resolves relative to cwd. Without a detected project, `--dir` is required. Writes only the target folder (and the package telemetry event): it installs, enables and trusts nothing. Prints each file and the ordered next steps: edit, check, add, apply, enable, trust only above inert, then iterate. Headless calls never prompt or read stdin. `--json` prints `{ ok, id, dir, files, contributions, tierMax, contentDigest, capabilityDigest, ignoredByGit, findings, next }`. Refusals include `template-missing` (a point names no template), `template-invalid` (invalid template or staged tree), `target-not-empty` (existing contents are preserved) and `target-forbidden` (the real target is inside the package store, any `.doflow/state/`, the config home or runtime root). Staging siblings older than 24 hours are removed by the next scaffold in that parent. Git ignore checking is read-only; when ignored, the output names `!.doflow/package/` as a negation a person may add. DoFlow never edits `.gitignore`. Exit 0 created, 1 refused, 2 usage; usage lists the open points with templates. |
| `doflow package --action enable --id <id> [--json]` | Set the package's lock bit under the global scope's run lock and print the resulting state (any caller; nothing prompts). An `inert` package goes `active`. Above `inert` with no valid `trust` grant it is `needs-trust`, exit 0: the output carries the exact `trust` line, the grants file path and the sentence that a person appends it (DoFlow never writes `grants.jsonl`; with no config home it says no grant can exist). A package in the state `incompatible`, or an id no lock row and no bundled package names, is exit 1 and changes nothing; no `--id` is exit 2. The bundled package `doflow` always enables: its lock row is dropped. When the resulting state is `active` on a grant, the grant line it matched, the grants file path and its mtime are printed (`list` and `show` print them too in text output; the `list --json` and `show --json` shapes are unchanged). When the package is `active` its skills and agents are projected (see the row "Package skills and agents in harnesses" below); with no harness installed at global scope the output says nothing is projected. `--json` prints `{ ok, action, id, state, reasons, enabled, matched, grantLines?, grantFile?, projection? }`. |
| `doflow package --action disable --id <id> [--json]` | Clear the lock bit (the bundled package gets a closed-shape `bundled` lock row with `enabled: false`) and keep the grant, so enabling again needs no new line, then unproject the package's skills and agents (a copy a person edited is kept and named; with nothing projected the output says so). Exit 0 for a known id, 1 for an unknown one. |
| `doflow package --action trust --id <id> [--json]` | Print the `trust` line for the recorded content digest and the capability digest the package view computes (`policy` is the registry's `trustPolicyVersion`, `at` the print time in whole seconds) and the grants file path. It reads `grants.jsonl` first: a valid, unrevoked line with the same key prints `already granted by line N` and no line (`--json`: `alreadyGranted`); a revoked line with the same key is named and the printed line replaces it (`replaceLine`), because a second line with that key voids the file; otherwise the line is appended. When a package was previously removed (a `removed` tombstone exists in `state.json`) and declares worker contributions, the printed trust line carries the `rebind` key listing its worker ids and lists every `workers.billing`, `workers.enabled` and `workers.models` setting that would apply again. Writes no file: `grants.jsonl` stays byte-identical, and no config home means no grant can exist and the line is printed with that sentence. Exit 0 for a known id (an `inert` package says no grant is required, the bundled package says it needs none), 1 for an unknown one. |
| `doflow package --action exempt --id <id> [--json]` | Print the `exempt` line (`acceptRisk: true`) for the running DoFlow version, the package version and its recorded content digest, and the grants file path. Like `trust`, it says `already granted by line N`, or names the line the printed one replaces (a revoked one, or one for another host). Exit 0 when the host version is outside the package's `doflow` range, 1 when it is inside the range (no exemption is needed), the package is bundled, or the version or the manifest cannot be read, and for an unknown id. Writes no file. |
| `doflow package --action revoke --id <id> [--json]` | Record a revocation of trust for the package's recorded content digest in `~/.doflow/packages/state.json` (mode 0600, under the run lock). A `trust` or `exempt` grant counts again only when its `at` is later than the revocation, so the person runs `trust --id` and puts its line in place of the revoked one, which the output names (the fresh line has the revoked line's key); `grants.jsonl` is never touched. The package's skills and agents are unprojected. Exit 0 for a known id, 1 for an unknown one, or when `state.json` is unreadable (run `verify --repair`). |
| `doflow package --action verify [--repair] [--json]` | Re-hash every enabled third-party entry through the package view and record (or clear) its suspension in `state.json`: a store entry that is missing or hashes to another content digest is `suspended` with reason `content-changed`. It then brings the projection in line: an active package's skills and agents are projected, a suspended or otherwise inactive package's are removed. Exit 0 when every enabled entry is intact, 1 on a suspended or incompatible entry, an unreadable `state.json`, or another fault of the lock, the grants file or the point registry. `--repair` rewrites only an unreadable `state.json`, with one revocation per grant line present at that moment, so every grant stays void until the person replaces it with a line printed after the repair (run `trust --id` or `exempt --id`, whose output names the line); on a readable file it changes nothing. |
| `doflow package --action remove --id <id> [--json]` | Remove a third-party package under the global scope's run lock, in this order: clear the lock bit, unproject the package's skills and agents and drop their ledger rows (a copy a person edited is kept and named), delete the store entry, its `.installed-from.json` marker and the id directory once empty, drop the lock row (a lock left with no package and no target is deleted), record a revocation for the recorded content digest, then a `removed` tombstone in `state.json` naming the package's qualified ids (`<id>.<local>` of every contribution in its recorded manifest). It then runs storage upkeep like the other writers and prints what still names the package, rewriting none of it: the settings values (the user, project and local layers of the current directory) of every key a contribution point lists in `referencedBy` that equal a qualified id or hold it in a list, as `dangling setting <layer> <key> = <value>`; every `findings.json` under the project's `.doflow/` whose `protocol.id` is a qualified id, as `dangling record <path> protocol.id = <id>`; and each grant line for the package in `grants.jsonl` (line number, kind, version) with the sentence that the person may delete it. `--json` prints `{ ok, action, id, version, qualifiedIds, dangling: [{ kind: "setting"\|"record", layer\|path, key\|field, value }], unscanned, grants, grantFile, leftover }`. Exit 0 once removed, 1 for an unknown id (nothing is written: no lock, store, state or audit row), for the bundled `doflow` (it cannot be removed; use `disable`) and for an unreadable `state.json` (nothing is changed; run `verify --repair`), 2 without `--id`. |
| Package skills and agents in harnesses | An `active` third-party package's `skill` and `agent` contributions are projected by the lifecycle, at global scope only, into every harness of the global lock's `targets`, through that harness's own renderer for DoFlow's skills (`skills.doflow`) and agents (`agents.shared`): a skill as the directory `<package>-<local>` beside DoFlow's skills, copied byte for byte, an agent as DoFlow's agents are rendered there. An agent file is projected only from a directory of its own. Each projected file is a ledger row with the ownership identity `doflow:<harness>:package:<qualified id>:<path>` (no scope, no destination). `enable` and `verify` project, and so do `install`, `update` and `reconcile` for the harnesses they run; `disable`, `revoke`, `remove`, an `update --plan` that changes the content digest, a suspension and a `doflow remove` unproject: a file whose bytes are still DoFlow's is removed, a file a person edited is kept and only released (the output names it). The `packages.disabled` setting does not change what is projected, because harness files serve every project. A harness whose `skills` or `agents` capability is `unavailable` gets nothing of that point and a warning; a project-scope install gets no package projection. A fault in one package (a person's own file at the destination, an unreadable store entry) is a warning and leaves that package's files as they are; it never fails the command. `--json` of `enable`, `disable`, `revoke`, `verify`, `remove` and `update --plan` adds `projection: { projected, unprojected, kept, warnings }` when there is anything to report. |
| Audit and telemetry of the verb actions | `enable`, `disable`, `trust`, `exempt`, `revoke`, `verify` and `remove` each append one `audit` row (`at`, `verb`, `package`, `result`; the latest 64 are kept) to `state.json` under the run lock, and write one `package.verb` telemetry line (`action`, `result`, `tier_max`; no package id or path; see the telemetry contract). The audit row is skipped, never an error, while `state.json` is unreadable. All seven are open to any caller and never prompt or wait on a person; `remove` runs storage upkeep, the other six stay on the no-upkeep list. An id no package carries appends no audit row. |
| `doflow primary --action resolve [--json]` | Print which harness and model the main session is taken to run, resolved by `resolvePrimary` for the project in the current directory from the `primary.harness` and `primary.model` settings and the model the harness reported at session start. The text form is one line per field in this order: `state`, `harness`, `model`, `family`, `declared` (`<harness>/<model> (<origin layer>[, model <origin layer>])`), `observed` (`<model> (<event>, <time>, sessions <n>)`), `probe` (`payload` or `none`), `reason`, then one `warning:` line each; a missing part prints `-`. `--json` prints the same object on one line. The state is one of `unknown` (nothing declared), `declared-only` (declared, and the harness reports no model), `unconfirmed` (the harness reports one but none was seen, sessions disagree, or the only one seen came from a compaction, which may be a subagent's; `reason` is `no-observation`, `ambiguous-sessions` or `unattributed-observation`), `confirmed`, `contradicted` (observed differs from declared; the warning names both), `refused` (contradicted while `primary.onContradiction` is `refuse`) and `not-primary` (the session is a worker). `doflow status` and `doflow doctor` each print one line with the state, harness, model and origin layer (`status --json` gains `primary`, `doctor --json` gains `primary` with `state`, `harness`, `model`, `origin` and `probe`). Read-only: the verb writes no file and no telemetry line, runs no upkeep and no probe. Exits 0 for every state except `refused` (1); 2 usage (an action other than `resolve`, or a flag other than `--action` and `--json`) |
| `doflow slot --action run\|list\|release [--name <name>] [--label <text>] [--timeout <seconds>] [--no-wait] [--by <who>] [--reason <text>] [--json] [-- <command> [args...]]` | A named, machine-wide queue that lets one command at a time run, for work that must not overlap across checkouts or sessions (a full test suite, a build). The name is 1 to 64 characters of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, starting with a letter or digit; capacity is one holder per name, there is no load threshold, and no verb takes a slot by itself: a caller opts in by wrapping its command. `run --name <name> [--label <text>] [--timeout <s>] [--no-wait] -- <command> [args...]` queues behind earlier waiters, then runs the command with inherited stdio and the environment plus `DOFLOW_SLOT=<name>`, forwards `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` to it, releases the slot when it ends and exits with its code (128 plus the signal number when a signal ended it, 127 when it could not start). On POSIX the command is started through an `sh` exec wrapper (same process id) only once the slot names it durably, so a runner that dies before that leaves the command unrun with exit 125, and a command that cannot be run reports `sh`'s exit 127 message. The grant line `doflow slot: <name> granted after <s> s (<label>)` goes to stderr, so stdout belongs to the command. `--label` (at most 80 characters) defaults to `<command>@<directory>`. `--timeout` (1 to 86400 seconds) gives up waiting and `--no-wait` refuses at once when the slot is held or another live waiter is ahead; both print the holder to stderr and exit 75. A missing `--`, an empty command, a bad name or timeout, or an unknown flag exits 2. `list` prints one line per slot (`<name>  held by <pid> (<label>) since <iso>; <n> waiting`, or `free`), `no slots held` when none, and with `--json` `{ok, slots:[{name, holder, waiters}]}`; exit 0. `release --name <name> --by <who> --reason <text>` clears the holder unless its `slot run` process or its recorded child is known to be alive, and records who and why in the slot file; a live holder is refused with exit 1 and `disposition: holder-alive`, a slot with no holder answers `free`, and success answers `released`. The verb never signals a process other than its own child. Slot files live in the `slots-user` store, one JSON file per name, deleted when the slot has no holder and no waiter. Exits 1 as well when `HOME` is not an absolute path (`no-home`) or a DoFlow-owned path component is a link (`slots-unsafe`); no skill calls it. After it reclaims a lock left behind, the verb waits 10 s before taking the slot and says so on stderr in one line starting `doflow slot:`. |
| `doflow primary --action observed [--json]` | List the project's checkouts and the hook records of the sessions that worked in them, for the Console's Sessions view (093 IC-018 a). Checkouts come from `git worktree list --porcelain` (DoFlow sandboxes left out), each as `worktreeKey` (the 16-hex hash of the checkout top), `role` (`main` or `linked`), `current`, `branch` (null when detached), `head` (12 hex) and `headCommittedAt` (from `git log -1 --format=%cI HEAD`, as UTC). Sessions are read from the config home: the main-session model observations at each checkout top (within the `model-observations` store's age bound; one recorded from a compaction, `SessionStart:compact`, is left out) and every `session-env/sessions/<id>/git-context.json` whose `cwd` lies in one of the checkouts (longest prefix), at most 2000 directories, newest first; an observation and a session directory with the same `sessionKey` (the first 16 hex of the sha256 of the session id) are one entry. Each entry is `sessionKey`, `worktreeKey`, `kind` (`launched` for a directory named `L.<run>.<lineage>.<attempt>`, else `hook`), `harness`, `model`, `modelFirstSeenAt`, `contextCapturedAt` (the `captured_at` of `git-context.json`, which every `SessionStart` rewrites, a compaction included, so it is the last time the session's context was captured and not when the session began: no start is recorded; the text form labels it `context-captured`), `branchAtStart`, `lastHookRecordAt` (the newest file time in the session directory) and `launched` (`{run, lineage, attempt}` or null). `--json` prints `{v: 1, store, checkouts, sessions, truncated, unreadable}`; the text form prints one line per checkout and per session. No path, pid, host or raw session id is printed, and sessions of other projects are left out. Read-only: no lock, no directory created, no upkeep. With no config home `store` is `absent` and `sessions` empty, exit 0; when git cannot list the checkouts it prints `{v: 1, error: "cannot-list-checkouts"}` and exits 1; 2 usage |
| `doflow slot --action peek [--json]` | Read every slot file as it is on disk, for the Console's Sessions view (093 IC-018 b): no lock, no sweep of dead entries, no directory created (a missing slots directory is an empty list) and no upkeep. Each slot with a holder or a waiter is `{name, holder: {label, since, alive, worktreeKey} or null, waiters}`: `alive` is `true`, `false` or `unknown` from the holder's `slot run` process and its recorded child, computed and not written, so a dead holder stays listed with `alive: false` until the next `run`, `list` or `release` clears it; `worktreeKey` is the hash of the checkout the holder's directory lies in when that is one of the project's checkouts, else null; `waiters` counts the waiters not known to be dead. `--json` prints `{v: 1, slots, unreadable}`, where `unreadable` counts slot files that cannot be parsed; the text form prints one line per slot. The holder's `pid`, `start` and `cwd` and the waiters' details are never printed. Exit 0; 1 on `no-home` or `slots-unsafe` as the other actions; 2 usage |
| `doflow ui [<project>] [--port N] [--lan [--listen-address <private IP>] \| --listen-address <private IP> --tls-cert <absolute path> --tls-key <absolute path>]`; `doflow ui [<project>] --action pair [--scope view\|control]`; `doflow ui --action devices [--json]`; `doflow ui --action revoke --device <id>` | Run the DoFlow Console (093, 092 IC-008, 160), a local web UI shipped as the separate package `@khoavu882/doflow-ui`, which shows runs and layers and, since row 162, can decide a gate, close a run or stop a worker, and, since row 165, start a session in a checkout; the Console's flags, security model, routes, actions and views are in [`console/README.md`](https://github.com/khoavu882/do-flow/blob/develop/console/README.md). The launcher finds the package by file existence and never loads it into the core: first the `doflow-ui` directory next to this core in `node_modules/@khoavu882/`, then `node_modules/@khoavu882/doflow-ui` walking up from the project (the `<project>` directory, else the working directory), then the `console/` directory of a development checkout of this core (accepted only when its `package.json` is named `@khoavu882/doflow-ui`). It prints `doflow ui: using <path to the package's bin/doflow-ui.js>` on stderr, then starts that file with the running Node, `--core <this core's bin/doflow.js>`, `--project <abs dir>` when `<project>` is given, and every other flag it was given, with inherited stdio. It checks only which flags may be combined (exit 2 otherwise); the Console judges the values. Without `--lan`, stdout carries the Console's loopback HTTP launch link. `--lan` serves HTTPS on a private address with a certificate the Console generates and keeps (a self-signed ECDSA P-256 certificate with one IP SAN per address, under `<config home>/console/tls`), on the remembered port or else the first free one of 47380 to 47399 (`--port` wins), and prints one block (it checks the certificate's end date daily and at each pairing, and serves no request past it): the address, the certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, a one-use 5-minute pairing code, a link carrying the code in its fragment, and a QR code on a terminal. A phone pairs once by opening the link, comparing the fingerprint and sending the code (`POST /api/pair`); it then holds a device credential (an `HttpOnly` cookie for a browser, a Bearer token for a native client) that expires after 30 days without use and is limited to the routes on one list, `console/src/scopes.ts`. Scope `view` reads info, runs, workflows, the live stream, sessions, the worker-run reads, `GET /api/harness-login` and `GET /api/actions`. Scope `control` reads the same and may also use `GET /api/start`, `GET /api/start/options` and the four action routes `POST /api/actions/decide-gate`, `close-run`, `cancel-worker` and `start-session`, but only over the native Bearer transport: a `control` credential carried in a browser cookie is refused `403 action-transport`. Only a native client's Bearer pairing, which pins the certificate, can hold `control`; a browser-paired device is `view` for ever, because a browser's fingerprint check can be faked by a hostile page. On loopback, the session that exchanged the launch code acts too (a `local` session, behind the CSRF checks every non-GET request passes). Every action is relayed to the core as one fixed `doflow` command with a `--via` label (and, for the three that act on a run, the run's expected revision; a start carries its request id and the first text on stdin), limited in rate, written to the action log (`console-action-log`, below: no free text) and shown behind a confirmation dialog; the routes, limits and refusals are in `console/README.md`. A browser on a phone, and an Android WebView, is browser class: it views only, until a native shell (row 163) supplies the Bearer transport. The device store is machine-wide, `<config home>/console/lan.json`, at most 64 active devices; a revoked or idle-expired one is kept for 30 days so it reads as revoked or expired. `--action pair` prints a fresh code block for the Console already running; `--action devices` lists the paired devices (`--json` for a machine-readable list); `--action revoke --device <id>` ends one device's access at its next request. The three actions run without the core handshake and listen on nothing. The older three-flag form (`--listen-address`, `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key` together) still serves your own certificate over HTTPS but pairs devices the same way. The launcher opens no browser, listens on nothing, reads no DoFlow state and installs nothing. It stays up until the Console exits and exits with its code (a Console killed by a signal takes the launcher down by the same signal); `SIGINT` and `SIGTERM` reach the Console as `SIGTERM`. Exit 2 on usage errors or when the Console package is not installed; exit 1 when the Console process cannot be started or an action fails |
| `doflow fleet --action projects [--json]` | List the projects this machine knows, for the Console (093 IC-019; the Layers view and the `events` `machine` channel read it). Two read-only stores are joined: the user-state `upkeep-roots` (one absolute root per line) and the `cwd` of each `<config home>/session-env/projects/*/meta.json` (at most 2000 directories, newest first; `truncated` is set past that). A candidate is kept when it is an existing directory that is not a symlink, its git work-tree top holds `.doflow/` (outside git, the directory itself), and it is not test scratch (a path under a folder named `doflow-test.noindex`, the one root every test temp folder lives in; counted in `excluded.temp`). A project under `realpath(os.tmpdir())`, `/private/tmp` or `/private/var/folders` that is not test scratch is listed with `temp: true` (else `false`); candidates that share a git common dir (`git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`) are one project. Each project is `projectKey` (the 16-hex hash of the main checkout top), `root` (the main checkout, printed by `--json` only and meant for the Console server, which drops it), `label` (its basename, at most 64 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9 ._:/@-]`, else `(withheld)`), `temp` (the main checkout is under a temp folder), `current` (the project of the working directory), `checkouts` (non-sandbox `git worktree list` entries), `sources` (`upkeep-roots`, `hook-dirs`) and `lastRecordAt` (the newest `last_active` of its `meta.json` files and the mtime of `.doflow/state/upkeep-due`), sorted by `current` first, then newest. `--json` prints `{v: 1, store, projects, excluded: {missing, temp, notDoflow, unreadable}, truncated}`; the text form prints one line per project and never a path. The `cwd`, `last_agent` and `branch` of a `meta.json` are never printed. A project that never wrote DoFlow state and never started a hooked session is in neither store, so it is not listed. Read-only: no lock, no directory created, no upkeep (`fleet` is in `NO_UPKEEP`). With no home `store` is `absent` and `projects` empty, exit 0; git missing prints `{v: 1, error: "cannot-list-projects"}` and exits 1; any other flag or action exits 2 |
| `doflow fleet --action tree [--json]` | One project's agent hierarchy as nodes and edges (093 IC-020), run with the working directory in that project. Nodes: `main-session` (hook observations joined to the session directory, at most 64), `harness-subagent` (the harness's own sub-agent records read through the transcript readers: Claude `subagents/*.meta.json`, Codex `session_meta.source`, Copilot `subagent.started`, Pi `parentSession`, Kiro `parent_session_id`), `doflow-run` (task attempts by run and lineage), `worker-run` and `child-worker-run` (worker runs, with the binding model, `ended` from the end record and a process check for at most 16 not-ended runs) and `started-session` (a run of kind `main-session` started by `worker --action start-session`, row 165, under its own agent key `sha16("worker:<runId>")`: no caller or attempt edge and no task hold, `modelBasis` `start-record`). A `started-session` node adds `runId`, `startedBy`, `phase`, `end` (null until the run ends, then `{state, exitCode, reason}`: `exitCode` is the process's exit code or null, `reason` is why a process never started or null), `fileKeys` (`{out, err}`, the keys of the two output logs) and `observed`: `{state: "observed", sessionKey, model, lastSeenAt}` once a hook session names the run (`started_run` in its `git-context.json`, written by `session-context` from `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN`), else `{state: "not-observed", reason}` with `not-yet-observed` when `core/registry/lifecycle.json` maps a session-start hook for the harness (`supported` or `different`) and `no-session-hook` when it does not. The join is on the exact run id only; the hook session is then not also a `main-session` node, and the node keeps the run's key, file keys and Stop and gains the session's `contextCapturedAt`, model (when the run has none) and transcript. Each node has `agentKey` (16 hex), `harness` (a registry id or `(other)`), `model` with its `modelBasis`, `label` (only the record's own role text, never invented; no "lead" unless a record says so), `depth`, `worktreeKey`, `startedAt` (a worker run's or a sub-agent's start; null for a `main-session` node, whose start is not recorded), `contextCapturedAt` (for a `main-session` node the `contextCapturedAt` of `primary --action observed`: the last context capture; null for every other kind), `lastRecordAt`, `liveness` (`process-seen`, `process-gone` or `not-checked`) and `transcript` `{reader, linked, reason}` (`not-linked` for Antigravity, `no-reader` for OpenCode, `not-found` when the reader finds no file for the session id). Each edge has a `basis`: `harness-subagent-record`, `worker-caller`, `worker-childOf`, `attempt-runKey` or `dispatch-claim` (the dispatch `primary` snapshot, read only when a dispatch store exists). Nodes merge only on a shared key (a run key, a run id, a session id), never on a harness or model. `--json` prints `{v: 1, projectKey, nodes, edges, limits: {nodes: 256, notChecked}, unreadable}`; the text form prints keys, labels and bases and never a path or raw id. The transcript is joined by session id alone and nothing is written (no lock, no directory, no upkeep). Another action or flag exits 2; git unable to list checkouts prints `{v: 1, error: "cannot-list-checkouts"}` and exits 1 |
| `doflow transcript --action read --agent <key> [--cursor <c>] [--limit <n>] [--json]` | One agent's recent events, for the Console's Layers view (093 IC-022), run with the working directory in that project. The agent is resolved inside core from the `fleet --action tree` node set of the project (a key outside it exits 1 with `agent-not-in-project`); its transcript is the file the tree joined by session id, and the transcript's own `cwd` must lie in one of the project's checkouts (else `session-not-in-project`, exit 1). The file, its header and its companion are read only as regular files below the harness's transcript directory (the registry location the tree found it under), with no symlink below that directory and the open descriptor checked against what was seen; anything else reads as `transcript: {linked: false, reason: 'not-found'}`. The body is `{v: 1, agentKey, harness, reader: {id, status}, transcript: {linked, reason}, events, cursor, reset, eof, otherRecords, unparsable, masked, warnings}`. An event is `seq`, `at`, `kind` (`reply`, `tool-call`, `tool-result`, `usage`, `model-change`, `subagent-start`, `turn-end` or `record`), `agentKey`, `model`, `tool` (`name`, `callKey`, `status`, `bytes`, `durationMs`), `text`, `args`, `cut`, `tokens` and `record`. Only a `reply` has `text` (masked, at most 4 KiB) and only a `tool-call` has `args` (masked, at most 512 bytes); a `tool-result` is metadata only, and thinking, prompts, attachments and environment records are never read into an event (they are counted in `otherRecords`). The masker is best effort, so a page that shows this text says so. Every agent also gets `record` events, DoFlow's own records of it (`worker-run-start`, `worker-run-end`, `attempt-claim`, `attempt-end`, `hook-record`, each with `task` and `result` when the record names them), whatever the transcript's status: for OpenCode (`no-reader`), Antigravity (`not-linked`) and a session with no file (`not-found`) the body is exit 0 with `reader.status` `events-only` (the reader's own status for `not-found`) and only those events. The cursor is `<inode>:<byteOffset>:<recordSeq>:<recordAtMs>`; a changed inode or a shorter file resets it to the last 64 records and sets `reset`, and with a cursor only the record events newer than `recordAtMs` come back (a Kiro record event newer than its newest turn can repeat, because that field is Kiro's turn clock). `--limit` defaults to 200 and is capped at 500; at most 256 KiB of the file are read per call; `format-unrecognized` (more than 20 percent of the window's records lack required keys) is a `warnings` entry, not an error. No path, raw session id, cwd, email or tool-result text is printed. Read-only: no lock, no directory created, no upkeep (`transcript` is in `NO_UPKEEP`). `--json` prints the body, the text form one line per event; a malformed `--agent`, `--cursor` or `--limit`, another action or any flag the action does not take exits 2 |
| `doflow transcript --action stats [--window <minutes>] [--json]` | Traffic over the last window (default 10, 1 to 60 minutes) and who works with whom, for the project of the working directory (093 IC-023). The agents are the `fleet --action tree` nodes, newest first, at most 32 (the rest are counted in `notChecked`); each linked transcript is read in metadata mode (no text, no arguments), at most its last 256 KiB. Each agent has `eventsPerMin`, `toolCalls`, `toolErrors`, `toolDurationMs` (`p50`, `max`, `n`), `tokens`, `model` and `lastRecordAt`. A value the harness does not record is `null`, never 0 (Codex records no tool status, so `toolErrors` is `null`; Kiro records no tool duration); an agent with no readable transcript is `null` throughout, and 0 means a transcript that was read and held nothing in the window. Kiro keeps no time per record, so its events count through the newest turn `end_timestamp`. `models` groups the agents by model id. `pairs` is `{a, b, basis, task, tasks}`: `same-model` (equal model id), `same-task` (agents holding an active attempt or a running worker run on one task id, in `task`) and `overlap` (a plan `overlaps` pair, each task held by one of the two agents, in `tasks`, ordered as `a` then `b`). `--json` prints `{v: 1, projectKey, windowMin, agents, models, pairs, notChecked}`; the text form prints keys, models and counts. Read-only like `read` (no lock, no directory, no upkeep); a `--window` outside 1 to 60, another action or a flag this action does not take exits 2 |
| `doflow events --follow --channels <channel>[,<channel>...] [--json]` | Follow the live output of a project's read verbs, run with the working directory in that project; this is the Console's live source (149 IC-001), and the same command in a terminal prints the same lines. stdout carries only NDJSON envelopes, one per line, each `{v: 1, ch, id, type, at, data}`: `ch` is the channel, `id` is `<epoch>-<n>` (8 lowercase hex minted once per process; `n` starts at 1 per channel and rises by one per frame on it) on every channel but a log channel, `type` is `snapshot`, `patch`, `append`, `reset`, `error` or `gone` (`append` only on a log channel, `patch` never on one) and `at` is ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds. `--json` is accepted, and the output is NDJSON either way. A channel's first frame is a `snapshot` of all its sections; after that a `patch` carries only the sections that changed, plus `removed` for sections that no longer exist. Each section is `{exit, json}`: the exit code and `--json` output of one read verb, computed in-process by the function that verb's handler calls, so a verb's documented failure is a section, not an error. The channels: `now` follows `list` (`orchestrate --action list`), `observed` (`primary --action observed`), `slots` (`slot --action peek`) and `roadmap` (`roadmap --action status`); `run:<slug>:<taskId>` follows `status` and `timeline` (`orchestrate --action status` and `--action timeline` for that task and slug); `tasks:<slug>` follows `attempts` (`task-attempt --action inspect --slug <slug>`), `runs` (`worker-runs --slug <slug>`) and one `worker:<task>:<token>` section (`worker --action status`) per active attempt, at most 16; `worker:<runId>` (`w-` and 12 lowercase hex) follows `status` (`worker --action status` for that run) and `result` (`worker-result --worker-run <runId>`). A section is recomputed when a watched directory changes or a 1 s pass sees a source change; sections derived from the clock or a process are recomputed every 5 s and everything every 30 s. The log channels `worker:<runId>:out` and `worker:<runId>:err` (150 IC-006 to IC-008) follow the run's masked output file, `.doflow/state/worker-output/<runId>.out.log` or `.err.log` under the project, read as `worker-log` reads it: no other file name is formed and `<runId>.last.log` is never opened. Their id is the byte cursor `<ino>:<offset>:<n>:<ms>`: the file's decimal inode (`0` while there is none), the frame's `to` (the last `to` for `reset`, `error` and `gone`), the frame counter and the clock at emit. Their first frame is a `snapshot` `{log, ino, from, to, pieces, phase, end}`, where `log` is `waiting` (no file yet, the run not ended; the wait has no limit), `open` (the newest page of pieces `{at, text, cut?}`) or `none` (the run ended without output), `phase` is the run's phase (null while no store holds the run) and `end` is null until the run ends, then `{capped}`, true when its output was truncated (null when the record has no pointer for the stream). After it an `append` `{from, to, pieces, phase, end}` carries the bytes past the last `to` in the same file, or only a change of `phase` or `end` when it has no pieces. Only whole lines are sent until the run has ended with the stream's output pointer; then its last line is sent without its newline, before `end` is. A log file is checked on a watch event, by the 1 s pass, and every 250 ms while the run has not ended; one check sends at most two `append` frames and checks again 25 ms later for the rest. A second snapshot follows when the file appears. `reset` `replaced` follows when the file's inode changes or it is shorter than the last `to`, then a `snapshot`. `gone` follows when the file seen on the channel is absent for 2 s while the run is not ended, or absent once the run has ended with a pointer or after it was seen. The kinds `machine`, `layers`, `file` and `transcript` (161) are in the row after the `--kinds` row. Every other channel form is refused as a usage error before any file is opened: `followups` and `advice` (refused by name), `fleet:...` and any other unknown kind, and any other worker stream, `worker:<runId>:last` among them; so are a malformed `machine`, `layers`, `file` or `transcript` channel, a channel named twice, more than 64 channels (log channels counted) and, among `layers`, `file` and `transcript` channels, more than 8 distinct projects (exit 2). The channels get their snapshots in the order given and are followed until SIGINT or SIGTERM. Masking, the line cap, error frames, exit codes and the read-only rules are in the next row |
| `doflow events --follow --control stdin [--json]` | Start with no channel and follow the channels that control lines on stdin name; this is the form the Console runs. Each line is one JSON object of at most 1024 bytes with exactly the keys `op` (`sub` or `unsub`) and `ch` (a channel as in the row above). `sub` follows the channel and prints its `snapshot`; `sub` of a channel already followed prints a fresh `snapshot`; `unsub` stops following and prints nothing. A `sub` that would be the 65th followed channel gets an `error` frame `too-many-channels` and is not followed. A `sub` of a channel the control form refuses is the same invalid line as a bad `--channels` entry. An invalid line (not JSON, other keys, another `op`, a malformed or refused channel, more than 1024 bytes) prints one `doflow events:` line on stderr and exits 2, after whatever envelopes were already printed; stdin EOF exits 0 once the earlier lines have taken effect. Both forms: every string value inside a section's `json` is masked with the core's masker before printing (object keys are not), and a section counts as changed only when its masked text changed. No line is longer than 262144 bytes, newline included: a `snapshot` or `patch` over the cap is split by whole sections, every frame but the last with `more: true`, and a section too large for a line of its own is not sent; the channel gets `reset` with `reason` `frame-cap` instead. `reset` `replaced` means the channel's record was rewritten with a lower revision, or, on a log channel, that its file was replaced or shortened; the next frame after any `reset` is a `snapshot`. An `error` frame carries `code` `unsupported-version`, `store-unreadable`, `producer-failed`, `too-many-channels` or `too-many-projects` (a `sub` that would follow a 9th distinct project across `layers`, `file` and `transcript` channels; the channel is not followed and an `unsub` of another frees the slot); the channel stays followed and its next `snapshot` ends the error. `gone` means the channel's record (the run's journal, the feature's attempt ledger, the worker run) is no longer there, or a log channel's file was pruned, and the channel is no longer followed; `now` is never `gone`. On a log channel a run store whose `version` is not 1 is `unsupported-version` for that run's channels only, and every piece text is masked again before printing; an `append` over the line cap is split at piece boundaries. Exit 0 after SIGINT, SIGTERM or stdin EOF, with every watcher closed; 2 for a usage error (an unknown or repeated flag, a positional argument, no `--follow`, both or neither of `--channels` and `--control`, a `--control` other than `stdin`, a bad channel), printed as one `doflow events:` line on stderr with nothing on stdout; 1 for an internal fault, printed as one stderr line containing `[ERROR]`, after which nothing more is printed. Read-only: files are opened read-only, nothing is created, written, renamed or deleted, no lock is taken, no telemetry is written and no upkeep runs (`events` is in `NO_UPKEEP`); the only processes started are the git reads the section verbs already run, and the git reads that find the project root once when a log channel is followed; nothing is started per `append`. From a terminal, run `doflow events` directly rather than through `doflow-run`: on its projected-runtime path the dispatcher holds a verb's stderr until the verb exits, so a long-lived verb's stderr lines would arrive only at the end |
| `doflow events --kinds [--json]` | Print the channel kinds this core follows and follow nothing: `{"v":1,"kinds":[...]}` with `--json`, else one kind per line, exit 0. The kinds are `now`, `run`, `tasks`, `worker`, `machine`, `layers`, `file` and `transcript`. The Console reads it once per core child to decide whether it may `sub` a `machine`, `layers`, `file` or `transcript` channel; a core that does not know the flag exits 2 and lists none of them. It takes no `--follow`, `--channels` or `--control` (a usage error, exit 2), opens no watch, takes no lock and writes nothing. |
| `doflow events` channels `machine`, `layers:<projectKey>`, `file:<projectKey>:<fileKey>` and `transcript:<projectKey>:<agentKey>` (161) | Four more channel kinds of the two `events --follow` forms; a key is 16 lowercase hex, a project key is the one `fleet --action projects` lists. A core lists the kinds it follows with `events --kinds`. **`machine`** (frame ids `<epoch>-<n>`) has two sections: `projects`, the `fleet --action projects` body without `root`, and `activity`, for the first 16 projects of `projects`: open and awaiting orchestration runs, the newest 8 awaiting gates per project (`{runKey, slug, taskId, gateId, name, state, at, by}`; `name` falls back to the gate id), open worker runs (`workers: {open}`, which leaves out started sessions), the started sessions (`started`: every open one, then the newest ended ones, at most 8 per project, newest `createdAt` first, each `{runId, harness, model, phase, startedBy, worktreeKey, createdAt, end}` with `end` `{state, at}` once ended and null while open, and `startedCount`, the number of started sessions the store holds, open and ended), observed sessions and their harnesses, and the stores that failed to read, with `limits.watched`. `activity` is recomputed at most once per 1000 ms (leading and trailing), because hook sessions write constantly, so a change reaches a follower at once only for the first one in a second. **`layers:<pk>`** (ids `<epoch>-<n>`) is one project, every checkout of it, in ten sections: `checkouts`, `tree` (the `fleet --action tree` body, at most one build per 1000 ms), `runs` (the `orchestrate --action list` rows with `runKey`), `gates` (at most 8 runs by 8 gate events, with `by` `autopilot`, `manual` or null for a record written before 161), `artifacts` (the 64 newest briefs, reports, envelopes, prompts, worker logs and started sessions' first texts (kind `session-prompt`, `.doflow/state/session-prompts/<runId>.prompt.txt`, read masked through a `file:` lane), each with a `fileKey`, never a path), `reviews` (the 16 newest features; a `findings.json` over 1 MiB is `unreadable` with reason `too-large`), `sessions` (at most 16, with their audit and denials; a log that exists but cannot be read is not observed, reason `unreadable`), `traffic` (the `transcript --action stats` body, at most one build per 5000 ms) and `coverage` (per registry harness, what is observed and why not; agent questions and permission prompts are not observed and say so) and `channels` (row 170: the control channel of each started session, at most 16, the open ones first and then the newest ended, each `{runId, agentKey, harness, channel, reason, words, turn, sent: {count, lastAt}, recent}`; `channel` is `open`, `closed` or `none`, `reason` the close reason (a session that has ended always reads `closed`, whatever its record says, with the record's close reason, else its end state, else `run-ended`), `opening` or, for `none`, `no-channel` or `started-without-channel` with the descriptor's sentence in `words`; `turn` is `{observed: true, state: "working" \| "waiting", since, seq}` or `{observed: false, reason, since}`; `recent` is the newest 8 ledger entries `{seq, kind, by, at, bytes, outcome, answer}`, never a message's text; it is recomputed when the project's `worker-runs` directory changes and on the 5 s derived pass, with no throttle, and reads the supervisor of an open session at most once per run per pass, at most 4 runs per call; a store it cannot read is the failed section `cannot-read-sessions`). Its joins use exact keys only: a record that names no key is not placed, never guessed.
