A project the child's index does not know after one refresh is `gone`. **`file:<pk>:<fk>`** follows one indexed artifact and **`transcript:<pk>:<ak>`** one agent's transcript; both are log-shaped: their id is `<ino>:<offset>:<n>:<ms>`, `snapshot`, `append`, `reset` `replaced` and `gone` as on a worker log channel. A `file` is found only through the project's artifacts index (a key it does not list, or a file that left the listing, is `gone`; one pushed off the 64 newest stays followed for at most about 25 s) and is read masked to its last newline while a worker log is active, to its last byte otherwise; a line sent unfinished is sent again whole, as a `snapshot`, when it grows, so it is masked as one line (lines under 32 KiB; a longer line is cut every 32 KiB, and a piece at a cut is masked with 4 KiB of its line on each side, so a secret within 4 KiB of the cut on both sides is masked whole). Every file a lane reads by name is read only as the regular file a check at that read finds below its root, with no symlink on the way; anything else reads as absent. A `transcript` frame carries the `transcript --action read` body (`snapshot`) or `{events, cursor, eof, masked, warnings}` (`append`), at most 200 events per read, text masked then cut; its record events follow the run, attempt, dispatch and session records, each sent once, at most about 2 s after a write; an agent whose session belongs to another project gets one `snapshot` with `transcript.linked` false and no events. None of the four kinds takes a lock, writes a file or sends a path; every string leaf is masked before printing. |
| `doflow worker-log --worker-run <runId> --stream out\|err [--slug <slug>] [--cursor <c> \| --before <c>] [--json]` | One masked page of a worker run's `out` or `err` log, for the Console Session screen (150 IC-002), run with the working directory in the project. `--slug` names the run store `worker-runs/<slug>.json` under the lifecycle project root; without it the verb uses the store that holds the run. The file is `worker-output/<runId>.out.log` or `.err.log` beside that store, formed from the run id and stream alone: no path is read from the run record and the `.last.log` file is never opened (`--stream last` is a usage error). No cursor flag reads the tail; `--cursor` reads forward from its offset and `--before` backward to it; a cursor is `<ino>:<offset>:<seq>:<ms>` and only `ino` and `offset` are used. A page covers at most 131072 file bytes and its pieces at most 204800 JSON bytes, so the body fits one 262144-byte line; a running run's partial last line waits for its newline, and a line longer than 32768 bytes is cut into pieces marked `cut`. `--json` prints one compact JSON line `{v: 1, runId, stream, phase, log, ino, size, from, to, pieces, eof, reset, capped, cursor, before}`: `log` is `open` (the file is there), `waiting` (absent, the run has not ended), `gone` (absent, ended, the record names the output, so retention pruned it) or `none` (absent, ended, the run never wrote output); each piece is `{at, text}` with `cut: true` when it ends inside a line; `reset` is true when the cursor's inode differs or its offset is past the end, and the page is then the tail; `capped` is null unless the run ended with the output recorded, then whether the sink capped it; `cursor` and `before` are the next forward and backward cursors (null without a file, `before` also null at offset 0). Piece text is masked again with the core's masker (a piece that starts or ends at a cut is masked with 4 KiB of its line on each side, so a secret crossing the cut is masked whole) and every string in the body with the envelope masker. Without `--json` it prints each piece as one line and one summary line `<runId> <stream> <log> bytes <from> to <to> of <size>` on stderr. Exit 0 with the body; 1 `{v: 1, ok: false, error: "unknown-run"}` (no store holds the run) or `log-unreadable`; 2 `usage` (a missing, repeated or unknown flag, a positional argument, a run id other than `w-` and 12 lowercase hex, a stream other than `out` or `err`, both `--cursor` and `--before`, a malformed cursor, an unsafe slug) or `store-unreadable` (a torn store, or one whose `version` is not 1); a failure also prints one `doflow worker-log:` line on stderr. Read-only: the file is opened read-only, nothing is created, written, renamed or deleted, no lock is taken, no telemetry is written, no process is probed and no upkeep runs (`worker-log` is in `NO_UPKEEP`) |
| `doflow worker-result --worker-run <runId> [--slug <slug>] [--json]` | The result fields of one worker run (150 IC-003), with the store found as `worker-log` finds it. The body is one compact JSON line, with or without `--json`, holding exactly `{v: 1, runId, task, token, harness, phase, endState, endedAt, reportStatus, readOnly, isolation, resultOffered, withheld, quota}`: `endState` (`exited`, `timed-out` or `cancelled`) and `endedAt` only once `phase` is `ended`, else null; `reportStatus` the report's `success`, `failure`, `denied` or `unparsed`, null before a report; `readOnly` the read-only check's `clean`, `violated` or `unverifiable` for a run without `--edit`, else null; `isolation` `{status, reason}` for an edit run, else null; `resultOffered` and `withheld` as the supervisor decides them, in every phase; `quota` the report's `{signal, basis}`, else null. The body is built field by field, never copied from the record, so the report's summary, reason, denials and usage, the check's change lists and heads, the output pointers, the prompt, identities, worktrees, process ids and the rest of the record never appear; every string is masked. Exit 0; 1 `unknown-run`; 2 `usage` (as `worker-log`) or `store-unreadable`, each failure with one `doflow worker-result:` line on stderr. Read-only as `worker-log`: no process is probed and no upkeep runs (`worker-result` is in `NO_UPKEEP`) |
| `doflow worker-runs [--slug <slug>] [--json]` | The worker runs of one feature (150 IC-004): `--slug`, else the feature of the current branch as the `worker` verb resolves it (none is a usage error). Every run in the store is listed whatever its phase and whether its attempt is still active, child runs (`--child-of`) included. The body is one compact JSON line, with or without `--json`: `{v: 1, slug, store, runs, limits: {shown, notShown}}`, with `store` `present` or `absent` (then `runs` is empty) and each run `{runId, task, token, harness, childOf, phase, endState, createdAt, startedAt, endedAt, edit}` (`endState` and `endedAt` null until the run ends, `edit` true for an isolated-edit run). At most the newest 100 runs by `createdAt` (ties by run id, a run without `createdAt` counts as oldest) are listed, oldest first; `limits.notShown` counts the rest. Every string is masked. Exit 0; 2 `usage` (an unknown or repeated flag, a positional argument, an unsafe or unresolvable slug) or `store-unreadable`, each with one `doflow worker-runs:` line on stderr. Read-only as `worker-log`: no process is probed and no upkeep runs (`worker-runs` is in `NO_UPKEEP`) |
| `doflow advice-picks [--kind <id>] [--json]` | The advice picks of the last 90 days, for the Console management screens (151 IC-001), run with the working directory in the project. The record roots are resolved as `advise` resolves them, and the partitions are read with the report's 90-day window. `--kind` narrows `kinds`, `days[].picks` and `picks` to one advice kind a caller asks (a kind of `core/registry/advice.json` with a non-empty `askedBy`) or `adhoc`; without it every kind is in. The body is one compact JSON line, with or without `--json`: `{v: 1, window: {from, to, maxAgeDays}, kind, kinds, stores, days, picks, limits: {shown, notShown, skipped}}`. `window.from` is the UTC date 90 days ago and `to` today; `days` holds every date between them inclusive, 91 entries, oldest first, each `{day, coverage, unreadableLines, picks}` with `coverage` `read` (every partition of that date was read and no store is unreadable) or `unknown`, and `picks` `{<kind>: n}` for kinds with picks that day, so a `read` day with no entry for a kind means 0 and an `unknown` day means not known. `kinds` is the kinds a caller asks, then every other kind a record names, at most 32. `stores` gives `project-state` and `user-state` as `read`, `absent` or `unreadable`. `picks` is the newest 50 picks by `at` (ties by `pickId` descending), each `{pickId, kind, at, mode, pick, static, agree, outcome}`: `mode` `shadow`, `act` or `unrecorded`; `pick`, `static` and the outcome's `actual` shown only when they match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,63}$` after masking, else `(withheld)`; `outcome` the latest outcome record for the pick as `{actual, at, agree}`, else null. `limits.notShown` counts the window's picks past the 50 and `limits.skipped` the records with no usable type, kind or time and those of kinds past the 32. The body is built field by field, so a record's question, options, instructions, probabilities, provider, model, task id and every other field never appear; every string is masked. Exit 0 with the body; 2 `{v: 1, ok: false, error: "usage"}` (an unknown, repeated or misplaced flag, a positional argument, a `--kind` that is not a kind a caller asks or `adhoc`) or `registry-invalid` (the advice or storage registry cannot be loaded), each with one `doflow advice-picks:` line on stderr. Read-only: partition files are only read, nothing is created, written, renamed or deleted, no lock is taken, no network connection or model call is made, no telemetry is written and no upkeep runs (`advice-picks` is in `NO_UPKEEP`) |
| `doflow worker-matrix [--json]` | Every harness's worker and orchestration cells, for the Console management screens (151 IC-003), read from the `core/registry/harnesses.json` the DoFlow install ships, never a project file. The body is one compact JSON line, with or without `--json`: `{v: 1, registryVersion, groups: {worker, orchestration}, harnesses, counts, limits: {shown, notShown, cellsNotShown, cut}}`. `groups` lists, per group, the cell names that carry a string `status` across the harnesses in registry order, at most 32 per group (the rest counted in `limits.cellsNotShown`). Each harness is `{id, billing, profile, cells: {worker, orchestration}}` and each cell `{status, declared, evidence, verification, verifiedOn, reason, fallback, cut}`: `status` `supported`, `unknown` or `unsupported` (any other recorded value is `unknown` with `reason` `unrecognised-status`); a group name the harness does not declare is `unknown` with `declared: false` and `reason` `not-declared`; `evidence` at most 8 entries of at most 1024 bytes each; `verification` at most 4096 bytes and `reason` at most 1024 bytes; every text is masked whole and only then cut at a UTF-8 character start, and `cut` is true when any text of the cell was cut or evidence entries were dropped. `counts` gives, per group, the shown cells by status. Harnesses are added in registry order while the body stays at or under 1048576 bytes; `limits.notShown` counts the rest and `limits.cut` the cells with `cut: true`. The registry's `binary`, `versionArgv`, `argv`, `promptVia`, `parser`, `quotaSignals`, `protectedPaths`, `modelChecks`, `retired` and every other field never appear. Exit 0 with the body; 2 `{v: 1, ok: false, error: "usage"}` (any flag but `--json`, or a positional argument) or `registry-invalid` (the registry cannot be loaded), each with one `doflow worker-matrix:` line on stderr. Read-only: no harness binary is run, no probe record or binding is read, no network connection is opened, nothing is written and no upkeep runs (`worker-matrix` is in `NO_UPKEEP`) |
| `doflow sessions-history [--days <n>] [--limit <n>] [--project-root <path>] [--json]` | Completed workflow sessions within retention window (152 IC-001), aggregated across sessions.log, orchestration journals and worker runs. `--days` defines the window (1 to 90, default 30); `--limit` caps the returned sessions (1 to 500, default 100). The body is one compact JSON line with or without `--json`: `{v: 1, window: {from, to, maxAgeDays}, sessions: [...], limits: {shown, notShown, pruned}}`. Each session item provides slug, taskId, taskClass, workflowName, harness, state, startedAt, endedAt, durationMs, stages, workers, cost and project. Cost reports input, output and total tokens, with unrecorded telemetry as unknown, never 0. Output is capped at 4 MiB. Read-only: no lock, no write, no worker run and no upkeep (`sessions-history` is in `NO_UPKEEP`). Exits 0 with the body; 2 usage on an invalid flag or parameter |
| `doflow worktree --action cleanup --slug <slug> [--json]` | Remove one merged feature's worktree and branch with the roadmap loop's own non-force cleanup, from the main checkout (autonomy plan 7c). It takes the roadmap lease (`lease-held` when another call holds it, exit 1), needs a develop worktree (`no-develop-worktree`), a seed row for the slug (`row-unknown`) and the loop's own merge evidence, a `DoFlow-Row` trailer merge on develop (`not-merged` otherwise, so a freshly claimed row whose tip merely sits inside develop is never removed), refuses to run from inside the target worktree (`cwd-inside-worktree`), copies the worktree's gitignored `agent-docs/doflow/<slug>` into the main checkout without overwriting, then runs `git worktree remove` and `git branch -d` (never `--force` or `-D`, G53). Exit 0 `cleaned` (also when the branch and worktree are already gone); exit 1 `kept` with a `reason`: `not-merged`, `cwd-inside-worktree`, `head-moved`, `not-on-develop` (the branch tip is not merged into develop), `wrong-branch`, `dirty`, `copy-failed`, `copy-conflict` (a main-checkout file differs), `worktree-remains`, or `git-failed`; the copy counts and any `failure` text are in `artifacts`. Exit 2 for another action, a missing or malformed `--slug` or a flag this action does not take. Writes no upkeep |
| `doflow attendance --task-id <slug> [--run <id>] [--json]` | Read-only. Prints `unattended <source>` or `attended <reason>` and exits 0 (2 for a usage fault). `unattended worker-role` when `DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE` is `worker` (case-insensitive, literal; any other value does not count); `unattended run-record <run>` when the run record `.doflow/state/roadmap/runs/<run>.json` (`--run`, else the newest open one) has no `closedAt`, its slot for the seed row with this slug holds a pending `perform` or the `postMerge` marker the loop sets when it issues the post-merge verification `perform` (the loop clears the marker on its next merge step), the slot is neither `held` nor `paused` nor done, the row is not on `hold` and the autopilot kill switch is on. A record the loop would call unreadable (it also needs integer `steps` and `n` and a `log` array) is `run-unreadable`. Everything else is `attended` with a reason: `no-run`, `run-closed`, `run-unreadable`, `seed-unreadable`, `row-unknown`, `row-held`, `slot-held` or `slot-paused` (the run record marks the row's slot held or paused), `slot-done` (the row is finished, whatever marker an interrupted step left), `no-pending-perform`, `switch-off`. Hold the row before working it by hand, so your own session reads as attended. The roadmap loop's `perform` envelopes (the stage envelope and the post-merge verification one) carry `attendance: {mode: "unattended", run}`. No lock, no write, no upkeep |
| `doflow worktree --action cleanup --slug <slug> [--json]` | Remove one merged feature's worktree and branch, never by force: it copies the worktree's gitignored `agent-docs/doflow/<slug>` back to the main checkout first, then runs the loop's non-force cleanup. Exit 0 `cleaned`; exit 1 when the feature is kept or denied, with the reason printed: whatever keeps it in the non-force cleanup, `lease-held` (the roadmap lease is held), `not-merged` (no `DoFlow-Row` trailer merge on develop; a tip merely contained in develop is not enough) or `cwd-inside-worktree`; exit 2 usage. There is no `--force` |
| `doflow goal --action add\|item\|check\|link\|list\|done --goal <id> [--json]` | Keep a goal in the project's own store, the same event folder as `followup`: DoFlow's own record of one outcome and its checklist, whatever a harness's own `goal` command does. The id is kebab-case, at most 40 characters. `add --statement <one line> --item <text> [--item <text>]…` needs at least one item (ids `C1`, `C2`, …) and is refused `goal-exists` when the id is taken; `item --text <text>` appends the next id. `check --item C3 --evidence <one line>` records the item as met, and `--unmet` records it as not met; evidence is required either way. `link --slug <slug>` makes a tracked feature serve the goal (`untracked-feature` for an untracked slug, `goal-already-linked` when it serves another goal unless `--replace`); a feature serves one goal at most, and a feature that serves none is unaffected. `list [--goal <id>]` shows each goal's items, `progress` (met over total), the linked features by status, `proposeDone` (every item met and the goal open) and a nudge per unchecked item while a linked feature is finished. `done [--reason <line>] --channel question\|gate\|prompt` is for the user only: `default` is refused (`not-user`), and a goal with unmet items is refused (`items-unmet`) unless `--reason` says why it is closed anyway. `--channel` records whether the user or the agent acted. Text is masked for secrets, one line and bounded. Reads git only to derive feature statuses; no network; nothing is committed or pushed. Exits 1 on a refusal (`goal-exists`, `unknown-goal`, `unknown-item`, `goal-already-linked`, `untracked-feature`, `not-user`, `items-unmet`, `already-done`, `store-locked`, `store-migration-failed`, `store-migration-pending`), 2 on a usage error or `-g` |
| `doflow failure --action list\|settle\|capture [--all] [--fp <16 hex>] [--as noise\|fixed\|imported] [--reason "<line>"] [--set on\|off] [--json]` | The failures DoFlow captured about itself on this machine, in one machine-wide folder `<config home>/failures/`, shared by every project and never sent anywhere. A line is written when a DoFlow command ends in an internal error (a programming error, a system error with a code, an escaped throw), the dispatcher crashes, or a guard hook aborts; findings, refusals, bad flags, hook denies and environment faults are not recorded, and the message is stored only in its normalised, masked form. Capture never changes a command's output or exit status and is skipped when there is no [config home](#config-home). `list` folds the lines into entries by fingerprint and shows `new` and `regressed` ones (`--all` for every status), with counts; `settle --fp <fp> --as noise\|fixed` needs `--reason` and `--as imported` creates a follow-up in the DoFlow repository only (`not-doflow-repo` elsewhere); a `fixed` entry seen again is `regressed`. `capture` reports the switch, and `--set off\|on` writes `failure.capture` to `settings.json`; `DOFLOW_FAILURE_CAPTURE=off` also turns capture off but cannot turn it on over the file. Works whatever the working directory and ignores `-g`. Exits 1 on `unknown-fp`, `already-imported`, `not-doflow-repo`, `write-failed` (`capture --set` could not write `settings.json`) and, for `--as imported`, `store-migration-failed`, `store-migration-pending` and `store-locked`, 2 on a usage error |
| `doflow leak-scan --path <file>… [--exclude <segment>…] [--json]` | Report DoFlow's own process vocabulary — `FR-###`/`NFR-###`, `US#`, `agent-docs/`, `.doflow/state/`, chain artifact names — appearing in files that ship to people who never used DoFlow. `--path` is repeatable. `--exclude` extends the built-in `agent-docs/` exclusion with extra path segments (an implementing repository's own `bin/`/`src/`/`core/`, say) and accepts either a repeated flag or a comma-joined value in either spelling (`--exclude a --exclude b`, `--exclude a,b`, `--exclude=a,b`). Occurrences under an excluded segment are correct usage and are excluded before matching. Every path given is accounted for as scanned or unscanned-with-a-reason; an unreadable path is reported, never fatal. Exits 1 on findings, 0 when clean. Two callers share it so they cannot drift: the Claude Stop hook scans the turn's edited files, and `/do-code-review` scans the reviewed set |

`retrieve` takes `--scope guidance` (the default) or `--scope code`; any other value exits 2 and names both. For
`--scope code` the search root is `--root`, or the current directory when it is omitted, while task state
(`--task-id`, `--slug`) stays in the task state root, so a linked worktree searches its own files and records
into the main checkout's store. When both providers answer, conceptual results are fused per file: each result is one file, and each of the
two providers (lexical and Semble) votes for its first requested-top distinct files, and the delivered span is the
widest of the spans each provider ranked first for that file (ties: better provider rank, smaller line, then
endLine, then provider id). Exact queries keep one result per span. `origins[].locator` names the
span each contributing provider returned for a file-grain result. Response `gaps[]` entries carry a `source`; two
sources describe coverage rather than provider faults: `admission` (one entry per skip cause with a `count`, for example a
skipped oversized file, the file-count limit, or an unstaged delete or rename; `coverage.complete` is then false) and `live-validation-origin` (an origin locator dropped by live validation while the
delivered span stands). The semantic index lock records its owner's pid and host; a lock whose owner pid is dead on
this host is reclaimed automatically, and any other lock (another host, no readable record, a live owner) once it is older than 10 minutes. In the
`hybrid-c-v1` benchmark result files, each configuration C class row carries `servedBy` (`coordinator`,
`delegated-A` or `delegated-B`) and, on coordinator-served classes, `rawValidity.semanticReached` (the count of answers whose
semantic leg answered); each absolute gate row (not the relative C versus B or A rows) carries `excluded`, the C class rows it covered but did not count,
with a reason.

`readiness`, `evidence`, `claim`, `context-pack`, `retrieval-plan` and `outcome` read per-project
state under the invoking repo's `.doflow/state/` (from a linked worktree, the main checkout's; see
[Where a task's records live](#where-a-tasks-records-live)); run them from the project the task belongs to,
or pass `-g` for the global scope. Those verbs and `research-request` accept `--slug`; whenever a
feature slug resolves (`--slug`, else the feature of the checkout the verb runs in: a task id that names
a feature folder there, else the branch's), with or without a decision register and wherever the
feature folder is, their records live in a `<slug>/` subdirectory of each store, as
`.doflow/state/evidence/<slug>/<task>.json` (claims are `evidence/<slug>/<task>_claims.json`), so the
same task id in two features never collides. A task id equal to the slug, and a call for which no slug
resolves (for example on `main`), keep the flat `.doflow/state/<store>/<task>.json` path. A flat record
written before this rule (by a feature without a register) is still read: when the namespaced file does
not exist, a flat file of the same task that names this feature (`featureSlug` on a run, `slug` on a
readiness record) or names none is read in its place, and the first write goes to the namespace,
starting from it. The two scopes are not merged: a list reads flat records only while the namespaced file does not exist, and then only a flat record that parses (an unparseable or oversized one is never read) and names this feature, or names none for a feature with no decision register; a task id equal to the slug has only the flat record; after the first namespaced write it reads the namespaced record alone. A call that resolves no slug reads the flat record alone, so the same task id used once with a slug and once without, from the main checkout on `develop` for one, has records in two places and a list shows one of them (empty, when the other holds them all). Pass `--slug <name>` to read the feature's; leave it out to read the flat ones. A `--slug` that names no feature folder prints one stderr note,
`--slug '<slug>' names no feature folder; its records are kept under that slug`. Every verb that takes `--slug`
(these, plus `verify`, `scaffold`, `decision`, `orchestrate`, `validate`, `prereqs`, `render-audit`,
`task-brief` and `parallel-check`) refuses an unsafe one with exit 2: `prereqs`, `parallel-check` and `task-brief` print `{"error":"invalid-slug",...}`;
the other verbs print it with `--json` and otherwise one `invalid-slug` message line on stderr. `capabilities` reports on the machine and is
scope-independent; `doctor` reports on both, so index freshness and command detection follow the
same project scope.

### Package manifest: forbidden fields

A package manifest, at its top level or inside a contribution entry, may not carry any of these fields; each is
refused `forbidden-field`. The list is the `forbiddenFields` array of `core/registry/contribution-points.json`,
and a guard asserts the two are equal.

```json
["enabled", "defaultEnabled", "trust", "trusted", "settings", "guards", "hooks", "floors", "invariants", "order", "position", "replaces", "override", "familyProven", "crossFamily", "firstParty", "scripts", "install", "postinstall"]
```

### Reuse of a recorded PASS

`verify` can answer a check from an earlier PASS instead of running it again. It ships **off**: turn it on by
writing `{"schema":1,"verify.reuse":"on"}` into `<config home>/settings.json` yourself (global-only; `config set` refuses it, as it refuses every security-relevant key; see [Settings keys](#settings-keys)), and `doflow config -g` then shows `verify.reuse = "on"`. The environment variable
`DOFLOW_VERIFY_REUSE` can only turn it off.

- **What is recorded and reused.** Every level stores a check's PASS (a FAIL, an INCONCLUSIVE or a timeout is never stored)
  in the `verification-reuse` store (see [Storage stores](#storage-stores)). Levels L1 and L2 look a stored PASS up; L3 (risk HIGH or
  CRITICAL) always executes its checks. A lookup refuses a record older than 24 hours, a record whose schema or payload
  digest does not match, and a record the key does not name. A stored output excerpt is bounded to 2,200 characters, so a
  reused check shows the same excerpt as the executed one; `truncated` and the log path are not kept for a reused check, so no log file exists for it. An executed non-PASS result for a key removes the stored PASS of that key while reuse is on, at every level.
- **The key.** A record answers a check only when its key is equal: the command, its timeout, the real working directory,
  `HEAD`, the digest of modified and untracked files, the sorted names of ignored entries, the bytes of the declared
  ignored inputs (`policy.reuse.ignoredInputs` in `core/registry/verification.json`), the identity (real path, size,
  modification time, inode) of `node` and of the command's first word on `PATH`, the Node version, platform, architecture
  and the environment digest. The digest of modified files also covers tracked files under `.doflow/` (except those under `.doflow/state/`), and the key covers the target of a tracked symlink that points outside the repository. A tree that cannot be keyed (git fault, over 500 modified files or 50 MB, an unreadable or
  linked declared input) runs unshared. A check is stored only when its inputs are the same after it ran as before.
- **How it shows.** A reused check is labelled `reused: {ref, recordedAt}` in the report, its telemetry entry says
  `shared: "reused"`, and the run record gains the `reused` count. The report counts gain `checksReused` when reuse is on; `checksRun` still counts every evaluated check, reused ones included. The text form prints `Checks: P/N passed (R reused, not executed)` when R is above 0 and a `reused <time> ref <ref>` line under the tier of each reused check.
- **Residual risk.** Reuse trusts the key, and the key does not see: network, time, external services or flaky tests; files outside the repository, such as `~/.npmrc`, the global git config or `<config home>` files; the contents of an ignored directory whose name is unchanged (`dist/`, `node_modules/`); ignored inputs other than those declared, which are read only at the git root, so a subpackage `.env` is not seen; untracked files under `.doflow/` or an in-repository config home, and any file under `.doflow/state/`, tracked or not; and the dependencies of a wrapper, such as `npm run`, `npx`, `env`, `exec`, `cd … &&` or `export PATH=…;`. A check is never reused when its command's first word or a leading `NAME=value` holds a quote, a backslash, `$` or a backtick, a leading assignment sets `PATH=`, or the first word starts with `~` or holds `*`, `?` or `[`; nor while any tracked symlink in the repository points at a directory, or at an unreadable or over-1 MB file, outside it (or the repository holds over 500 tracked links). A check that changes the files it reads is never stored; the check is compared on its state before and after the run only, so a check that changes its inputs and restores them during its own run, or leaves a process that does, is not detected. A process that cannot write the store does not reuse from it, because it could not remove a PASS after a failure. Reusing a producer check (a build) skips its side effects, so a later check that executes may fail spuriously. An executed failure removes the stored PASS only while reuse is on, so a PASS stored while reuse was on can be reused for up to 24 hours after reuse is switched on again, even if a FAIL ran in between with reuse off. Use reuse only for deterministic checks. The 24-hour limit and the off default bound the rest. The store trusts its own account: someone who can swap a directory above the store between a check and its lookup can already edit the source, so reuse does not defend against that.
  Turn reuse off with `"verify.reuse": "off"` in `<config home>/settings.json` or `DOFLOW_VERIFY_REUSE=off` when a run must execute everything.
- **Test selection is not wired in.** `verify` does not choose tests by impact. `bench impact-replay` (Runtime &
  Diagnostics Commands) only measures how such a selection would have done on past commits; its verdict enables nothing.

### Where a task's records live

`evidence`, `claim`, `readiness`, `context-pack`, `context-emit`, `research-request`, `outcome`,
`retrieval-plan`, `retrieve --scope code` (when it has a `--task-id` or `--slug`) and `orchestrate` read and write a
task's records in one root. Run from a linked worktree they always use the main checkout's
`.doflow/state` (the first entry of `git worktree list`), whatever folders the worktree holds, so one
task's records are never split across two stores and none is lost by `git worktree remove`. This also
holds for a project that tracks `agent-docs/`; before feature 115 a worktree was redirected only when it
lacked the feature folder. Delivery records (`delivery/`, written by `context-pack` and `context-emit`
alike) and the orchestration run follow the same root and the same namespace rule: the run of a task
id other than its feature's slug is `orchestration/<slug>/<task>.json`, the run of the feature itself
`orchestration/<slug>.json`. `render-audit` reads the run from the main checkout. A DoFlow sandbox
checkout (`.doflow-worktree-base` at its root) keeps its own store, and `-g` keeps the home store. Every
redirected call except `context-emit` prints one stderr note, `records are kept in the main checkout <root>`.

Source files stay in the checkout the verb runs in: `context-pack` and `context-emit` read the feature
folder, plan and pointers there, and a relative evidence `locator.file` is resolved and its freshness
hash taken there. Only a locator under `agent-docs/doflow/<slug>/` whose feature folder is absent from
that checkout and present in the main checkout is read from the main checkout; any other file missing
from the checkout is missing. Only the records move.

Records an earlier version left in the worktree's own `.doflow/state` are moved by one sweep on every
redirected call except `orchestrate --action list`, `status` and `timeline`, which only read, whatever task it names (or none). The stores swept are the ones
`core/registry/storage.json` lists with `src/runtime/state-root.ts` among their writers (evidence and
claims, delivery, readiness, research requests, outcome, retrieval plan, orchestration run), each with
its flat files and every `<slug>/` subdirectory, matched by file name exactly. Each file goes to the same
path in the main checkout, except that a flat record of a task id other than its feature's slug goes to
`<store>/<slug>/` (the slug it names, else the call's), where every reader looks. Under the target's lock
and then the source's, the source is read again (gone means another sweep moved it); equal bytes at the
target delete the worktree copy, and otherwise the bytes are written to a temporary file, renamed into
place, read back and compared before the worktree copy is deleted. Nothing is swept when the worktree's
`.doflow/state` is the main checkout's (a link to it), and a sweep where the worktree has no store
directory costs one `stat` per store. A file present in both checkouts with different bytes, a link on
either side's own path below the checkout, or a worktree file that is the main checkout's file itself
(the same device and inode) is left as it is and refuses the call with exit 2 and
`{"ok":false,"error":"store-conflict","adopted":[...],"removed":[...],"conflicts":[{"worktree":<file>,"main":<file>,"reason":"differs"|"link"|"same-file"}, ...]}`
(without `--json`, a stderr line, then one line per pair). Every pair is named, and the files the sweep
had already moved stay moved: keep one copy of each pair, remove the other and run the call again.

Readers that run from a linked worktree without the redirect (`verify`, and the edit hook) read the
main checkout's readiness record first; a copy at the same path in the worktree with other bytes
refuses, with the runtime's text `doflow gate readiness-before-implementation: task '<task>' has a
readiness record in this worktree (<file>) that differs from the main checkout's (<file>). Next: keep one
of the two and remove the other, then re-run. Nothing was changed.` (the edit hook records an advisory, prints this text and exits 0). A record or run
an earlier version left in another worktree is still read from there when it is the one copy of this
feature's.

`doflow-run paths --json` carries one more key, `feature_root`: the checkout that holds
`agent-docs/doflow/<slug>`, the current checkout when it has the folder, else the main checkout when the
folder is only there, else the current checkout (a new feature, or a slug that names no feature).
`repo_root` keeps meaning the current checkout, and every emitted artifact path stays relative to it.
`validate` reads default targets, `render-audit` writes `audit.md` and the `decision` verb resolves the
feature folder under `feature_root`. `validate` also checks each `plan.md` with the `task-graph` rule:
one finding per problem `plan-check` reports (for example a duplicate task id, an unknown dependency or a
cycle). It prints one `note:` line on stderr and adds no finding when the dispatcher, Node or `jq` is
unavailable.

`--task-id` and `--task-class` are **required**, not defaulted. Readiness, evidence, claims and a
context pack all belong to one named task under one named contract; substituting `default` or
`feature` for an argument the caller omitted produces a confident verdict about a task or a
contract nobody asked about, which is exactly the failure mode the runtime is being corrected for.
Every refusal names the valid set.

An evidence `--kind` is one of ten: `exact-search`, `semantic-retrieval`, `structural`,
`historical`, `documentation`, `test-result`, `runtime-observation`, `user-statement`, `diff`,
`generated-analysis`. An unknown one is refused with all ten named. The ledger mints the record's
`id` and measures its `freshness`, so neither is accepted from the caller, and `supports` /
`contradicts` belong to `claim --action link`, which updates both sides of the relationship rather
than leaving a claim unaware of the evidence pointing at it.

Every command in this table is also a verb on the runtime seam — `doflow-run <verb>` — which is how
skills reach them. The two spellings run the same implementation; the seam additionally records a
metadata line in the run ledger.

`indicators` reads a different file from the three below it: the per-task orchestration records at
`<config>/state/orchestration/<slug>.json`, which the workflow orchestrator writes as stages complete.
That is why it is a separate verb rather than a mode of `stats` — a run-ledger record names no task,
stage or class, so no extension of `stats` could group by any of them. It reports per-stage intervals,
gate waits charged to no stage, recorded stage outcomes and rerun counts, grouped by task class, and it
prints what it cannot measure: anything needing git, pull-request or CI data, and the fact that a stage
figure is an interval since the previous recorded event rather than pure execution time. It writes
nothing.

`trace`, `stats` and `discover` read the run ledger at `<config>/state/runs/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`,
which `doflow-run` appends to once per dispatched verb. They locate it the way the dispatcher does
— nearest `.doflow` walking up from the working directory, or the global one — so they work from a
subdirectory. The walk passes over a `.doflow` inside another `.doflow` (a dispatch worktree's) and
continues to the project's store; that worktree `.doflow` used to hide the project store because the
launch writes the worker's delivery pack into its own `.doflow/state/delivery`. Records are metadata
only: a verb, a capability, a provider, an exit code, a
duration, counts and byte volumes. No argument value, command output or file content is recorded.
An empty ledger is a normal state and is reported as "no conclusion can be drawn", never as a
clean bill of health.

`inventory` reads neither the run ledger nor the orchestration record: it reads the two *ownership*
ledgers, one per scope, and derives everything else at read time. Nothing it reports is persisted.
Its optional positional is the project root (default the working directory) and `-t` restricts it to
named harnesses; the global scope's root comes from the process home directory and is not an
argument. Its report and `doflow reconcile` deliberately disagree about how much is repairable —
`reconcile` converges a scope onto what `doflow.lock` pins and names a harness the ledger holds and
the lock lacks — so the report states that gap as repair coverage rather than hiding it by adopting
the narrower definition. The modules behind it live under `src/runtime/inventory`.

`scaffold` resolves which feature is active the same way every chain skill does — from the working
directory, branch-derived in a git repo — so run it from the project root. Where a non-git root
holds more than one feature directory it cannot choose: it exits 2 naming every candidate, and
`--slug <name>` re-runs against the one you pick.

**Dense/rerank retrieval slots.** `core/registry/models.json` accepts an optional top-level `slots`
array binding a retrieval stage (`dense` embedding lookup, `rerank` cross-encoder pass) to one
declared provider: entries are `{ "id": "dense" | "rerank", "provider": <models.json provider id>,
"model": "<concrete model>", "enabled": false }`. A slot changes nothing until it is both declared
and `"enabled": true` — until then (and whenever the bound backend does not answer a PATH probe)
retrieval stays lexical BM25 plus the import graph, which remains the correctness floor. There is
deliberately no HTTP client here: enabling a slot only routes lookups to the named provider through
the same advisory availability probe `model-role` uses; the provider implementation itself (local
vs API) is a separate decision and no caller invokes one today. A malformed slot entry fails registry
load loudly; a `slots` value that is not an array is currently read as no slots (FU-7bnh0m).

## Settings keys

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED:settings-keys -->
| Key | Type | Default | Scope | Env | Legacy file | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `advice.enabled` | boolean | `false` | narrow-only | — | — | Turns the local decision model on; a project may only turn it off. |
| `advice.provider` | enum | `ollama` | global-only | — | — | Decision-model provider id from advice.json. |
| `advice.endpoint` | url | `http://127.0.0.1:11434` | global-only | — | — | Decision-model endpoint; default is the provider's defaultEndpoint. |
| `advice.model` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name; a project may choose it while advice is on. |
| `advice.kinds.tier` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the tier kind: off, shadow \(record only\) or act; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.kinds.task-class` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the task-class kind: off, shadow \(record only\) or act; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.kinds.verify-risk` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the verify-risk kind: off, shadow \(record only\) or act; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.kinds.review-depth` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the review-depth kind: off, shadow \(record only\) or act; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.kinds.mcp-need` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the mcp-need kind: off, shadow \(record only\) or act; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.kinds.claim-support` | enum | — | project-overridable | — | — | Advice mode for the claim-support kind: off or shadow \(record only\); act is accepted but held at shadow with a warning; unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, the highest layer that sets it decides and an invalid value is off. |
| `advice.models.tier` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the tier kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `advice.models.task-class` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the task-class kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `advice.models.verify-risk` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the verify-risk kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `advice.models.review-depth` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the review-depth kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `advice.models.mcp-need` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the mcp-need kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `advice.models.claim-support` | string | — | project-overridable | — | — | Decision-model name for the claim-support kind; unset uses advice.model. |
| `storage.prune` | enum | `on` | project-overridable | — | — | Storage pruning; project value applies to project roots only. |
| `storage.retentionHours` | integer | `4320` | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_RETENTION_HOURS` | — | Lifecycle-events window in hours; an explicit value applies even while pruning is off. |
| `failure.capture` | enum | `on` | global-only | `DOFLOW_FAILURE_CAPTURE` | — | Capture of DoFlow's own failures on this machine; any layer saying off wins. |
| `autopilot.enabled` | enum | `off` | global-only | `DOFLOW_AUTOPILOT_ENABLED` | `autopilot/off` | Roadmap autopilot kill switch, off until the owner turns it on; the env and the autopilot/off sentinel can only turn it off. |
| `hooks.harnesses` | string-list | `["claude","codex","antigravity","pi"]` | global-only | — | — | Harness ids that get DoFlow's hooks, gates and bridge; every other harness gets content only \(guidance, skills, agents, MCP\), and its DoFlow hooks are removed on the next install or update. An invalid list keeps the default. |
| `verify.reuse` | enum | `off` | global-only | `DOFLOW_VERIFY_REUSE` | — | Reuse of a recorded PASS at L1/L2 when the check key is unchanged, off until the owner turns it on; the env can only turn it off. |
| `dispatch.fallback` | enum | `ask` | narrow-only | — | — | Quota fallback ladder default for dispatch runs: ask holds and proposes, off holds without a proposal; auto exists only per run via --fallback. |
| `workers.billing` | worker-billing | — | global-only | — | — | Person-written billing record per headless worker id: free or metered, with the date asserted. Lets dispatch --billing-cap free launch a worker the registry lists as unknown; global-only. |
| `review.preset` | enum | `review-single` | narrow-only | — | — | Review protocol preset: review-single by default, 1 seat and 1 round; auto follows the risk rule; review-deep is 2 seats; a project may only pick a stricter preset than its user. |
| `review.rounds` | integer | — | narrow-only | — | — | Review rounds; unset uses the preset's rounds, a project may only choose more rounds than its user layer. |
| `review.sameFamily` | enum | `warn` | narrow-only | — | — | Whether a reviewer seat from the author's model family is refused or only warned about; a project may only choose refuse. |
| `review.crossFamilyRereview` | enum | `on` | narrow-only | — | — | Cross-family re-review of a changed record; a project may only keep it on. |
| `review.extraLenses` | string-list | `[]` | project-overridable | — | — | Lens ids added to every review seat; lists from all layers add up and never remove. |
| `review.blockerClauses` | string-list | `[]` | project-overridable | — | — | Extra project rules a reviewer must treat as blockers; lists from all layers add up and never remove. |
| `debate.preset` | enum | `debate-2` | project-overridable | — | — | Debate protocol preset for this project. |
| `primary.harness` | enum | `unknown` | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_PRIMARY_HARNESS` | — | Which harness the main session runs, so DoFlow can tell the primary from its workers; unknown means undeclared. |
| `primary.model` | model-id | — | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_PRIMARY_MODEL` | — | Model the main session runs, as the harness names it; a declaration the hook probe checks where it can. |
| `primary.onContradiction` | enum | `warn` | narrow-only | — | — | What happens when the probe sees a model other than the declared one: warn, or refuse; a project may only choose refuse. |
| `workers.enabled` | worker-list | `["claude","codex","opencode","pi","copilot","kiro","antigravity","primary"]` | narrow-only | — | — | Ordered list of worker ids a run may use, optionally ending in primary; a project can only remove entries. |
| `workers.models` | worker-models | — | global-only | — | — | Model per worker and tier \(light, standard, frontier\) plus the orchestrator role; a worker or key not listed keeps the harness default. A slot is a model id, or an object with model, an optional provider and an optional effort. Written by doflow models --action bind or unbind, by a person. |
| `models.enabled` | model-list | `[]` | project-overridable | — | — | Allow-list of models permitted for task bindings and dispatch rungs. |
| `packages.disabled` | string-list | `[]` | project-overridable | — | — | Package ids turned off for this view; lists from all layers add up, so a project can only turn packages off. |
| `rollout.profile` | enum | `baseline` | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_ROLLOUT_PROFILE` | — | Active rollout profile governing retrieval, decision advisor, and verification automation. |
| `team.writerCap` | integer | `1` | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_TEAM_WRITER_CAP` | — | Maximum concurrent mutating worker sessions considered by team admission, default 1; dispatch enforces the cap only when team.enforcement is block. The user may raise it. |
| `team.maxAttempts` | integer | `3` | project-overridable | `DOFLOW_TEAM_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | — | Maximum attempt count permitted for any single plan task before escalating to operator intervention. |

<!-- END GENERATED:settings-keys -->

DoFlow configuration layers resolve settings across registry defaults, environment variables, and user and project settings files.

`doflow config schema` lists every key with its type, allowed values, bounds, resolved default and scope, and marks each one `writable` or not. `doflow config set <key> <json-value> --layer user|project|local` writes a writable key into that layer's file after checking it the way the resolver reads it (the key's scope, its value rules, and for a `narrow-only` key that a project or local value is not wider than the layers below); `config validate` runs the same checks and writes nothing, and `config unset` removes a key from a layer. `config validate --prepared-run <file>` checks a prepared-run file the same way, one entry per fault keyed by JSON path, with no harness, model call or write: its `settings` entries are judged as `config validate <key> <value> --layer project`, and its `presets.review` and `presets.debate` must not be looser than the preset the settings give (the user, project and local files that `review --action start` reads, with the file's own `review.preset` or `debate.preset` taken as the project layer; a looser one is `not-narrower` naming the setting and its layer), and `presets.review` must also have at least the class's gate-b seat floor (the risk-policy step needs the diff and is not applied). A `config set` or `config unset` at the project or local layer may remove or lower a project's own stricter value back to what the lower layers give (for example `doflow config unset review.rounds --layer project`); that is intended, since any session may write these keys. The class seat floor in `gates.json` and its override, the preset's `rounds.min`, and the invariants are applied after the settings and cannot be lowered this way. `config set` and `config unset` refuse every security-relevant key (`autopilot.enabled`, `verify.reuse`, `workers.billing` and every `advice.*` key, `advice.model` included) and every `invariant.*` entry, which is locked in `core/registry/protocols.json`. A person writes a security-relevant key in the file. A settings file that is a symbolic link (for example from a dotfile manager) is written through to its target and stays a link; a project or local file whose link leads outside the project, or nowhere, is refused naming the file. A write that would rewrite a number of more than 15 digits that is not a safe integer (JSON cannot write it back exactly) is refused naming the key; edit the file by hand. `advise --action configure` is that person's command for `advice.model` and is not affected. Because `advice.model` is security-relevant, an invalid `advice.model` at a layer that may widen it (the user file, or a project file) now trips `advice.enabled` to its fail-safe `false`, and `config list` exits 1 until the value is fixed. This holds for an invalid `advice.model` at any layer, including a malformed project `settings.json`, which turns advice off instead of being ignored. When the user layer has already turned advice off explicitly (`advice.enabled` false), the value is the same either way (off), but the two readers name different causes: `doflow config list` credits the malformed project file as the origin of `advice.enabled` (its fail-safe) and exits 1 until the file is fixed, while `advise` reports the state as `user-disabled` and never reads the project file.

`config set` and `config unset` write one layer file at a time under a lock beside it (`<file>.lock`). A lock held by another writer is waited on for 2 s and then the write is refused with `locked`, naming the lock file and its age, nothing written. A lock left behind is removed automatically: at once when its record names this host and its owner process is gone, otherwise when it is older than 10 minutes. A key that another `config set` wrote meanwhile is kept: when the file changed between the check and the write, the command checks again (three tries in all) before it refuses with `changed`. A write that changes an existing file first keeps the bytes it replaces in one `<file>.config.bak` beside it, replaced on each write, so a layer has at most one config backup. The check judges the bytes the writer would write: a value that would take the layer file over the reader's 64 KiB limit is refused with `too-large` and the file is left as it was. `review.rounds` below the `rounds.min` of the effective `review.preset` is refused as `not-narrower`, naming the floor, because the resolver would ignore it; under `auto` it is accepted, with a note (a text line, and `notes` in `--json`) when it is below the strictest preset's `rounds.min`.

`autopilot.enabled` is the roadmap loop's kill switch: `off` unless a person turns it on. It is global-only, so a project layer never sets it. To turn it on, write `{"schema":1,"autopilot.enabled":"on"}` into `<config home>/settings.json` yourself (`config set` refuses it: it is security-relevant). `DOFLOW_AUTOPILOT_ENABLED` accepts only the off words (`off`, `0`, `false`, `no`) and can only turn the loop off; it cannot turn it on. `src/runtime/settings/autopilot.ts` holds two readers: `readKillSwitch`, used by the roadmap loop and `orchestrate --action decide-gate --policy`, which fails to off on any doubt and counts the env off layer; and `readSeedSwitch`, used by the `roadmap seed` verb, which fails closed the other way: it refuses on any doubt, ignores the env layer, and allows only a verified durable off. The sentinel `<config home>/autopilot/off` also forces off, and a sentinel that cannot be read (any stat error but a missing file) counts as present: the loop's tripwire writes it when it sees a revert of an autopilot merge or a `merged-unverified` result, and only a person removes it. The loop and `orchestrate --action decide-gate --policy` read every layer on every call and fail to off on a missing config home or an unreadable setting. The key combines by `override`, so `doflow config -g` can show `on` while only the sentinel is set (the sentinel is listed as a shadowed candidate); the loop still stops and names `sentinel` in `annotations.switch`. Trust the loop's answer, not the `config` value.

`dispatch.fallback` accepts only `off` and `ask`, and `auto` never takes effect from settings: a user-scope `settings.json` naming `auto` is invalid and the key's fail-safe `off` applies, while a project or local file naming it is ignored and the other layers decide (default `ask`). `auto` exists only per run, and a project may only narrow the setting to `off`.

`workers.billing` is a person-written record of which headless workers have no per-run charge for your account. It has type `worker-billing`: a JSON object of 1 to 16 entries keyed by worker id (a registry harness id, for example `codex` or `antigravity`, the `agy` CLI; lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, at most 32 characters, starting with a letter). Each entry has exactly the fields `billing` (`free` or `metered`), `asserted` (a real `YYYY-MM-DD` date you state) and, optionally, `basis` (one line of 1 to 120 characters); any other field, a wrong type or an impossible date makes the whole value invalid, never a partial one. For example, `{"schema":1,"workers.billing":{"codex":{"billing":"free","asserted":"2026-10-23","basis":"flat plan"}}}` in `<config home>/settings.json`. The key is `global-only`: a project or local value is ignored and listed as `ignored-scope`, so a repository cannot widen what counts as free. The record is read from the caller's config home (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `$HOME`) and trusted as written: DoFlow does not guard who wrote that file, the same as `autopilot.enabled`. It is security-relevant with a fail-safe of invalid, so `config list` exits 1 while the record is invalid, and `config set` refuses it, so a person writes it in the file. `free` means no per-run charge for your account; a flat subscription counts, and quota limits are still handled by the fallback ladder (`dispatch.fallback`). Only `dispatch --billing-cap free` reads the record: `free` lifts a registry `unknown`, `metered` narrows, and a registry `metered` is never lifted; see the `dispatch` row in the [command index](#command-index) for the exact rule. A record naming an id that is not a registry harness is not caught by `config`; dispatch treats it as an invalid record.

`primary.harness` tells DoFlow which harness runs the primary orchestrating session (`claude`, `codex`, `opencode`, `pi`, `copilot`, `kiro`, `antigravity`), so DoFlow can distinguish the primary orchestrator from headless worker runs; `unknown` means undeclared. Default is `unknown`. It may be set through `DOFLOW_PRIMARY_HARNESS` or `config set primary.harness <id>`. Scope is `project-overridable`.

`primary.model` declares the model the main session runs (e.g. a model name string up to 120 characters), which the session hook probe checks where supported (`primaryProbe.model: payload`). Default is null. It may be set through `DOFLOW_PRIMARY_MODEL` or `config set primary.model <id>`. Scope is `project-overridable`.

`primary.onContradiction` sets what happens when the session hook observation contradicts the declared model: `warn` (default) prints a warning line on stderr, while `refuse` refuses `primary --action resolve` and `review --action start` with `primary-refused`. Scope is `narrow-only`: a project or local setting can only narrow `warn` to `refuse`, never relax `refuse` to `warn`.

`workers.enabled` is an ordered list of worker ids a run may use, optionally ending in `primary`. Scope is `narrow-only` and combine is `subset`: a project or local layer can only remove worker ids from the user's enabled list, never add new ones. When unset, all shipped harnesses followed by `primary` are enabled. It is security-relevant (`writable: false` via `config set`); write it directly into `<config home>/settings.json` after validation.

`workers.models` maps worker ids to model names by tier (`light`, `standard`, `frontier`) and by a fourth key, `orchestrator`, the role that runs the plan (agent owner `delivery-lead`; it is not a dispatch tier). Keys not listed fall back to the harness default. A slot is a model id, or an object `{"model": <id>, "provider": <name>, "effort": <level>}` where `model` is required and `provider` and `effort` are optional; a plain id is the object with only `model`. The orchestrator's model is, first match wins: the bound `orchestrator` id, the main session's model on its own harness (`primary.harness`, `primary.model`), counted only when the hook observed it or the user settings file declares it; a project, local or env declaration is ignored here, the bound `frontier` id, the harness default; a task line's explicit tier still wins over the owner's default. Scope is `global-only` and the key is security-relevant (`writable: false` via `config set`); `doflow models --action bind` and `unbind` are its writer. Both, like `advise --action configure`, refuse from a worker session (`DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE` set and not `main`): defence in depth, not a boundary.

Listing a worker id in `workers.enabled` only allows it; it does not make the harness eligible for `doflow worker --action run`, which depends on the registry's capability records and the evidence under `core/worker-evidence/`. Which harnesses qualify today, and the `doflow-worker-ro` agent that `doflow install` writes for opencode, are under [External worker support by harness](#external-worker-support-by-harness). `workers.models` reaches a worker launch only through a `supported` `model` capability of that harness.

To write the worker order or a model binding by hand instead of with `doflow models --action bind`, validate the values using `config validate` with `--layer user` first:
```bash
doflow config validate workers.enabled '["worker-a","worker-b","primary"]' --layer user
doflow config validate workers.models '{"worker-a":{"standard":"vendor-model-std","frontier":"vendor-model-adv"}}' --layer user
```
Once validated with no errors or warnings, save the configuration in `<config home>/settings.json`:
```json
{
  "schema": 1,
  "workers.enabled": ["worker-a", "worker-b", "primary"],
  "workers.models": {
    "worker-a": {
      "standard": "vendor-model-std",
      "frontier": "vendor-model-adv"
    }
  }
}
```

The review keys (`review.preset`, `review.rounds`, `review.sameFamily`, `review.crossFamilyRereview`) are narrow-only: a project can only make its own review stricter. `review.extraLenses` and `review.blockerClauses` are lists that add across layers and never remove. `review.rounds` unset means the preset's rounds. A looser preset setting is ignored and shown under `protocol.disagreements` in `doflow review --action start` and `doflow protocol --action resolve`. An invalid `review.*` value in the project or local file, and an unknown or invalid `review.preset` or `debate.preset` in any layer (user, project or local), is not read as the default: `review --action start` and `protocol --action resolve` refuse with `settings-unavailable` naming the key and file, `review --action check` exits 1 with `same-family-setting-unread` (for `review.sameFamily`, for a findings record that declares an `author`) or `review-preset-setting-unread` (for `review.preset`), and roadmap gate-b fails closed. A malformed user `settings.json` (bad JSON or another schema) therefore refuses review start, review check and gate-b in every project until it is fixed. When `review.preset` is neither `auto` nor the loosest preset (`review-single`), a findings record without `protocol` is invalid (`protocol-missing` at `review --action check`, `findings-invalid` at gate-b) and is held to that preset's rounds and seats; under `auto` or `review-single` it stays valid. A preset added to `core/registry/protocols.json` needs no edit to `settings.json` or `telemetry.json`: the order of the `review.preset` values (`auto` ranks right after the preset of depth `deep`) and the `protocol` and `depth` vocabularies of the review telemetry line are derived from it; an unusable `protocols.json` leaves `review.preset` at `auto` and writes `protocol` and `depth` as null.

The default endpoint is the selected provider's `defaultEndpoint` in `advice.json`. `storage.retentionHours` defaults to the `lifecycle-events` bound in `storage.json`.

`advise --action configure` writes advice settings to `settings.json`. The old `decision-model.json` and one-line `prune` files are no longer read; an existing one is ignored and left in place.

`failure --action capture --set on|off` writes `failure.capture` in the global `settings.json`. A `failures/off` file is no longer read; capture is on unless the environment or `settings.json` says off.

**Thin by default (maint-thin, 2026-10-26).** The shipped `review.preset` is `review-single`: one seat, one round, whatever the change's risk or scope. A person who wants more sets it: `review-deep` for two seats, `auto` for the risk rule (which picks `review-deep` for a high-risk or broad change), or `review.rounds` for more rounds. `narrow-only` still holds, so a project or local file can only make a review stricter than the user's, never thinner. A layer that names a preset this install does not have is not read as the thin default: `review --action start` refuses with `settings-unavailable` (see above), so the review is blocked until the value is fixed, which fails closed. The debate default is unchanged (`debate-2`).

**The one exception: autopilot's unattended gate-b.** The class seat floor in `core/registry/gates.json` (`minSeats: 2` for `feature`, `bug`, `refactor`, `documentation` and `trivial-edit`) binds unless autopilot is verifiably off, because with autopilot on no person reads the review before the merge. Verifiably off means a resolved config home whose `autopilot.enabled` is `off` (the shipped value) or whose `autopilot/off` file exists; `on`, a value or file that cannot be read, or no config home counts as unattended, so the floor binds. An explicit `DOFLOW_AUTOPILOT_ENABLED=off` in the review's own process also counts as attended: roadmap gate-b still judges the recorded seats against the class floor itself. A review a person starts with autopilot off follows the preset, so a feature review is one seat by default. `doflow review --action start` and `protocol --action resolve` report `floor` 0 in that case, and the floor's value with `raisedBy: gate-floor` otherwise.

**Writer admission.** `team.writerCap` defaults to 1. With `block` enforcement, dispatch skips a task when the admission controller refuses it for exceeding the cap. Read-only work may still run in parallel. In `advisory` (the default) the verdict is recorded as a `team.verdict` telemetry line and never refuses a task. Review verbs only record, whichever value is set. A project may only choose `block`, not loosen a user's `block`. Raise the cap (`doflow config set team.writerCap 2 --layer user`) when the write sets are disjoint and you want the speed. Sources for the default: Cognition, "Don't Build Multi-Agents" (2025) and "Multi-Agents: What's Actually Working" (2026), on conflicting implicit decisions between parallel writers; Anthropic, "How we built our multi-agent research system" (2025), where a multi-agent run used about 15 times the tokens of a chat, so it pays only for wide, parallel, read-heavy work; Anthropic, "Building effective agents" (2024), on starting with the simplest pattern that works.

### Config home

DoFlow's per-machine files (`prompt-nudge`, `settings.json`, `failures/`, `reports/`, `session-env/`, `sessions.log`) live in one folder, `<base>/doflow`. The base is `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` when it is set to an absolute path, else `$HOME/.config` when `HOME` is set to an absolute path, else there is no config home. A relative or empty value is ignored, never read against the working directory, and DoFlow does not fall back to the account's home folder. Node code and every shell script and hook resolve it by the same rule.

With no config home, nothing is read from it, failure capture is skipped, session state is skipped (session hooks write nothing and exit 0; guard hooks still reach their own decision), and `doflow advise --action configure` without `--project` exits 2 with a usage message naming an absolute `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, or `--project`.

Failure capture also reads `failure.capture` from `<config home>/settings.json` with one text rule that the dispatcher, the shell policies, the JS hook runner and Node all apply to the file's bytes up to the first NUL: every `"failure.capture": <value>` pair found must have the value `"on"`, and any other value means capture is off. JSON validity does not matter, one pair that is not `"on"` makes it off even when another says `"on"`, and a file that is not a regular file or cannot be read means off. A missing file or a file without the key leaves capture on. The environment can still only turn capture off.

### Decision-model kind: claim-support

`advice.kinds.claim-support` takes `off`, `shadow` or `act`. Unset records in shadow when a decision model is configured and makes no call otherwise, and an invalid value is off. The kind is held at `shadow`: a value of `act` is accepted and runs as `shadow` with a warning (see [The evidence path](#the-evidence-path)). The advice setting must be on, and the kind needs a decision model. `advice.models.claim-support` names the model for that kind; unset uses `advice.model`. What the kind records, and what `doflow advise --action report` shows, is under [Decision model](#decision-model-report-evidence-and-shadow-kinds).

The `rerank` kind has no setting and no runtime caller: `retrieve` makes no decision-model call, and the kind stays in `core/registry/advice.json` only so that `bench advice-gate --kind rerank` can measure it (see [Rerank, offline only](#rerank-offline-only)). A user or project `settings.json` that still holds a removed key (`advice.kinds.rerank`, `advice.models.rerank`, `advice.criteria.rerank`, `advice.kinds.followup-triage` or `advice.models.followup-triage`) is told so: `doflow advise` prints one line per key on stderr, or lists them in `warnings` with `--json`, and `doflow doctor` lists them under the decision model. The key is ignored; delete it.

## Decision model: report, evidence and shadow kinds

This section covers the report action, the evidence path that decides whether a kind may run in `act` mode, and the kind that only records (`claim-support`). Setup is in [Setup](setup.md#decision-model-optional) and the keys are in [Settings keys](#settings-keys). With advice on and a decision model set, every kind not set to `off` makes a shadow call, claim-support included; a kind left unset takes the same path as one set to `shadow` (see [Shadow calls](#shadow-calls)), and with no decision model configured it makes no call. Set `advice.kinds.<kind>` to `off` to stop one. A shadow call records a pick and shows nothing. Every figure below states its n. The one measured latency table, under [Rerank, offline only](#rerank-offline-only), comes from one laptop, one local decision model and one session; it describes that run and nothing else.

### The report

```bash
doflow advise --action report [--kind <id>] [--json] [-g]
```

The report makes no model call and writes nothing. It reads the advice records of the last 90 days (the retention bound of `advice-picks-project` and `advice-picks-user`) from the project's `.doflow/state/advice/picks` and from the user-level folder; `-g` reads the user-level folder only. Without `--kind` it lists every kind that a caller asks and any other kind found in the records (a pick of a kind since removed still shows); `--kind` takes a kind that a caller asks or `adhoc`, and an unknown id exits 2 with the known ids. Exit 0 on success, 1 when the advice registry cannot be read. The text form ends with the line `Records are from this project only; every figure shows its n.` (with `-g`: `Records are from the user-level store only; every figure shows its n.`, and `scope` reads `user-level store`), and the first lines give the covered date range. `--json` prints `schema` (1), `scope`, `window`, `unreadable` (lines skipped), `kinds`, `claimSupport` (only when that kind is shown) and `verdicts`.

For each kind the report shows these figures; `n=0` is printed as `no data`.

| Figure | What it counts | Its n |
|---|---|---|
| picks, misses | Calls that gave a pick, and calls that did not, with the misses grouped by reason (`timeout`, for example) | picks and misses |
| agrees with the static answer | Picks equal to the static answer recorded with the call. This is agreement, not correctness | picks that recorded a static answer |
| agrees with the recorded outcome | Picks for which `advise --action outcome` recorded what happened, and the share that matched | picks that have an outcome |
| elevated | Picks that raised a decision | picks |
| modes | Picks recorded under `shadow` and under `act`; lines written before modes were recorded count as `unrecorded` | picks |
| duration ms | Nearest-rank p50, p90 and p95 of the call time, for picks and for misses separately | the calls with a duration |

The `claim-support` block groups picks by the relation recorded with the link (`supports`, `contradicts`) and shows how many of them the model called `yes` at a probability of 0.5 or more and how many agree with the relation (`supports` means yes, `contradicts` means no). A recorded link is an assertion and not ground truth, so this agreement is not accuracy.

When `core/registry/advice-verdicts.json` holds verdicts, a `Bench verdicts` list gives the result of each and, when it is stale, the reason. The shipped file holds none, so the list is empty.

### The evidence path

`act` is reachable only for a kind and a model that have a matching passing verdict in `core/registry/advice-verdicts.json`. Nothing ships a verdict, so no kind runs in `act` today: a kind set to `act` runs as shadow and names `BENCH_BAR_UNMET` with the reason. A kind left unset never runs in `act`. A file in a user's project cannot supply a verdict; the evaluator reads only the shipped file and recomputes pass or fail from the stored numbers and the current bar, so a stored `pass` flag is not trusted.

**Frozen corpus, format 2.** `bench/advice/<kind>.json` holds `schemaVersion` 2, the `kind`, an integer `corpusVersion`, and cases with `id`, `split` (`train` or `heldout`), `state`, `staticPick`, `expected`, `origin` and `note`. `origin` is `synthetic` or `consented`. A case is never copied from a user's prompt, state or records. The gate scores the `heldout` cases only. The corpus digest is the sha256 of the heldout cases without `note`, ordered by id. `npm run bench -- advice-coverage` checks the format. The heldout counts in the committed corpora (n per kind, all `synthetic`): `tier` 32, `task-class` 36, `verify-risk` 32, `review-depth` 31, `claim-support` 36, `mcp-need` 1. `mcp-need` is below the minimum of 30 cases, so it can only produce `insufficient-data`. The `rerank` kind has no file there: it is scored on the retrieval bench's dev split (`bench/retrieval/tasks/dev.json`), never on the sealed holdout.

**Runner.**

```bash
npm run bench -- advice-gate --kind <id> [--arm choice|ladder|both] [--model <name>] [--write] [--json]
npm run bench -- advice-gate --freeze
```

It uses the decision model configured for the user, so advice must be on, the endpoint reached, and the model installed and decision-capable. Every heldout case is asked. A case with no valid answer (timeout, unreachable, malformed, an option outside the set) is scored as the static answer and stays in the count, so the model is never judged on the cases it answered alone. The verdict holds digests, counts and numbers: no case text and no per-case row. Per-case outcomes (id, pick, latency) go to stdout under `--json` and are not stored. `--write` replaces the verdict for the same kind, model and arm; it refuses when the corpus changed and `corpusVersion` did not rise. For `--kind rerank` the runner scores nDCG@5 of the model order against the first-stage order on the dev tasks of both corpora, one verdict per arm, and prints the paired difference of the arms, the tasks skipped for having fewer than 2 candidates, and how many judged-relevant candidates each ladder threshold would have dropped.

**The bar.** The numbers are in the `gate` block of `core/registry/advice.json` and are illustrative: starting values chosen from the checks in the paper [arXiv 2610.34816](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.34816), not guarantees and not measured on DoFlow's data. Tune them there.

| Check | Shipped value | Result when unmet |
|---|---|---|
| cases (`minCases`) | 30 | `insufficient-data`, never a pass |
| valid share (`validShareMin`) | 0.99 | `fail` |
| quality | lower end of the 95% bootstrap interval (10000 resamples, seed 20261106) of the mean per-case difference between the model and the static answer is above 0 | `fail` |
| latency | p95 of the case latency at most 5000 ms (3000 ms for `rerank`) | `fail` |

Quality is accuracy for choice and yes/no kinds and nDCG@5 for `rerank`. Every number in the block is read by a check and is part of the digest, so changing one makes verdicts stale.

**What makes a verdict stale.** The evaluator refuses a verdict, and `act` stays shadow with the reason, when the pass-bar numbers changed (`gate` version or digest, over every number in the block except `illustrative`), when the corpus changed (`corpusVersion` or digest), or, for a `rerank` verdict, when the digest of the criteria in force (the registry text) differs from the one the verdict was measured under. A verdict is also keyed by kind, model and arm: another model, or another arm, has no verdict. A stored result that differs from the one its numbers give is `inconsistent`.

For `claim-support` the registry sets `maxMode: shadow`, so no verdict can make it act. A setting that names `act` for it is accepted, and the kind runs as `shadow` with the warning `ACT_NOT_SUPPORTED: kind 'claim-support' is limited to shadow`; `claim` does not print it. The `rerank` arm and criteria binding exists for a later, separate decision.

### Rerank, offline only

Nothing asks the `rerank` kind at run time: `retrieve` prints its results and makes no decision-model call, and no mode, model or criteria setting exists for it. The kind stays in `core/registry/advice.json` (with an empty `askedBy`) so that `npm run bench -- advice-gate --kind rerank` can score a model as a reranker on the retrieval bench's dev split. The criteria text the model is shown comes from the `rerank` entry of that file only.

- **Shortlist.** The first 16 first-stage candidates of a dev task (at least 2, else no call). Each candidate is cut to 800 bytes and the query to 2048 bytes. These caps and the arm are in the `rerank` entry.
- **Arm `choice` (the default).** One question over the labelled candidates (`A` to `P`); the labels are ordered by the probability the model gives them, ties by first-stage rank.
- **Arm `ladder`.** One 0-to-4 question per candidate, all in one request, each answered with an expected score and a confidence; candidates are ordered by expected score, then confidence, then first-stage rank. The ladder is a hypothesis. The paper reports per-candidate scoring weaker than one choice in zero-shot use, and no result here shows the ladder better than the choice arm. `--arm` picks which arm the gate scores.
- **Verdict.** One per model and arm, bound to the digest of the criteria in force; the `would-drop` counts for the illustrative thresholds in the `rerank` entry (`confidenceBelow` 0.5, 0.7, 0.9 and `expectedBelow` 1, 2) are printed and not stored. Nothing is dropped, filtered or reordered.

**Measured latency, with its limits.** One session on 2026-11-06 on one laptop, one local decision model, load average falling from about 9 to about 2 during the run, so the cells run first are slower: 16 candidates, 20 distinct guidance queries per cell (n = 20; n = 19 in the two 800-byte cells, because one query had been answered from a cache). All 120 of 120 replies were valid. Cold p95 latency was 4.5 to 11.6 s for the choice arm and 12.8 to 21.7 s for the ladder arm, over three candidate sizes of 400, 800 and 1600 bytes. The shipped 800-byte choice cell had a p95 of 11.6 s. Identical repeated requests returned within 116 ms (n = 10 per cell), so a repeated query tells nothing about a new one. The original measurement table was lost and is not in the repository, so these figures cannot be re-checked from it. The queries are about this repository's guidance, so they say nothing about other content, and 20 queries is a small n for a p95.

What follows for the settings in the registry: the `budgetMs` of the `rerank` entry is 5000 ms, and at that budget many calls on that laptop would end as `timeout` misses (at 800 bytes the choice arm took longer than 5 s in 17 of 19 requests, and the ladder arm took longer in every uncached request, n = 59).
