The rerank latency bar of 3000 ms was not met by any measured cell (best p95 4456 ms, choice arm at 400 bytes, n = 20), so the gate is expected to report a latency failure for this model on that laptop. A cap was not changed to fit the result.

### Shadow calls

A shadow call records the model's pick beside the static answer and shows nothing. It runs in a detached child process (the shadow runner beside the dispatcher in `src/runtime/advice/`, started with the dispatcher's own extension: `.ts` in the checkout, `.js` in the published package), so a command that waits for its result does not wait for the model: the caller writes the state text and the question to a handoff file beside its slot (readable by the user only), starts the child with that file's path and no stdio at all, prints its own output and goes on. Nothing in the handoff waits on the child, so a child that never reads cannot hold the command. The child reads and removes the handoff before anything else; a handoff whose child never read it is removed with its slot. A record holds digests and byte counts only, never the state text.

- **Input cap.** A state over `limits.maxStateBytes` (the cap the client applies) records an `over-limit` miss before any slot is taken or file written, and starts no child.

- **Slots.** The caller takes one of at most `shadowSlots` (4) slot files in `~/.doflow/state/advice/shadow-slots`, shared by every project of the user (store `advice-shadow-slots-user`), before it starts the child; the child frees it when it ends. With none free the caller starts no child and records a `busy` miss. The bound: at most `shadowSlots` shadow children are alive per user at any time, so a burst of calls across projects starts no more than that, and every call past it is a `busy` miss. A slot whose child is gone, or that has been held longer than its budget plus 5 s, is reclaimed by one call only; a child that started late ends before its slot can be reclaimed for its age.
- **Budget.** Each kind has its own `budgetMs` in `core/registry/advice.json`: `tier` 1500, `task-class` 3000, `verify-risk` 2000, `review-depth` 1500, `mcp-need` 1500 and `claim-support` 1500 ms (`rerank` has 5000, used only by the offline gate). A budget is three times the kind's warm measured maximum server duration, rounded up to 500 ms, with a floor of 1500; its `description` in the registry says so. `timeoutMs` (15000) bounds only a question with no kind.
- **Spawn failure.** A runner file that is missing, a handoff that cannot be written, a child that cannot start, and a child that cannot load the modules it asks with each record a `spawn-failed` miss; only a child that cannot load `record.ts`, which writes every record, leaves none. The command's output and exit status are those of the run with the kind off.
- **Measured, with its limits.** The command's median time in ms against a model that never finishes its reply, kind off / kind in shadow, before the detached child and after it (n = 3 runs per cell, one machine): `classify` 87 / 15123 and then 87 / 89; `claim --action link` 233 / 5171 and then 133 / 137; `review --action start` 235 / 15230 and then 183 / 220; `retrieve` 120 / 5125 and then 101 / 112 (`retrieve` no longer makes a decision-model call, so its row describes the removed hook). Before the change, shadow made a waited command last as long as the model took, up to the kind's budget.

### Claim-support in shadow

`advice.kinds.claim-support` set to `shadow`, or left unset while a decision model is configured, makes `claim --action link` ask the model, after it has saved and printed, whether the claim is fully supported by the linked evidence text. The model's yes probability is recorded beside the relation and the claim status that the link produced. The call has a `budgetMs` of 1500 ms and runs in a detached child, so the command does not wait for it; the claim is cut to 1024 bytes and the passage to 2048 bytes. It is skipped when the relation is not `supports` or `contradicts`, or the claim or the evidence has no text. The kind never changes a claim, an evidence item, a readiness result, the command's output or its exit status, and it writes no score into the claim or evidence records. The pick line holds the claim id, the evidence id, the relation, the claim status, byte counts and a truncation flag; no claim or passage text.

### Sources

The rerank shape comes from the repository [Ryenhails/rerank-as-decision](https://github.com/Ryenhails/rerank-as-decision) (reranking posed as one labelled choice of up to 16 options) and the paper [arXiv 2610.34816](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.34816) (reliability, quality and latency checks, candidate truncation, fallback to first-stage order). The repository's README cites a different report (arXiv 2610.28435).

The article "When the reranker becomes the decision layer" was read from text the user pasted; its images, its tables and its tradeoffs section were missing, so no benchmark table was available. It describes a commercial engine, not DoFlow. Its cost, latency and calibration figures are the vendor's claims about its own product and are not results of DoFlow or of `clef-flash`, so none is repeated here. Two of its ideas entered scope: the score ladder with a confidence measure, and the support check on evidence links. Its pre-retrieval guardrail corresponds to the existing `task-class` kind, and nothing was added for it. This feature promises no saving in cost or latency.

## Storage and retention

DoFlow prunes what it writes by default. A pass runs silently after a runtime verb (at most once a day per root), after `verify`, after `install` and `update`, and from the session hooks of harnesses that have them. It removes only entries of finished work past the bound in the [Storage stores](#storage-stores) table, never open, waiting or unfinished work, stops after 750 ms or 5,000 removals and continues on the next pass. A pass never changes a command's output or exit status.

### Storage stores

Roadmap `next` holds `.doflow/state/roadmap/lease.json` for one call, keeps one record per run in
`.doflow/state/roadmap/runs/<runId>.json` (budget, per-row checkpoint, step log) and remembers the
events its tripwire has already reported in `.doflow/state/roadmap/tripwire/events.json`; the
tripwire writes the `autopilot/off` sentinel in the config home, which only a person removes.
A call that finds a lease whose holder is provably dead (same host, process gone) takes it over
through `.doflow/state/roadmap/lease.reclaim`, created exclusively and removed by that call. A lease
or a `lease.reclaim` whose holder cannot be proven dead (another host, a process id that may have
been reused, unreadable contents) is never taken over: every call answers `lease-held` until a
person confirms no `next` is running and deletes the file. Each call re-reads `lease.json` at
every step boundary and stops with `lease-lost` when it no longer holds this call's own bytes.
`.doflow/state/roadmap/audit.jsonl` (store `roadmap-audit`) keeps the last 10000 gate, merge,
render and cleanup entries with their input locators and sha256 hashes; the step log inside a run
record is capped at 500 entries and goes with the record, so `audit.jsonl` is the durable audit.
Roadmap merge intents live in `.doflow/state/roadmap/intents/<row>.json` and move to
`.doflow/state/roadmap/done/<row>.json` only after render and non-force cleanup. Open intents are
never pruned; archived intents follow the 30-day finished-work bound. A run record is kept while its `closedAt` is null (a run that hit its step budget never closes), while it is the newest one, a merge intent names its run or the lease names it, and is otherwise pruned after 30 days; `tripwire/events.json` is kept. `status` includes open intents
and reports `merged-unverified` when no clean PASS verify record for the row on the new develop head answers the post-merge check (the `roadmap` row above states the rule).
That failure keeps the merge, worktree and branch; the next attempt rechecks before render and
cleanup.

`verify --action report` appends one line per report to
`.doflow/state/verification/runs/<taskId>.jsonl` in the repository's main checkout, the lifecycle project root, whichever checkout it ran in (store
`verification-runs`): `schema`, `taskId`, `head` (the commit it ran on), `clean` (whether the tree
was clean), `verdict` (the report's status), `risk` (the report's risk level, `LOW` to `CRITICAL`; a reader treats a missing or invalid one as the default risk level, and gate-b ignores it), `at`, `reused` and `reportSha256`. `reused` (the number of checks the report labels `reused`) is written only when reuse is on for that run, and sits before `reportSha256`; with reuse off the record is byte-identical to the one before reuse existed. A record is pruned 30 days after its task's work is finished (the `work` rule, as for the other per-task records); a task with an unchecked plan or an open run is never pruned by age. A failure to write the line
loses only the record and never changes the report. The roadmap gates read only lines whose `head`
is the head under decision and whose tree was clean. The roadmap state under `.doflow/state/roadmap`
is one store (`roadmap`) with the sub-directories `intents`, `done`, `runs` and `tripwire`; the
audit file is its own store (`roadmap-audit`) and the sentinel is its own store (`autopilot-switch`)
in the config home.

These are the stores DoFlow keeps on this machine. A pruned store has the bound shown; an `existing` store keeps the bound its own code already enforces, and the cleanup never touches it. Paths starting `.doflow` are relative to the project root; `~/.config/doflow` is `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/doflow` when that variable is set to an absolute path. `core/registry/storage.json` declares the stores and `test/guards/storage-declarations.test.ts` (G26) checks this table against it.

| Store | Location | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| `verification-logs` | `.doflow/state/verification/logs` | keep newest 40; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `verification-runs` | `.doflow/state/verification/runs` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `verification-v0` | `.doflow/state/verification/v0` | keep newest 40; prune entries older than 7 days |
| `verification-reuse` | `.doflow/state/verification/reuse` | keep newest 400; prune entries older than 2 days |
| `task-evidence` | `.doflow/state/evidence` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-readiness` | `.doflow/state/readiness` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-research` | `.doflow/state/research` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-outcome` | `.doflow/state/outcome` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-retrieval` | `.doflow/state/retrieval` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-orchestration` | `.doflow/state/orchestration` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `task-attempts` | `.doflow/state/task-attempts` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `worker-runs` | `.doflow/state/worker-runs` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `worker-probes` | `.doflow/state/worker-probes` | keep newest 16; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `worker-output` | `.doflow/state/worker-output` | keep newest 200; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `session-prompts` | `.doflow/state/session-prompts` | keep newest 200; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `channel-spool` | `.doflow/state/channel-spool` | keep newest 16; prune entries older than 1 days |
| `dispatch` | `.doflow/state/dispatch` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `roadmap` | `.doflow/state/roadmap` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `dispatch-envelopes` | `.doflow/state/dispatch-envelopes` | keep newest 400; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `dispatch-worktrees` | `.doflow/state/dispatch-worktrees` | attempt worktrees removed by dispatch cleanup; the integration worktree stays for the user to merge |
| `task-delivery` | `.doflow/state/delivery` | prune finished work after 30 days |
| `runs-project` | `.doflow/state/runs` | prune partitions older than 180 days |
| `runs-telemetry` | `.doflow/state/runs/telemetry` | prune partitions older than 180 days |
| `runs-user` | `~/.doflow/state/runs` | prune partitions older than 180 days |
| `advice-picks-project` | `.doflow/state/advice/picks` | prune partitions older than 90 days |
| `advice-picks-user` | `~/.doflow/state/advice/picks` | prune partitions older than 90 days |
| `advice-shadow-slots-user` | `~/.doflow/state/advice/shadow-slots` | at most shadowSlots slot files (core/registry/advice.json), one per live detached shadow child, each taken before the child starts and removed when it ends, with at most one handoff file each, which the child removes on start; a stale slot (its child gone, or older than its budget plus 5 s) is reclaimed in place by one call, with its handoff |
| `slots-user` | `~/.doflow/state/slots` | keep newest 64; prune entries older than 30 days |
| `recovery-user` | `~/.doflow/state/recovery` | keep newest 3, plus the snapshot named by the ledger |
| `recovery-project` | `.doflow/state/recovery` | keep newest 3, plus the snapshot named by the ledger |
| `lifecycle-events` | `.doflow/state/lifecycle/events` | retain settled events for 4320 hours |
| `report-bodies` | `~/.config/doflow/reports` | prune orphan bodies after 24 hours; foreign folders are kept |
| `session-dirs` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/sessions` | prune inactive directories after 30 days |
| `project-dirs` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/projects` | prune inactive directories after 90 days |
| `summary-scopes` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/summaries` | prune inactive directories after 90 days |
| `summary-grants` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/grants` | prune inactive directories after 90 days |
| `model-observations` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/primary` | prune inactive directories after 7 days |
| `worker-model-observations` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/worker-model` | keep newest 1000; prune entries older than 7 days |
| `installer-backups-user-doflow` | `~/.doflow/backups` | keep 3 |
| `installer-backups-project-doflow` | `.doflow/backups` | keep 3 |
| `nudge-markers` | `~/.config/doflow/session-env/nudge` | prune after 30 days |
| `sessions-log` | `~/.config/doflow/sessions.log` | keep last 500 lines |
| `failure-events` | `~/.config/doflow/failures` | keep 4 rotated files |
| `autopilot-switch` | `~/.config/doflow/autopilot/off` | one sentinel file; only a person removes it |
| `roadmap-audit` | `.doflow/state/roadmap/audit.jsonl` | keep the last 10000 gate, merge, render and cleanup entries |
| `install-ledger-user-state` | `~/.doflow/state/ledger.json` | one file, overwritten |
| `install-ledger-project-state` | `.doflow/state/ledger.json` | one file, overwritten |
| `lifecycle-control` | `.doflow/state/lifecycle` | self-cleaning |
| `task-locks-project-state` | `.doflow/state/<store>/<file>.lock` | removed on release; a stale lock is reclaimed in place |
| `install-projections-user-doflow` | `~/.doflow/{scripts,runtime,guidance,shared,templates,.install-manifest.json,doflow.lock}` | owned by the install ledger |
| `install-projections-project-doflow` | `.doflow/{scripts,runtime,guidance,shared,templates,.install-manifest.json,doflow.lock}` | owned by the install ledger |
| `knowledge-index-user-doflow` | `~/.doflow/index` | rewritten atomically, sha-keyed |
| `knowledge-index-project-doflow` | `.doflow/index` | rewritten atomically, sha-keyed |
| `code-semantic-project` | `.doflow/index/code/semble` | Explicit bounded local semantic lifecycle; corpus/model manifests, ordinary mirror copies, provider cache, operation lock, in-progress marker and staging; query temporary stats/ sink capped at 64 KiB and library/ (provider grammar and library cache) capped at 128 MiB (libraryBytes), both removed in finally or on the next explicit operation; sourceReadBytes is an over-count of provider reads under Semble 0.7.0. |
| `bench-worktrees` | `.doflow/worktrees` | removed per case by the bench runner, not shipped |
| `user-settings-project-doflow` | `.doflow/{prompt-nudge,settings.json,settings.local.json}` | prompt-nudge is one line; settings.json and settings.local.json are JSON objects; settings.json files are written atomically by settings writers |
| `user-settings-config-home` | `~/.config/doflow/{prompt-nudge,settings.json}` | prompt-nudge is one line; settings.json is a JSON object; settings.json is written atomically by settings writers |
| `settings-writer-project-doflow` | `.doflow/{settings.json,settings.local.json}{.lock,.config.bak}` | a settings writer holds the file's .lock for one read-merge-rename and removes it; a lock is removed automatically when its owner is gone on this host or when it is older than 10 minutes, and a younger lock held by a live or unknown owner refuses the write; a lock is removed by moving it aside first (<lock>.<pid>.<hex>.gone), and a moved-aside file a dead writer left is deleted, once it is older than a minute, by the next writer that finds the lock held; config set and unset keep the bytes they replace in one .config.bak beside the file, replaced on each write |
| `settings-writer-config-home` | `~/.config/doflow/settings.json{.lock,.config.bak}` | a settings writer holds the file's .lock for one read-merge-rename and removes it; a lock is removed automatically when its owner is gone on this host or when it is older than 10 minutes, and a younger lock held by a live or unknown owner refuses the write; a lock is removed by moving it aside first (<lock>.<pid>.<hex>.gone), and a moved-aside file a dead writer left is deleted, once it is older than a minute, by the next writer that finds the lock held; config set and unset keep the bytes they replace in one .config.bak beside the file, replaced on each write |
| `console-device-store` | `~/.config/doflow/console/lan.json{,.lock}` | one JSON file of the Console's paired devices (at most 64), pending pairing codes (at most 16) and LAN Console entries, written whole through a temp file and a rename while lan.json.lock is held; a device idle for 30 days reads as expired and is dropped on the next write 30 days after that, a revoked one 30 days after its revocation, a Console entry 90 days after it started; a stale lock is moved aside before it is removed |
| `console-tls` | `~/.config/doflow/console/tls/{key.pem,cert-<address>.pem}` | one private key (mode 0600) kept until it is missing or unreadable, and at most 4 certificate files, one per LAN address; a certificate is issued again when fewer than 30 days remain and the oldest file is deleted when a fifth is issued |
| `console-action-log` | `~/.config/doflow/console/actions{,.1}.ndjson`, `actions.lock` | two NDJSON generations of the Console's action attempts, each at most 1 MiB: actions.ndjson is renamed over actions.1.ndjson when it reaches 1 MiB; a line is at most 2 KiB and holds no free text; actions.lock exists only during a rotation |
| `packages-store` | `~/.doflow/packages/store` | one directory per package id, kept until the package is removed |
| `packages-plans` | `~/.doflow/packages/plans` | keep newest 256; prune entries older than 1 days |
| `packages-staging` | `~/.doflow/packages/store/.staging-*` | prune inactive directories after 1 days |
| `packages-fetched` | `~/.doflow/packages/fetched` | keep newest 256; prune entries older than 1 days |
| `packages-fetch-staging` | `~/.doflow/packages/fetched/.staging-*` | prune inactive directories after 1 days |
| `packages-state` | `~/.doflow/packages/state.json` | narrowing facts only, rewritten atomically with mode 0600 and kept |
| `authored-packages-project-doflow` | `.doflow/package/<id>` | the person's authored package source: DoFlow never prunes, moves or removes it |
| `authored-packages-staging-project-doflow` | `.doflow/package/.staging-<id>-<8 hex>` | removed by the scaffold itself after 24 hours |
| `feature-pointer` | `.doflow/state/feature` | one file per checkout naming its feature, rewritten in place by the runtime and removed when the feature finishes |
| `project-models-catalogue` | `.doflow/state/models/catalogue.json` | one file, overwritten |
| `user-models-catalogue` | `~/.config/doflow/state/models-observed.json` | one file, overwritten |
| `harness-login` | `~/.config/doflow/state/harness-login.json` | one file, at most 64 harness entries, overwritten |

Notes on six rows:

- **Verification logs** are bounded by count and age, not size. One log of a very verbose suite can reach about 128 MiB; at the typical size the 40 newest logs take a few megabytes. A log named by the evidence of a run waiting at a gate is kept past its bound.
- **Verification V0 state** (`verification-v0` at `.doflow/state/verification/v0`) holds failed-first digests and transient coordination locks and bounded result excerpts. The store keeps the newest 40 and prunes entries older than 7 days (`count-and-age` rule); in-flight sweeps remove results after 2 minutes and abandoned temp/claim files after 10 minutes. Writers are `src/runtime/verification/engine.ts` and `src/runtime/verification/inflight.ts`. Mode 0600; coordination state is excluded from telemetry export.
- **Run partitions** are pruned after 180 days, so `trace`, `stats` and `discover` without `--days` now cover at most the last 180 days.
- **Telemetry partitions** (`runs-telemetry` at `.doflow/state/runs/telemetry`) are pruned after 180 days (`date-partition` rule). Writers are the shared bridge (`core/harnesses/shared/hooks/bridge/bridge.sh`), Claude post-shell observer (`core/harnesses/claude/hooks/post-shell-observe.sh`), and the runtime telemetry writer (`src/runtime/trace/telemetry.ts`).
- **Channel spool** (`channel-spool`, row 170) holds one directory per started session that has a control channel, `.doflow/state/channel-spool/<runId>/`, and in it the messages not yet taken, one file each. The store directory and the run's directory are mode 0700 and each file 0600; no link is followed (the directories are checked with `lstat`, a file is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` where the platform defines it, after an `lstat` that refuses anything but a regular file). `src/runtime/worker/channel-spool.ts` is its only writer. The supervisor deletes each file as it takes it and removes the directory when the session ends; `worker --action cancel` removes it when it finds the supervisor not alive; the `count-and-age` rule of the table is the last resort for a directory nothing removed. Windows is designed for (the same files and a pipe that closes with its writer) and not run.
- **Readiness records** that could not be parsed are renamed to `<task>.json.unreadable`. The `task-readiness` store does not select that name, so pruning never removes those files; delete them by hand when you no longer need them.

### Reading `doflow storage`

```bash
doflow storage                    # report: every store, its entries, size, bound and what a pass would remove
doflow storage --json             # the same as JSON, with the effective setting and the file that decided it
doflow storage --action prune     # run one pass now, ignoring the daily throttle
doflow storage -g                 # user and machine roots only
```

The report changes nothing on disk. It prints one line `Settings: project <on|off> (<file>|default); user <on|off> (<file>|default)`, naming the `settings.json` that decided each setting, then one block per store: its `id`, `Location:`, `Entries: <n>; size: <bytes> bytes`, `Bound:` and `Would remove: <n>`, with up to 20 of those names followed by `... and <n> more`. `--action prune` prints the same and ends with `storage: removed <n> entries`. `--json` adds each store's `failed` entries, each with a `code`; `link-in-record-store` means a work record store holds a link, so log protection could not be checked and that pass removes no logs. `--action upkeep` is what the session hooks run: it prints nothing and always exits 0.

### Switching pruning off

`storage.prune` in the applicable `settings.json` decides whether pruning runs. Set it to `"off"` in `<project root>/.doflow/settings.json` for that project's stores, or in `<config home>/settings.json` for user and machine stores. A project value applies only to project roots. Use `doflow config list --show-origin` to see which file decides the value; `config` is read-only.

The old one-line `prune` files (`<project root>/.doflow/prune`, `<config home>/prune`) are no longer read. For project roots the project file decides when it sets the key, else the user file; user and machine roots read the user file only; with no `storage.prune` value pruning is `on`. For lifecycle-event retention, an explicit `DOFLOW_RETENTION_HOURS` takes precedence over `storage.retentionHours` in a settings file. If the environment value is unset, an explicit settings-file value still applies while pruning is off; with neither explicit value, the registry default applies only while pruning is on. An invalid `DOFLOW_RETENTION_HOURS` is reported as invalid and does not fall back. `storage --action prune` reports whether pruning is off and whether the explicit retention window removed lifecycle events. The report still lists what a pass would remove from other stores; for lifecycle events it lists only what an explicit environment or settings-file value would remove. The ignore file below is still written, and `existing` stores keep their own behaviour.

### What the locks assume

DoFlow's cross-process locks (the run lock, the task-state and slot locks, the settings, intake and upkeep locks, the semantic index lock) are made of the local file system and of one machine: an exclusive create or `mkdir`, an atomic `rename`, and a file's modification time read against the clock of the process that judges it. They judge an owner by its process id and, where the platform gives it, its start time, so they assume one process-id namespace (one machine, no containers sharing a state folder with different pid spaces) and one clock. A lock whose record names another host is never taken for dead; it is reclaimed only when it is old. Age also beats a live owner for every lock except the slot lock: the task-state and intake locks after 10 s, the upkeep lock after 120 s, and the semantic index lock, the settings lock and the run lock after 10 minutes; a run-lock claim file is judged alike, except that a live owner whose recorded start time still matches keeps it at any age. A holder suspended (stopped, or paused in a debugger) for longer than that can lose its lock to another process. Network file systems (NFS, SMB), folders synced between machines and a home folder shared by two machines are not supported: their rename, create and timestamp behaviour is not what the locks rely on, and two machines would not see each other's processes. Windows is not tested for these locks, and the process start time they use to tell a reused process id is not available there. Run DoFlow on a local disk, one machine per state folder.

### The state ignore file

DoFlow writes `.doflow/state/.gitignore` (and `~/.doflow/state/.gitignore`) when the folder exists and holds no `.gitignore`:

```
# DoFlow runtime state: local to this machine, kept out of git by DoFlow.
# Removed by `doflow remove` when DoFlow leaves this project.
*
!/ledger.json
```

`git add -A` then stages nothing under `.doflow/state/` except `ledger.json`, while `.doflow/prompt-nudge`, `.doflow/prune` and `.doflow/scripts/` stay stageable. A `.gitignore` with other contents is yours and is never overwritten. DoFlow never edits the repository's own `.gitignore` or `.git/info/exclude`.

### Uninstall

When `doflow remove` removes the last harness of a scope it deletes that scope's control files: the ignore file (only when its bytes are DoFlow's), the throttle marker `upkeep-due`, the pass lock `upkeep.lock`, and the project's line in the user roots index. It prints `[INFO] Storage: removed <n> DoFlow control file(s)` when it removed any. The state itself and your `prune` and `prompt-nudge` files are left in place.

## Hook envelope and migration notes

Shipped hook bridges decode native events into envelope v1, call a canonical policy and render the harness's native response. The versioned coverage block in `core/registry/lifecycle.json` records policy, harness, event, profile and evidence; `doflow enforcement` reports what that evidence supports.

### Envelope, decision and diagnostics

Envelope v1 is the object under `doflow_event` in the native payload. It carries `version: 1`, the registry harness id, the native event name verbatim and a canonical operation; tool, paths, command, patch, correlation and identity fields appear only when the native input supplies them. `projectRoot` is reserved for a launcher and is never inferred from native `cwd`. Policies read v1 only; every other version is unsupported, and no native-field fallback remains. A direct policy caller must send v1 or pipe its native payload through `bash .doflow/shared/hooks/bridge/bridge.sh <harness> <event> <policy>`.

When `DOFLOW_DECISION_FD` names an open single-digit descriptor from 3 through 9, each policy writes a compact version-1 record on that descriptor: `v`, `policy`, `policyVersion`, `outcome` and `code`. The provisional allow record comes first; the last record determines the policy outcome. With the variable unset or an unusable descriptor, no record is written. Records contain no free text.

`DOFLOW_FAULT_PROFILE` selects `compat-1` (default) or `strict-1`; an unknown value uses `compat-1` and records `fault-profile-unknown`. Under `compat-1` nothing blocks on a fault: an error, an unsupported input or a spawn fault proceeds on every door, and a raw exit 2 without a deny record is an error that proceeds with status 0 on Claude Stop and Kiro PreToolUse. Only a policy's own deny blocks, under both profiles; the one deny left is `mcp-tool-guard` on a blocked pattern. `strict-1` is a person's opt-in that also blocks faults (error, unsupported, `bridge-spawn`, `envelope-encode`) of `pre-implementation-gate` and `mcp-tool-guard`; Stop faults proceed under both profiles. The implement gate and `stop-check` record an advisory and exit 0; they never deny. A missing `policies/contract.sh` is a `bridge-spawn` fault. `DOFLOW_BRIDGE_TRACE`, when set to a file path, appends the bridge's decision object there; it is not model-visible. `DOFLOW_RUN_ID`, `DOFLOW_ATTEMPT` and `DOFLOW_TASK_ID` are reserved inputs for a launcher: in 077 they populate envelope identity or task fields when supplied and are otherwise absent. `DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE` is normalized case-insensitively: unset or empty means `main`, `worker` means `worker`, and any other value emits a warning and is treated as `worker` by session policies; the envelope includes the role only when the supplied value is exactly `main` or `worker`.

Bridge diagnostics use the existing failure store and leave its storage declaration unchanged. Bridge failure-store points include `eval-error:exit-2-unmarked`, `eval-error:exit-<n>`, `eval-error:signal-<NAME>` (not SIGTERM, SIGINT or SIGPIPE), `bridge-spawn`, `bridge-timeout`, `bridge-missing`, `envelope-missing`, `contract-missing`, `envelope-encode` and `fault-profile-unknown`. The Antigravity runner records `runner-event-missing` when its event argument is missing or unknown. A `decision-mismatch` appears in the decision object and bridge trace only; it is a policy contract bug, not a failure-store point (DEC-028). Policies may record `unsupported:envelope-version`, `edit-without-target`, `dependency-missing:jq` and their existing policy-specific points. Diagnostic messages remain empty.

### Migration notes

The legacy native-field decoder has been removed. This is a breaking change for scripts that
pipe native payloads directly to a policy: update them to send a v1 envelope or use the bridge
command above. Input without a v1 envelope is out of step. The gate and MCP policies allow and print
`[<policy>] DoFlow hooks out of step; run: doflow update -t <harness>` on stderr, recording
`unsupported`; Stop and context policies exit silently, with the prompt hook's `{}` no-op. If a
door cannot load the bridge, it allows; only `DOFLOW_FAULT_PROFILE=strict-1` renders the fixed
native out-of-step response (a deny).

When an install or update changes shared hooks, it also refreshes outdated hook front doors for
other installed harnesses at that scope. Install/update plans report companion refreshes,
including harnesses the command did not name; dry-run lists them too. Edited
companion doors refuse the plan unless `-f` is supplied. The single backup includes companions,
and `doflow rollback <id>` restores them with the shared tree. On installs made before 077, Codex
hook scripts have no ledger rows, so an edited script is replaced on the first update,
not refused. That update's backup keeps the edited copy for `doflow rollback <id>`; subsequent
edits are detected and refused. Antigravity shims are always overwritten and have no edited-door
check.

The Antigravity plugin bundle no longer declares hooks because its old entries pointed at files
the Antigravity projection does not install. Its skills, rules, agents and MCP configuration
remain available.

Compared with the 0fcd973 front doors, the shared bridge intentionally changes these cases:

- A missing policy file is an evaluation error: `compat-1` proceeds and records a diagnostic; `strict-1` denies for the gate and MCP policies (DEC-025). Previously the shell's missing-file exit 127 could accidentally deny. A missing `policies/contract.sh` is the same `bridge-spawn` fault, and the gate and MCP policies also print the out-of-step line below (Claude and Codex as PreToolUse advice, Kiro on stderr).
- The bridge captures policy output without a temporary file; an unwritable `TMPDIR` does not turn a gate deny into an allow. Background jobs close inherited bridge descriptors, so they do not hold Stop or SessionStart open. In `strict-1`, missing `jq` still produces the native deny; `compat-1` keeps its allow behavior (FX.1–FX.3).
- Codex `update -t codex -f` re-merges edited `hooks.json`, preserving user entries. Without `-f`, the conflict remains and the message gives a working remedy (DEC-040).
- Under `strict-1`, a blocking evaluation error of the gate or MCP policy renders `[<policy-id>] DoFlow policy error; details: doflow-run failure list` (IC-013/DEC-019), rather than exposing raw policy or runtime stderr as the reason; Kiro PreToolUse and Stop doors use stderr for the fixed reason where their native contract requires it. Under `compat-1` the same error proceeds, renders nothing to the model and records a diagnostic. Codex Stop crash stderr is suppressed (DEC-037).
- Context and advisory policy faults render nothing to the model and record a diagnostic (DEC-022).
- The gate's and `stop-check`'s advisory line reaches the model only on Claude and Codex PreToolUse (`additionalContext`, no permission decision); on Claude and Codex Stop it is a user-facing `systemMessage`. On Copilot, Pi, Antigravity and Kiro Stop it is record-only (the bridge trace), and on Kiro PreToolUse it is a stderr line with no documented path to the model.
- Codex `subagent-audit` now prints nothing for an error outcome; previously it printed `{}` when the policy failed.
- Kiro's agent-config event names (`agentSpawn`, `preToolUse`, `stop`) resolve to the same effects and native responses as their hook-config aliases (`SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `Stop`); the envelope retains the native event name.
- Antigravity's runner decodes its routed command, MCP and edit tools in the shared envelope path. Under RA1, `apply_patch` with `AbsolutePath` now reaches the gate, which advises when design artifacts are absent; the legacy matcher did not inspect it.
- A policy terminated by a signal exits with status `128+n` (128 plus the signal number), such as `139` for SIGSEGV, on the session-context, user-prompt-submit and subagent-audit doors; the gate, MCP and `stop-check` doors proceed with exit 0. Under `strict-1` a door whose fault blocks renders its native deny response instead.
- A missing v1 envelope is the out-of-step state described under Migration notes above (DEC-032,
  DEC-033). A missing `policies/contract.sh` allows with the same printed command but records the
  `bridge-spawn` fault, because no policy can run to record `unsupported`.
- A missing bridge file makes a shell door allow; under `strict-1` it renders its fixed native
  out-of-step response. The Pi extension allows, and blocks with the fixed native out-of-step
  reason only under `strict-1`; its `cli-extension` pre-implementation gate row is
  advisory per the code; that has not been observed live. Project-interactive and user-interactive rows remain unknown; the live record does not
  establish interactive loading or project-trust behavior (DEC-036). The Antigravity runner
  applies this rule for a missing bridge: its gate allows under `compat-1` and denies under
  `strict-1`, and Stop stays silent (DEC-035).
- The bridge preserves a caller-set, non-empty `DOFLOW_AGENT`; otherwise it sets the harness id.

## External worker support by harness

`doflow worker --action run` starts a read-only external worker only on a harness that is eligible: every applicable requirement (installed, runnable, one-shot, structured output, read-only, spawn denial) must be `met` on recorded facts, and a requirement that is `unknown` is a gap (`doflow worker --action describe --harness <id> --json` lists the gaps). A requirement is `met` for a capability only when the registry (`core/registry/harnesses.json`) points at accepted evidence in `core/worker-evidence/<harness>/`, either a passing free check of the CLI or a live run whose report, read-only check and result were accepted.

Output parsers exist for five schemas besides codex's: `claude-stream-json-v1`, `opencode-run-json-v1`, `copilot-json-v1`, `kiro-stream-json-v1` and `pi-json-v1`. Those five are code. Codex's, `codex-exec-json-v1`, is a field map: data in the `structuredOutput.fieldMap` entry of its row in `core/registry/harnesses.json` (row 171), which a package worker can also carry (see [Adding a harness](adding-a-harness.md)). A parser alone does not make a harness eligible. Only a harness with accepted live evidence can be run as a worker, and today that is codex and opencode. Dispatch policy and the fallback ladder order are unchanged. The agentic bench config (`bench/agentic/config.json`) pins `harness.pin: codex`, so `bench agentic execute` keeps selecting codex now that opencode also qualifies; with the pin null it stops with `harness-ambiguous`.

| Harness | Worker support | What the committed record shows |
|---|---|---|
| codex | Supported, unchanged | Two live runs (read-only and edit) on 2026-10-18 and free checks through 2026-10-24, in `core/worker-evidence/codex/`. |
| opencode | Supported for `runnableProbe`, `oneShot`, `structuredOutput`, `readOnly` and `spawnDenial` | 2 live runs on 2026-11-07, opencode v2.2.5, on the model `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free` (pinned through `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`; the stream reports cost 0 and does not name the model). Both ended `success` with a clean worktree and unchanged HEAD and refs. One asked for a file write: the worker replied it had no write tool and no file appeared. The other asked for a hand-off to a sub-agent: the worker replied it had no such tool and the stream held no `task` or `subagent` call. The records hold only the report status, the clean worktree check and the prompt hash, and the streams show the model declining, not a tool call being blocked, so they do not prove a write or a spawn would be refused. The mechanism is the shipped agent's deny permissions: `opencode debug agents` in a scratch HOME on 2026-11-07 (opencode v2.2.5) resolved `edit`, `shell` and `subagent` to deny for `doflow-worker-ro`; that output is not stored as a record. Limits: one model, one prompt per run, and the spawn run showed an absent tool, not a refused spawn. Evidence: `core/worker-evidence/opencode/`. `isolatedEdit` and `model` stay `unknown`. |
| claude | Unknown | 1 live run, 2026-11-07 (claude 2.1.311), ended with report status `failure` and exit 1. The captured stream holds a `rate_limit_event` of type `seven_day` with status `rejected`, so the account's weekly usage limit stopped the run. The parser is merged and tested on that capture, but no run reached a model answer. |
| copilot | Supported for `runnableProbe`, `oneShot`, `structuredOutput`, `readOnly` and `spawnDenial` | 2 live runs on 2026-11-07, Copilot CLI 1.0.105, on models that copilot's auto mode picked (`mai-code-1.1-flash` and `gpt-6-luna`). Both ended `success` with a clean worktree and unchanged HEAD and refs. In the write run the stream's tool starts were only `view` and `grep`, and the model's closing line still claimed it had created the file, so the model's own claims are not evidence here; the tree and the tool starts are. In the spawn run no tool was called. The four user MCP servers still connect in the stream, no MCP tool was called, and whether the allowlist hides them is not checked. An earlier run on the permission-only recipe had `bash` and `apply_patch` refused and ended `denied`, which showed those tools were visible to the model; the recipe now uses a tool allowlist. |
| pi | Supported for `runnableProbe`, `oneShot`, `structuredOutput`, `readOnly` and `spawnDenial` | 2 live runs on 2026-11-07, pi 1.5.0, on the model `openai/gpt-6.1-terra` (the stream reports it; it was added with a `pi` wrapper on PATH, because a manual worker run does not take a `workers.models` binding). Both ended `success` with a clean worktree and unchanged HEAD and refs. In the write run the stream's tool starts were `ls` and `find` only, and the worker said it had no file-writing or shell tool; in the spawn run no tool was called and it said it had no sub-agent tool. The streams show the tools called, not a refused call, so the runs do not by themselves prove that a write or a spawn would be refused. The extensions and MCP sources the user has installed in pi were switched off by the recipe and not exercised. |
| kiro | Supported for `runnableProbe`, `oneShot`, `structuredOutput`, `readOnly` and `spawnDenial` | 2 live runs on 2026-11-07, kiro-cli 3.1.0, on the model `claude-haiku-5.5` (pinned in the scratch project's `.kiro/settings/cli.json`; the stream's `currentValue` reports it). Both ended `success` with a clean worktree and unchanged HEAD and refs. One asked for a file write and the worker replied that its tools only read and search files; the other asked for a sub-agent hand-off and it replied it had no sub-agent tool. The streams show the model declining, not a tool call being blocked, so the runs do not by themselves prove that a write or a spawn would be refused. An earlier run on the legacy agent file was graded `violated` because kiro-cli rewrote that file itself; the agent no longer carries the key that caused it. |
| antigravity | Excluded | 0 live runs. `readOnly` and `spawnDenial` cannot be proved on agy 1.5.0 (follow-up FU-h1pcpq). It has no worker parser and no recipe change from this work; every worker capability stays `unknown`. |

**Precondition for opencode.** The opencode recipe runs `run --agent doflow-worker-ro`, an agent that denies `edit`, `bash`, `write` and `task`. Its permission `*` stays `allow`, so MCP and webfetch tools are not denied: read-only holds for file edits, shell commands and sub-agents, not for every tool the CLI can load. The agent ships with DoFlow (asset `opencode.worker-ro-agent`) and is written by `doflow install` for that harness. A project that has not run `doflow install` for opencode has no such agent, and the run fails with an unknown-agent error. That outcome is a report `failure` or a refusal, never a pass.

**Precondition for kiro.** The kiro recipe runs `chat --no-interactive --agent doflow-worker-ro --trust-tools=read`. The agent lists only the `read` tools and allows `fs_read`, so the model has no write, shell or sub-agent tool; MCP tools and other tools were not exercised. The agent ships with DoFlow (`core/harnesses/kiro/agents/doflow-worker-ro.json`) and `doflow install` for kiro writes it. A project without it has no such agent, and the run fails with an unknown-agent error, never a pass. Keep the agent in the `permissions.rules` form: kiro-cli 3.1.0 rewrites a file that still carries the legacy `allowedTools` key, which a read-only check reads as a violation.

**Precondition for copilot.** The copilot recipe runs `--available-tools view glob grep --deny-tool write --deny-tool shell --excluded-tools task`. `--available-tools` limits the model to the listed tools (`view` reads, `glob` and `grep` search); the deny rules stay as a second layer. No companion agent is needed. The tool names are the ones copilot 1.0.105 reports in its stream, and a newer copilot that renames them would leave the model with fewer tools, not more. On 2026-11-07 `COPILOT_MODEL` did not take effect in the live runs (the stream shows the auto-picked model), and a `--model` id that is not in the account's catalogue was refused, so a live run takes the auto-picked model unless a valid id is bound.

**Precondition for pi.** The pi recipe runs `--tools read,grep,find,ls --no-extensions --no-mcp --mode json -p --no-session --thinking off --no-skills --no-context-files --offline`. `--tools` is an allowlist of tool names, so the model has no write, edit or shell tool, and `--no-extensions` and `--no-mcp` drop the extension and MCP tool sources (including the packages the user installed in pi). Pi takes its model from its own settings (the first of `enabledModels` unless `--model` is given), so a run uses the user's pi default; the recipe has a supported `--model` entry for a bound model.

**Model flag.** `model` is `supported` for codex (`-m`) and for claude, copilot, pi, opencode, kiro and antigravity (`--model <id>`), each from a `--help` line recorded by the free `help-model` check on 2026-11-07. A model bound in `workers.models` for one of these harnesses is therefore passed with `--model`. The check shows the flag exists, not that a given id is accepted; the id is passed through. Antigravity is `supported` too (`--model <id>`, from the `--help` line on stderr that its 2026-11-07 cli-check records: the check reads both streams).

**Provider and effort.** `provider` and `effort` are descriptor entries beside `model` (see [Adding a harness](adding-a-harness.md)); a part with no recorded evidence is `unknown` with the reason, and `bind` and a prepared run refuse it. Today `provider` is `supported` on opencode and pi (joined to the model id with `/`: `<provider>/<model>`) and `unknown` elsewhere; `effort` is `supported` on claude (`--effort <level>`: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`, from the help line recorded 2026-11-16) and on pi (`--thinking <level>`: `off`, `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`), and `unknown` on codex, opencode, copilot, kiro and antigravity. The records behind the supported entries carry `handAdded`: their matched lines were copied from an earlier record or a capture, and no CLI was run for them. That a CLI accepts a given provider or level is not checked; the value is passed through. Pi may run a lower level than bound when the model cannot run it, and every display of a pi effort says so. With a bound pi effort the one-shot argv carries `--thinking <level>` in place of the fixed `--thinking off`; that argv has not been verified on a live run (the live-run records hold the argv without a bound effort). With no effort bound the argv is the one above.

**Known limit.** The claude, opencode, copilot, kiro and pi recipes pass the prompt as one argv element and add no `--` end-of-options separator. The launcher puts its launch-emission text before the brief, so in practice a prompt does not start with `-`. If a prompt did start with `-`, the CLI might read it as a flag; no test or live run checks that.

Sources: `core/worker-evidence/*/`, `core/registry/harnesses.json`.

## Started sessions

Row 165. DoFlow starts a main session: a harness CLI run headless in a checkout of a project, with a first text, supervised like a worker run but not tied to a task. It is started by `doflow worker --action start-session`, or from the Console (see below). The harness's own permission settings apply: DoFlow adds no flag that widens what the session may do. The deny list is data with one matcher (`src/registry/widening-flags.ts`): for each CLI the widening flags its own `--help` lists, recorded as `core/worker-evidence/<harness>/2026-11-16-cli-check-widening-flags.json` (check `widening-flags`; kiro's two trust flags come from its 2026-11-07 record, as `kiro-cli` is never run), plus the owner's list (`--full-auto`, the `--dangerously*`, `--yolo*`, `--allow-all*` and `--add-dir*` families, and codex's `sandbox_mode=danger-full-access`, `approval_policy=never` and `sandbox_workspace_write` config keys). The registry validator refuses a `oneShot.argv` or `mainSession.argv`, of a built-in or a package worker, that holds one in any form a CLI accepts: `--flag`, `--flag=value`, `--flag value`, a short form (`-s danger-full-access`, `-sdanger-full-access`; short forms count only for their own CLI), and a config override (`-c key=value`, `--config key=value`, matched by key, also as a dotted segment, and value, quotes stripped). A long flag of any CLI counts for every CLI. The workspace-trust flags (`--trust-folder`, `--trust-workspace`, `--trust-tools`) are refused in a main session only, since copilot's and kiro's one-shot worker recipes carry them. A worker block may add its own `wideningFlags` (`[{flag, short?, values?} | {prefix} | {config, values?}]`, at most 32). The launcher runs the same check on the final argv, the model part spliced in, so a model id cannot add one: such a run ends not started with `widening-flag`. An approval prompt that nobody can answer makes the session wait or exit.

**Command.** `doflow worker --action start-session --project-key <16 hex> --worktree-key <16 hex> --harness <id> [--model <id>] [--provider <name>] [--effort <level>] --entry do|none --request-id <id> --stdin [--via console|device.d-<12 hex>] [--json]`. `--provider` and `--effort` need `--model` (else exit 2 `usage`) and follow the same patterns as `models --action bind`; the model, provider and effort are one triple that `start-options` must offer, else the start is refused `model-not-offered`. The first text is read from stdin; `--stdin` is required. `--request-id` is 22 base64url characters beginning with a letter or digit (the CLI refuses a flag value that begins with `-`); it is the retry key. `--via` is the relay label recorded as `startedBy` (default `manual`). `--project-key` must be the key of the project the command runs in, and `--worktree-key` the key of one of its checkouts, as `fleet --action projects` and `worker --action start-options` list them. `--entry do` sends the fixed line `Use the DoFlow do skill for this request:`, a blank line, then the text; `--entry none` sends the text alone. The flags belong to `start-session` only (`--project-key` also to `start-options`); any other action given one is exit 2 `usage`.

**The first text.** Carriage returns become line feeds and control characters other than tab and line feed, and the bidirectional controls, are removed; leading whitespace is kept. It is refused when it is empty (`prompt-empty`), holds NUL (`prompt-nul`), is over 8000 code points or 32768 bytes (`prompt-too-long`), starts with `-` when the entry is `none` or the harness takes the prompt as an argument (`prompt-leading-dash`), or starts with `@` when the harness takes it as an argument (`prompt-leading-at`). What is sent is stored in the `session-prompts` store, `.doflow/state/session-prompts/<runId>.prompt.txt`, mode 0600; the run record keeps its SHA-256 and byte count, never the text. The store keeps the newest 200 files and none older than 30 days, and a prompt of a run that has not ended is never removed. The text is not in any reply, error or Console log.

**Check order (design IC-001).** Each check refuses before anything is written. Exit 0 for a start or a replay, 1 for a refusal `{ok: false, code, message, current?}`, 2 for a malformed or missing flag (`usage`).

| # | Check | Refusal id (`current`) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN` is set in the environment: a started session cannot start another | `nested-start` (`{runId}`, as the environment gives it) |
| 2 | every flag by its pattern (`--harness` only given: an id the worker catalogue does not list is check 6's `not-listed`); `--stdin` given | exit 2 `usage` |
| 3 | the command runs in the project `--project-key` names, and the index lists it | `project-mismatch`, `project-not-indexed` |
|   | the request id is already in the store: the run it started is returned with `replayed: true` and nothing is started, whatever has changed since | |
| 4 | `--worktree-key` names a checkout of the project (a DoFlow sandbox does not count) | `unknown-worktree`, `worktree-gone` |
| 5 | the first text, as above | `prompt-empty`, `prompt-nul`, `prompt-too-long`, `prompt-leading-dash`, `prompt-leading-at`, and for a harness with a supported control channel `command-not-allowed` |
| 6 | the harness can start here (below) | `harness-not-startable` (`{harness, reason}`) |
| 7 | the model is one the options offer for the harness; no `--model` is the harness default | `model-not-offered` (`{harness, models}`) |
| 8 | no dispatch of this project is running: a driver with no end whose process is seen alive or cannot be checked, or a dispatch not ended in a main-session mode, or a store that cannot be read, or a dispatch directory that exists but cannot be listed (`{slug: null, reason: "dispatch-unreadable", detail}`; only a missing directory means no dispatch) | `dispatch-running` (`{slug, ...}`) |
| 9 | under the run store's lock, the limits below | `checkout-busy` (`{runId}`), `checkout-busy-unknown` (`{runId}`: a run there whose liveness cannot be read; the message names the cancel that clears it), `actor-limit`, `machine-limit` (`{open, max}`), `start-rate-limited` (`{started, max, retryAfterSeconds}`) |

**Limits.** One open started session per checkout, 2 per actor (the `--via` label) and 4 on the machine; the core also limits an actor to 6 starts per hour (`start-rate-limited`, row 170: counted from every started session of that actor created in the last hour, ended or not, in this project's store under the lock and in every other indexed project's store read just before it; `retryAfterSeconds` is the whole seconds until the oldest of them leaves the hour). A run is open until it ends, and it stops counting only on positive evidence that it is dead: every process it records (supervisor and worker, each by pid and start time) is seen gone, and when it records fewer than both, none of this user's processes names the run in its command line or environment (the supervisor's arguments carry the run id, the harness's environment `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN`; on macOS the environment of a platform binary is not shown, so such a process is read from its command line alone). A run with neither process recorded counts as open for 60 seconds after it was created, while its spawn is in flight. A run whose liveness cannot be read counts as open, and in its own checkout a start is refused `checkout-busy-unknown`; `worker --action cancel --operator --slug started-sessions --worker-run <id> --via console --reason ended` clears it. The per-checkout limit is exact under the lock. The actor and machine counts are the project's own store under the lock plus every other indexed project's store read just before it, so two starts from the command line in different projects at the same moment can both pass and exceed the limit by one; the owner accepted this race. Starts that come through one Console are queued one at a time, so they cannot. A started session's timeout is 3600 seconds; no flag changes it.

**Reply.** `{ok: true, replayed, run}`, where `run` is `{runId, kind: "main-session", phase, harness, model, modelSource, worktreeKey, startedBy, createdAt, agentKey, layers, files: {out, err}}` and, once the run has ended, `end`. `modelSource` is `orchestrator`, `frontier`, `standard`, `light` (a bound tier model), `observed` (the harness's own model list) or `default`. The command waits up to 10 seconds for the record to leave `starting`, so `phase` may still be `starting`; a prompt that cannot be written ends the run at once. `layers` is the page address `/layers/<projectKey>/agent/<agentKey>` and `files` are the file keys of the two output logs, which the Console follows. The text form prints `started <runId> <phase>`, `replayed <runId> <phase>` or `refused <code>`.

**Stop, status, output.** The run is in the reserved run store `.doflow/state/worker-runs/started-sessions.json`; `worker --action run` and `dispatch` refuse that slug with `slug-reserved`. `worker --action status --slug started-sessions --worker-run <id>` shows `kind`, `startedBy`, `entry` and `model` and no task attempt, and `worker --action cancel --operator --slug started-sessions --worker-run <id> --via <label> --reason <text>` stops it (no task attempt is read or written). On a session with a control channel, `cancel` ends the session; `worker --action stop` ends only the turn in progress ([Talking to a started session](#talking-to-a-started-session)). Output is masked into `.doflow/state/worker-output/<runId>.out.log` and `.err.log`, as for a worker.

**`worker --action start-options --project-key <16 hex> [--json]`** reads what the start form offers and writes nothing. It answers `{project: {projectKey, label, root: null}, checkouts, harnesses, limits, open: {machine}, dispatch: {running, slug?, reason?}, promptMax: {codePoints, bytes}, entries, entryLine}` or `project-mismatch` / `project-not-indexed`. Each checkout is `{worktreeKey, label, branch, main, busy, observedSession}` (at most 64; `busy` is `{runId}` while a started session is open there). `dispatch.slug` is null, with `reason: "dispatch-unreadable"`, when the dispatch records cannot be read. Each harness is `{harness, startable, reason, words, warnings, login, version, liveVerified, channel, models}`, in the worker catalogue's order: the built-in harnesses, then every active package worker under its qualified id (`<package>.<local>`), held to the same checks. A harness is startable when its `mainSession` descriptor is `supported` with evidence, its binary is found, and its login answer is not `logged-out`; the shape is checked first, so a harness DoFlow cannot start is never run, and no probe is run. `reason` is `not-listed`, `no-main-session-shape`, `not-installed` or `logged-out`. `login` is the harness's login answer ([Login state](#login-state)), read from the `harness-login` store; it is null until the harness has been found installed. `warnings` are `runnable-not-checked` (no fresh `runnable` probe), `login-expired` (the answer is `expired`), `login-unknown` (the answer is `unknown`: no recipe, never checked, stale, or the check gave no answer), `prompt-in-argv` (the harness takes the prompt as an argument and has no supported control channel) and `channel-not-verified-live` (its channel is supported and no live-run record backs it). `channel` is the harness's control channel as the form needs it, `{state, reason, liveVerified, unverified}` ([Talking to a started session](#talking-to-a-started-session)): `state` is `supported`, `unsupported` or `unknown` (a descriptor with no `controlChannel` entry reads `unknown`, never `unsupported`), `reason` the descriptor's sentence (null when supported), `liveVerified` true only when the channel's own `evidence` cites a `live-run` record of that harness, and `unverified` the descriptor's phrases for a supported channel. `liveVerified` is true only when the harness's `mainSession.evidence` cites a `live-run` record of that harness; a shape resting on `--help` checks alone (today claude, codex, opencode and pi), an unreadable record and a package worker are false, and the status stays `supported`: the start form says "not yet verified on a live run" beside such a harness. `models` lists the tier-bound choices, then the harness's observed models, then the default, at most 50, and only the default when the harness has no supported `model` entry. An entry is `{model, provider, effort, source, check, refused?}`: the binding cell of one triple (`check` is `{model, effort}` as `models --action list` shows it, plus `effortMayLower: true` where it applies) with its `source` (the tier name for a bound tier, `observed` for a listed model, `default`). The same model under another provider or effort is another entry. A bound entry whose provider or effort the harness cannot take (the descriptor entry is not `supported`, or the effort is outside its levels) is listed with `refused` set to the reason (`provider:unsupported`, `effort:unsupported` or `effort:unknown-level`) and cannot start. The default is `{model: null, provider: null, effort: null, source: "default", check: null}`. The field that was `id` before row 169 is `model`; the old name is not read.

**Harness descriptor `worker.mainSession`** (`core/registry/harnesses.json`). Keys: `status` (`supported` or `unknown`), `evidence` (record paths under `core/worker-evidence/`), `verification`, `verifiedOn`, `argv`, `promptVia` (`stdin` or `argv`, as in `oneShot`) and `parser` (a code parser id, the `fieldMap.id` of the same worker's `structuredOutput`, or `null`: the end state then comes from the exit code alone); an unsupported entry has `reason` instead of `argv`. `argv` may hold `{prompt}` (a whole element, exactly once when `promptVia` is `argv`) and `{lastMessageFile}` (at most once); `{model}` stays in `model.argv`, which is spliced in before the first of `-`, `--` or `{prompt}` (else at the end) when a model is chosen.

| Harness | `mainSession` | Recipe (prompt via) | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| claude | supported | `-p --output-format text` (stdin), no parser | `claude --help` 2.1.311 lists `-p, --print` and `--output-format`; that `-p` reads stdin is read from its usage line, not stated outright |
| codex | supported | `exec --json -o <last message file> -` (stdin), `codex-exec-json-v1` | `codex exec --help` 0.164.0: `--json`, `-o, --output-last-message`, `-` reads stdin |
| opencode | supported | `run --format json <prompt>` (argv), `opencode-run-json-v1`; no `--` | `opencode run --help` v2.2.7; `--` is not documented there, so a text starting `-` is refused |
| pi | supported | `-p --mode json --no-approve -- <prompt>` (argv), `pi-json-v1`; with its control channel supported (below) a started session runs `--mode rpc --no-approve` and takes the prompt as its first record on stdin instead | `pi --help` 1.5.2 documents `--` and `--no-approve`; a text starting `@` is read as a file when it is an argument, so it is refused there, and is no longer refused once the prompt goes through the channel |
| copilot | unknown | none | its non-interactive prompt mode needs `--allow-all-tools`, which widens what the session may do; no widening flag is ever used, so copilot is not started |
| kiro | unknown | none | no probe was made: `kiro-cli` is out of quota and is never executed, so no headless invocation is recorded |
| antigravity | unknown | none | no recorded check shows a flag that sends a prompt and exits |

All four supported entries rest on `--help` text recorded on 2026-11-15 (pi's on 2026-11-16, pi 1.5.2); the registry says of each that the combination is untested until a live run, and no started session has been run live against any of them.

**Pi worker facts (row 168, pi 1.5.2).** The `pi-json-v1` parser reads usage and cost from the stream's assistant `message_end` events: Pi's docs give each assistant message the usage of its own model call (`message_update`'s top-level `usage` is the cumulative figure for that one response, and is not read), so the report sums over the stream's assistant messages. A message's tokens are `usage.input + usage.output` (as the claude, opencode and copilot parsers count them); Pi's `totalTokens` also adds cache reads and writes and is not read. A message whose `input` and `output` are not both non-negative safe integers has no count, and an all-zero usage is read as not reported, since Pi starts every usage at zero. Tokens are all or nothing: if any assistant `message_end` has no count, the run's tokens and `usage` are `null`, except that a message with `stopReason` `error` or `aborted` and no count (Pi's synthetic failure message) is skipped. The cost is `usage.cost.total` on its own field by the same rule: summed only when every counted message carries a finite non-negative cost, otherwise `null`, and a cost on a message with no count is never added. A token total beyond a safe integer, or a cost sum that is not finite, is `null`. An assistant `message_end` that gave no count counts in `ignoredLines`. Compaction calls and a tool's nested model work are outside the assistant messages and not counted. A last assistant message with `stopReason: length` (the answer was cut off at the output token limit) is a `failure` with reason `length`, and the partial text is the summary; it is no longer `unparsed`. A length stop followed by a tool result as the last message is reported as `unparsed`. The main-session recipe passes `--no-approve`, so a run that cannot answer Pi's trust prompt loads no unapproved project code; the approving `--approve, -a` is refused by the widening guard. The one-shot recipe does not pass it yet: the two 2026-11-07 live-run records fix that recipe's argv, guard G41.5 holds the registry to them, and re-recording them needs a model call. `resume` and `persistentSession` are `unsupported` for the headless `pi -p --mode json` mode DoFlow drives (the session flags exist but no recipe drives them), read from help and installed docs with no live run. Pi's RPC mode, which keeps a process and takes `prompt` records with `streamingBehavior` `followUp` or `steer` and an `abort` record, is Pi's `controlChannel`: `supported` on capture C-1 (`core/worker-evidence/pi/2026-11-17-cli-check-control-channel.json`: `pi --version` and `pi --help` lines and lines of Pi's own docs and source, read with `--docs-root`; pi was never run in rpc mode) and not verified on a live run. The entry's `unverified` list says what only a live run can show: "turn events in rpc mode", "steer taken while working" and "exit after stdin closes". Its argv is `--mode rpc --no-approve`, so the trust rule above holds for the channel too, and its `commandPrefix` is `/`, since Pi runs a message that starts with `/` as an extension command or a template. Pi's login is read by its `loginProbe` recipe (see [Login state](#login-state)): for each provider `pi --list-models --offline` shows (those have usable credentials; at most 8) it runs `pi auth check --provider <p> --json --no-refresh`, and exit 0 reads logged in, exit 1 with `status: not_ready` reads logged out and exit 2 reads `unknown` (`invalid`), never opening `~/.pi/agent/auth.json`. Exit 1 with `reason: provider_not_found` for a provider the listing named (Pi's auth command loads no extensions) is `unknown` (`provider-not-found`), as are a timeout, a signal and a missing exit code. A `pi` whose `check` command is missing exits 1 with no `not_ready` status and reads `unknown`, never logged out. The harness is logged out only when every listed provider was checked and every one answered not ready; when more than 8 are listed the answer is `unknown` (`providers-partial`) unless a provider is logged in, and mode and billing stay unknown unless every listed provider was checked. Limit: with `--no-refresh` an expired OAuth login still reads as logged in, so the recipe's `expiry` is `not-seen`; its expiry signs (`OAuth refresh failed for {provider}`, `OAuth refresh returned a token that expires too soon for {provider}`) come from Pi 1.5.2's source (basis `source`) and have not been seen on a live run.

**What the session gets.** The harness runs in the checkout (`--worktree-key`), with the caller's environment minus the identity variables (`DOFLOW_RUN_ID`, `DOFLOW_LINEAGE_ID`, `DOFLOW_ATTEMPT`, `DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE`, `DOFLOW_WORKTREE`), `DOFLOW_WORKER_RUN` and the primary declaration (`DOFLOW_PRIMARY_HARNESS`, `DOFLOW_PRIMARY_MODEL`), plus `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN=<runId>`. No context emission is prepended and no grant is made. `opencode` gets the text as a command-line argument, so it is visible in the process list while the session runs. `pi` gets it as the first record on its stdin, through its control channel, and not in argv.

**One node in the tree.** When the harness's `SessionStart` hook runs in the started session, the `session-context` hook records `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN` as `started_run` in that session's `git-context.json`, only when it matches `w-<12 hex>`. `fleet --action tree` joins the run and that hook session on the exact run id (never by time or checkout; when several sessions name one run the earliest captured joins and the others stay their own nodes) and shows one node of kind `started-session` (see the `fleet --action tree` row). A session that never reports through the hook stays one `started-session` node with `observed.state` `not-observed`.

**Console.** `GET /api/start` (the projects a session can start in: `{v, projects: [{projectKey, label, current}], truncated}`, no path), `GET /api/start/options?projectKey=<16 hex>` (the options above, closed fields, `root` never sent; 400 `usage`, 404 `project-not-found`, 501 `core-lacks-verb` on a core without `start-options`, 502 `core-contract-mismatch`) and `POST /api/actions/start-session` (body `{v: 1, requestId, projectKey, worktreeKey, harness, model, provider, effort, entry, prompt}`, at most 40 KiB, `model`, `provider` and `effort` each required and nullable; the request id is 22 base64url characters beginning with a letter or digit, else 400 `usage`). The two reads and the action are for the loopback `local` session and a `control` device over the native Bearer transport; a `view` device is `403 phone-scope`. A start answers 200 `{v, action, requestId, outcome, replayed, by, result: {replayed, run}}` or an error with the core's refusal id (400 `unknown-worktree`, `worktree-gone`, `prompt-*`, `command-not-allowed`, `model-not-offered`; 409 `checkout-busy`, `checkout-busy-unknown`, `dispatch-running`, `harness-not-startable`, `nested-start` (a Console run inside a started session passes that session's `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN` to the core for this action only; every other `DOFLOW_*` variable but its pass-throughs is stripped); 429 `actor-limit`, `machine-limit`, and `start-rate-limited` (the core's limit of 6 starts per actor per hour; the Console holds no start limit of its own and maps the core's refusal, with `Retry-After` from its `retryAfterSeconds`); 404 `project-not-found`; 501 `core-lacks-action`). The full list, the limits and the log are in [`console/README.md`](https://github.com/khoavu882/do-flow/blob/develop/console/README.md). The start form words each harness's login from `login` (see [Login state](#login-state)).

### Talking to a started session

Row 170. A started session whose harness declares a supported control channel (`worker.controlChannel`, [below](#the-control-channel-descriptor)) keeps its stdin open, and three verbs put a message to it while it runs; the Console calls the same three. Today only Pi declares one. It rests on help and docs lines recorded on capture C-1 (pi 1.5.2) and is not verified on a live run. Every other harness is `unknown` with its reason: a session on one runs its one prompt as in row 165, its turn reads `not observed`, and a message to it is refused `no-channel`. A harness with a supported channel always starts through it; there is no per-start opt-out.

**Commands.**

```bash
doflow worker --action send  --project-key <16 hex> --worker-run <w-12 hex> --request-id <id> [--via console|device.d-<12 hex>] --stdin [--json]
doflow worker --action steer --project-key <16 hex> --worker-run <w-12 hex> --request-id <id> [--via console|device.d-<12 hex>] --stdin [--json]
doflow worker --action stop  --project-key <16 hex> --worker-run <w-12 hex> --request-id <id> [--via console|device.d-<12 hex>] [--json]
```

`send` queues a follow-up: a session that is waiting takes it as its next turn. `steer` redirects a turn in progress. `stop` ends the turn in progress and leaves the session open and waiting; `worker --action cancel` is what ends the session. The text of `send` and `steer` is read from stdin and `--stdin` is required; `stop` takes none and `--stdin` with it is exit 2 `usage`. The text is framed as read: the verb removes and rewrites nothing. `--project-key` must be the key of the project the command runs in. `--worker-run` is the run id, `w-` and 12 lowercase hex characters. `--request-id` is 22 base64url characters beginning with a letter or digit, the retry key, as for `start-session`. `--via` is the relay label recorded as the message's actor, `by` (default `manual`), and the actor of the limits below. Any control actor may send to any open started session of the project, as any may cancel one.

**Check order (design IC-001).** Each check refuses before anything is written.

| # | Check | Refusal id (`current`) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN` is set (a started session cannot send; this is answered before any flag is read); the caller is inside a workflow run or a worker session | `nested-send` (`{runId}`), `sender-in-run` (`{reason}`) |
| 2 | every flag by its pattern; `--stdin` given for `send` and `steer` | exit 2 `usage` |
| 3 | the command runs in the project `--project-key` names, and the index lists it | `project-mismatch`, `project-not-indexed` |
| 4 | the run | `unknown-run`, `not-a-session`, `run-ended` (each `{runId}`) |
| 5 | the request id is already in the run's ledger, or its file still waits in the spool: the recorded answer is returned with `replayed: true` (a file still waiting reads `pending`) and nothing is written | |
| 6 | the session has a channel | `started-without-channel` (a record older than row 170), `no-channel` (the message gives the descriptor's reason, or says the channel did not open) |
| 7 | the text, for `send` and `steer`: the descriptor has a steer record (for `steer`), the text is not blank, it is within the caps below and it does not start with the descriptor's `commandPrefix` | `no-steer`, `prompt-empty`, `prompt-too-long`, `command-not-allowed` |
| 8 | the channel is open and its supervisor is not seen gone | `channel-closed` (`{runId, reason}`: `supervisor-gone` or the close reason; the message names the cancel that ends the session) |
| 9 | `steer` and `stop` while the turn is observed `waiting` (both are allowed while it is `working` or not observed; `send` is always allowed) | `not-working` (`{turn}`) |
| 10 | fewer than 4 messages wait in the spool (never for `stop`) | `channel-busy` |
| 11 | the actor's limits below | `actor-rate-limited` (`{retryAfterSeconds}`) |

The verb then writes one spool file, its only write, and reads the run record every 25 ms for at most 2 seconds, waiting for the supervisor's ledger entry with its request id; for a record the harness answers by id, it waits up to 500 ms more, inside the same 2 seconds, for that answer. A spool that is missing or unsafe is `channel-closed` (`reason` is the spool's error code), and a file over 64 KiB is `prompt-too-long`.

**Reply.** `{ok: true, replayed, message: {seq, kind, state, answer, at, bytes}, turn, channel}`. `kind` is `follow-up`, `steer` or `stop`. `state` is `written` (the supervisor wrote the record to the harness's stdin; `seq` is its ledger number) or `pending` (the 2 seconds passed and the file still waits; `seq` is null, and the same call again with the same request id gives the final state). `answer` is `accepted` or `refused` when the descriptor declares an `answer` and the harness has answered the record's id, else null. `bytes` is the UTF-8 size of the text (0 for a stop), on every reply, a `pending` one included. `turn` is `{observed: true, state: "working" | "waiting", since, seq}` or `{observed: false, reason, since}`, and `channel` is `open` or `closed`. A refusal is `{ok: false, code, message, current?}` with `current` holding the `runId`, `turn`, `reason` or `retryAfterSeconds` the table names. A message the supervisor refused or could not deliver comes back as `ok: false` with the entry's reason as the code: `not-working`, `actor-rate-limited`, `no-steer`, `prompt-empty`, `prompt-too-long` and `command-not-allowed` when it re-checked and refused (`channel-busy` is the verb's alone); `stdin-stalled` (the record did not reach the pipe within 10 seconds), `write-failed` (the pipe failed, and the channel closes `write-failed`) and `channel-closed` (the channel closed before the message was taken) when it failed. A replay of a refused or failed message answers the same refusal. A retry that passed check 5 while its first message was still being written is taken and skipped by the supervisor, never written twice, and its reply is the first message's entry (`replayed` reads `false` on that one reply; a later call with the request id reads `true`). Exit 0 for `ok: true`, a replay and a `pending` reply included; 1 for `ok: false`; 2 for a malformed or missing flag (`usage`). The text form prints `<kind> <state>` with `(<answer>)` when there is one, `replayed ` first for a replay, or `refused <code>`.

**Limits (`CHANNEL_LIMITS` in `src/runtime/worker/channel.ts`).** The actor is the `--via` label, which is the caller's own assertion: the Console sets it from the authenticated device, while a terminal caller chooses it, so a caller that picks another label gets that label's own windows. The limits below count per label, a guard against a runaway sender and not an identity check. Per actor and per session: 6 messages a minute and 60 an hour (`send` and `steer` together), and 6 stops a minute, counted from the messages that were written. The count is kept apart from the 128-entry ledger, so a busy session never under-counts: the supervisor keeps each actor's stamps (times only, no text) for an hour of messages and a minute of stops, at most 60 and 6, in the run record's `channel.stamps`, which the verb reads for its own check. At most 4 messages wait in a session's spool; the verb refuses a fifth as `channel-busy`, and a stop is never refused for that. Text: at most 8000 code points and 32768 bytes (the first prompt's caps), checked on the person's text; the framed record at most 65536 bytes; a spool file at most 64 KiB. The verb checks first and the supervisor checks again, exactly, so a message that raced past the verb is refused with the same code; the one exception is `channel-busy`, which only the verb refuses (the supervisor takes the spool in order, so at its admission the messages queued ahead of one and not yet written number 0). The limits are the core's alone for a request that reaches the core: the Console shows what the core answers and adds none to it. A request the Console refuses before it reaches the core (scope, transport, usage, request-reused) counts against the actor's own windows there, as for every action, since nothing else limits it.

**The text is data.** It is read from stdin and written to one spool file, and nowhere else: not in argv, not through a shell, and in no reply, ledger entry, run record, log line or error.
