The core keeps who, when, which session, the kind and the byte count. The Console's action log keeps `textBytes`, the UTF-8 size of the text. The harness's reply is in the session's masked output log. A text that starts, after leading whitespace, with the descriptor's `commandPrefix` is refused `command-not-allowed`, by the verb and again by the supervisor, and the first prompt of `start-session` is refused the same way before anything is written; DoFlow does not escape or rewrite a text to get it past the harness.

**What a start does with a channel.** `start-session` on a harness whose channel is supported records `recipe.promptVia: "channel"` and copies `descriptor.controlChannel` into the run record. The supervisor launches the channel's argv (`controlChannel.argv`, the model part spliced at the end, the widening and secret-flag checks run again on the final argv) with stdin a pipe it keeps open, writes the stored first prompt as the first follow-up record (ledger entry 1, kind `first`, `by` the starter, which counts toward no limit), then takes messages from the spool.

**The run record's `channel`.** Written by the supervisor only, in `started-sessions.json`: `{v: 1, state: "opening" | "open" | "closed", closedReason, turn, sent: {count, bytes, lastAt}, ledger, stamps}`. `stamps` holds, per actor, the times of its written messages for one hour (at most 60) and of its stops for one minute (at most 6), numbers only: it is what the rate check counts, apart from the ledger. The ledger keeps the newest 128 entries `{seq, kind: "first" | "follow-up" | "steer" | "stop", by, requestId, at, bytes, outcome: "written" | "refused" | "failed", reason, answer}`. `closedReason` is `harness-exited`, `unused-timeout`, `cancelled`, `timeout`, `write-failed`, `first-refused` (the first prompt was refused when framed) or `not-started`. The supervisor takes spool files by a watch and a 100 ms poll, oldest name first and at most 16 at a time, and deletes each before it acts on it; a record that does not reach the pipe within 10 seconds is `failed`, `stdin-stalled`. A turn that has waited 30 minutes with no message closes stdin, `closedReason` `unused-timeout`; the 3600 second timeout of a started session still applies. When the harness exits, every message still in the spool is logged `failed`, `channel-closed`, and the spool is removed.

**The turn.** It comes only from the channel's own events on the harness's stdout: a line that meets every test of `turn.working` reads `working`, one that meets `turn.settled` reads `waiting`, and nothing else sets it; a message just written is not read as work. Until one matches, the turn is `{observed: false, reason: "no-event-yet"}`, and a refused answer while it still is makes it `message-refused`. `seq` rises once per observed change, and the supervisor writes a change at most once per 250 ms and never loses the last one. A reader reports `not observed` with `supervisor-gone` when it sees the recorded supervisor gone and the run not ended, with `channel-closed` when the channel is closed and the run has not yet ended, and with `run-ended` once it has; a session with no channel reads `no-channel` or `started-without-channel`.

**When the supervisor dies.** The supervisor holds the only write end of the harness's stdin, so its death closes the pipe, and a harness that declares `close.stdinEof: "exits"` reads the end of input and ends on its own. One that does not end is not reaped by a read: every reader (the `channels` section, the verbs, `worker --action status`) reports it `not observed`, reason `supervisor-gone`, from then on, the verbs refuse `channel-closed` and name `doflow worker --action cancel --slug started-sessions --worker-run <id> --operator --via console --reason ended`, and that cancel ends it. A started session without a channel whose supervisor died is marked the same way.

**Bounds.** One channel per open started session, so at most 4 on the machine and 1 per checkout (row 165's limits). Per channel: a ledger of 128 entries, at most 4 spool files of at most 64 KiB, and row 165's 4 MiB output files. When something has to be refused, a new start is refused first (`machine-limit`), then a fifth waiting message (`channel-busy`), then an actor over its rate (`actor-rate-limited`).

**Console.** `POST /api/actions/session-send`, `session-steer` and `session-stop` take the body `{v: 1, requestId, projectKey, runId, message}` (`session-stop` has no `message`), at most 40 KiB; `message` is normalized as a start's prompt is, and an empty or too long one is 400 `prompt-empty` or `prompt-too-long`, the core's own ids. They are for the loopback `local` session and for a `control` device over the native Bearer transport, as `start-session` is. A `view` device is `403 phone-scope`. A phone browser page cannot act even with a control cookie (`403 action-transport`): `deviceMayAct` is unchanged (owner ruling R-3). Each action runs the write shape `worker --action send | steer | stop --project-key … --worker-run … --via <label> --request-id … [--stdin]`, with the message as the shape's stdin. A reply is 200 `{v, action, requestId, outcome, replayed, by, result: {replayed, message, turn, channel}}` with `message` and `turn` as the verb gives them, or an error with the core's refusal id: 409 `no-channel`, `no-steer`, `channel-closed`, `not-working`, `channel-busy`, `run-ended`, `started-without-channel`, `stdin-stalled` and `write-failed`; 429 `actor-rate-limited` with `Retry-After` from the core's `retryAfterSeconds`; 404 `unknown-run`, `not-a-session` and `project-not-found`; 400 `prompt-empty`, `prompt-too-long` and `command-not-allowed`; 403 `nested-send` and `sender-in-run`; 501 `core-lacks-action` from a core without the verbs; 502 `core-contract-mismatch`. The core's words ride as the error's `message` when they are one line of at most 300 characters and name no path. A request of the three actions that reaches the core is not counted against the Console's own action limits (6 a minute and 30 an hour per actor, 20 a minute per Console), and the Console holds no hourly start limit either: both limits are the core's. A refusal before the core (`phone-scope`, `action-transport`, `usage`, `prompt-empty`, `prompt-too-long`, `request-reused`) counts against the actor's own windows, never the Console's, and past them is 429 `action-rate-limited`. The action log records the actor, the time, the run id, the action and `textBytes`, never the text; the outcome is `failed` for `stdin-stalled` and `write-failed` and `refused` for any other refusal. On the Layers page of a started session and on the start page's flow panel, a badge says `Working`, `Waiting for you` or `Not observed: <reason>` from the `channels` section alone; the message box offers Send and Steer, with Stop turn (`session-stop`) and End session (`cancel-worker`, the button that was Stop session) beside it; Steer and Stop turn are disabled while the turn is waiting; and the recent messages show kind, who, when, size and result, never text. A session with no channel says why Send is unavailable, and a view device sees the badge and the replies and a sentence that sending needs this machine's Console or a phone paired with control over the app.

AC-8 ("the phone page sends a follow-up and sees the reply within row 161's budget") is met by a `control` device over the native transport, tested with fakes, and by this machine's Console page; `console/scripts/measure-steer.ts` (a dev script, not in the package) measures the lag from the supervisor's admission of the message to the page's data (not a browser paint, and not from the Console POST) against row 161's budget of p50 under 300 ms and max under 1000 ms. The real phone app is row 163's.

### The control channel descriptor

**Harness descriptor `worker.controlChannel`** (`core/registry/harnesses.json`), grammar `control-channel-v1` (`src/registry/control-channel.ts`). It replaces the status-only `worker.steering` entry: a descriptor that still carries `steering` is refused with words naming `controlChannel`, and nothing reads the old key. A package worker declares its channel on the open `worker` contribution point; the reserved point `control-channel` no longer exists. Every worker block has the entry. `status` is `supported`, `unsupported` or `unknown`, with `evidence`, `verification` and `verifiedOn` as for every capability; an `unsupported` or `unknown` entry carries a `reason` and no recipe field. A `supported` entry holds the keys below and no others, in at most 8 KiB, and needs a supported `mainSession`:

- `argv`: the start argv. At most 32 strings of at most 200 characters, no `-` or `--` element and no placeholder (a message travels in a record, never on the command line). It passes the same widening-flag and secret-flag checks as `mainSession.argv`.
- `framing`: `jsonl-lf`, the only value. One record is one JSON object and a line feed.
- `parser`: a code parser id, this worker's `structuredOutput.fieldMap.id`, or `null`, as for `mainSession`.
- `messages`: `{followUp, steer?, stop}`, each a template: an object of at most 8 keys and 2 levels whose values are literals (a string of at most 200 characters, a finite number, a boolean, null) or the whole-string placeholders `{text}` and `{id}`. A placeholder is never part of a string, and braces anywhere else in a literal are refused. `followUp` and `steer` hold `{text}` exactly once and `stop` none; `{id}` appears at most once per template, and in every template exactly when `answer` is declared; no two templates are equal. The record is `JSON.stringify` of the filled template and a line feed, so a line feed in the text is escaped and one record is one line. The first prompt of a session uses the `followUp` record.
- `turn`: `{working, settled}`, each 1 to 8 tests in the field-map test grammar (`{path, equals | in | is}`), and no event may meet both lists.
- `answer` (optional): `{id, accepted, refused}`. The harness repeats the record's `{id}` (`d-<seq>`) at the path `id` on its answer event, and `accepted` or `refused` (test lists that no event meets together) says which.
- `close`: `{stdinEof: "exits", graceSeconds}`: closing stdin ends the harness within `graceSeconds`, an integer from 1 to 60.
- `commandPrefix` (optional): 1 to 4 printable characters with no space. A text that starts with it, after leading whitespace, is a harness command and is refused `command-not-allowed`.
- `unverified`: 1 to 8 phrases of at most 120 characters that name what only a live run can show. It is required unless an `evidence` item is a `live-run` record of the same harness. The start form warns "Talking to it is not verified on a live run" beside such a harness, and `start-options` adds the warning `channel-not-verified-live`.

Evidence for a channel is a set of recorded checks whose `establishes` carry the tokens `controlChannel:argv`, `:messages`, `:turn` and `:close`, and `:answer` when `answer` is declared; guard G41.4 counts the key established only when every token the entry needs is, and guard G41 pins a built-in channel's argv, messages, turn, answer and `commandPrefix` as literals, so a changed template is a reviewed edit beside its record. `worker --action conform` proves the entry against a fake (its `control-channel` part, above).

### Doc checks in worker evidence

`worker --action evidence --harness <id> --checks <path> --out <path> [--docs-root <dir>]` accepts a check that names `doc` in place of `argv` (exactly one of the two): `{id, doc, expect, establishes}`, where `doc` is a path relative to `--docs-root`, plain segments of letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` with no `.` or `..` segment. A checks file with a doc check needs `--docs-root` (exit 2 `usage`, reason `docs-root-required` or `docs-root-invalid`). Every doc is read before the binary runs, so a refusal leaves no process behind: its real path must stay inside the real docs root, and it must be a regular file of at most 1 MiB, else the verb is refused (exit 1) `doc-outside-root`, `doc-not-file`, `doc-too-large` or `doc-unreadable`, each naming the doc as given and no absolute path. The file is split on line feeds and each `expect` pattern is tested against its lines as for help output. The record keeps `{id, doc, sha256, expect, matched, result, establishes}`, with no absolute path and no `argv` or `exit`; guard G41.4 compares `doc` in place of `argv` and needs a `sha256` instead of an exit code. The version check of the checks file still runs the binary's `--version`.

Sources: `src/runtime/worker/start-session.ts`, `session-prompt.ts`, `launch.ts`, `steer.ts`, `channel.ts`, `channel-spool.ts` and `supervisor.ts`, `src/registry/control-channel.ts`, `src/runtime/events/session-channels.ts`, `src/registry/index.ts` (the `mainSession` checks), `core/registry/harnesses.json`, `core/worker-evidence/*/2026-11-15-cli-check-main-session.json`, `core/worker-evidence/pi/2026-11-16-cli-check-project-trust.json`.

## Login state

Row 172. DoFlow learns whether a harness is logged in from one place: the login recipe the harness's own worker descriptor declares (`worker.loginProbe`). It runs only that recipe's argv, which is the harness's own status command, and nothing else. It opens no credential or account file, reads no token variable and runs no other program (such as `gh`). A harness with no recipe reads `unknown` with the reason; DoFlow does not guess.

**The answer.** Every reader (readiness, `select`, `start-options`, doctor, `models --action sync`, `worker --action login-state` and the Console) gets the same `LoginAnswer`, at most 1 KiB: `{harness, state, reason, source, at, billing, providers, provider, hint, caveat, expirySeen, last}`.

- `state` is `logged-in`, `logged-out`, `expired` or `unknown`. `reason` is null only for `logged-in`.
- `source` is `recipe` (the recipe's check), `last-run` (a worker run's outcome) or `never-checked`; `at` is the ISO time of that source, null for `never-checked`.
- `billing` is `{mode: "subscription"|"api-key", billing: "free"|"metered"|"unknown"}`, `"mixed"` when providers disagree, or null. `providers` (at most 8) is `{id, state, mode, reason}` per provider a recipe checked. `provider` is the provider an expiry names.
- `hint` is the recipe's re-login command. `caveat` is the recipe's own statement of how much its check proves, set only on an answer whose source is `recipe`. `expirySeen` is true when the recipe can see expiry. `last` is `{state, source, at}`, only with reason `stale`.

**Precedence.** (1) A harness with no supported recipe and no run outcome is `unknown`, `no-recipe: <the descriptor's reason>`. (2) A run outcome `expired` that is not cleared wins: `expired`, source `last-run`; a later successful run clears it, and so does a later recipe answer `logged-in` only when the recipe's `expiry` is `seen` (a `not-seen` recipe cannot see expiry, so its yes does not clear one). (3) Otherwise the newer of the recipe answer and a successful run (`logged-in`, source `last-run`). A recipe answer older than 24 hours, or made under a different recipe (its digest changed), and a successful run older than 24 hours, read `unknown`, reason `stale`, with `last` set. (4) No record: `unknown`, reason `never-checked`.

**The store.** The answers live in the `harness-login` store (`<config home>/state/harness-login.json`: one slot per harness holding the last recipe answer and the last run outcome; at most 64 harnesses and 128 KiB; see the storage table). It is a cache: a failed write leaves it as it was. Nothing in it is a credential; stdout of the check is never stored and only the enum-mapped fields are kept. The store starts empty, so a harness reads `never-checked` until its recipe has run once (by `models --action sync` or `worker --action probe`). Because every descriptor's digest changed with this row, cached readiness records read `probe-stale` and are probed once more.

**Recorded by a run.** After a `failure` worker run, the supervisor matches the first 2,000 bytes of the summary against the recipe's `expirySigns` and records `expired` (with the provider a sign names); a succeeded run records `succeeded`, at most once an hour while the harness already reads logged in from a run.

### The recipe (`worker.loginProbe`)

A `loginProbe` entry has the usual capability keys (`status`, `evidence`, `verification`, `verifiedOn`, `reason`). A `supported` entry carries the recipe; an `unknown` or `unsupported` one carries none (and may carry `expirySigns` and `hint`). The validator refuses an entry over 8 KiB.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `argv` | Arguments for the harness binary; non-empty; the `{provider}` placeholder only as a whole element, once, and only with `providers`. |
| `output` | `json` (stdout, at most 64 KiB, is read as one JSON object where a rule or `billing` needs it) or `none`. A rule with `when`, or a `billing`, needs `json`. |
| `providers` | Optional `{argv, parse, emptyMeans, max}`: a listing run once; `parse` is a `modelList` parse (`lines`, `tsv-first-column`, `table-column:<name>`); `emptyMeans` is `logged-out` or `unknown`; `max` is 1 to 8, default 8. Each listed id matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}$` is checked with `{provider}` set. |
| `answers` | 1 to 8 rules `{exit, when?, state, reason?}`, first match wins. `when` is 1 to 8 tests in the field-map grammar of [Adding a harness](adding-a-harness.md); `reason` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`. At least one rule says `logged-in`; a rule hidden by an earlier one is refused. |
| `billing` | Optional `{path, values}`: the value at `path` of the JSON, when it is a key of `values` (1 to 16), gives `{mode, billing}`. The raw value is not kept. |
| `expiry` | `seen` (the recipe can tell an expired login from a missing one) or `not-seen`. |
| `expirySigns` | Optional `{signs, basis, evidence}`: 1 to 8 texts of 4 to 120 characters (one `{provider}` slot, at least 4 literal characters before it) that a failed run's summary shows when a login expired; `basis` is `source`, `transcript` or `unverified`; `evidence` is non-empty. |
| `hint` | Optional, 1 to 120 characters: the re-login command shown with a `logged-out` or `expired` answer. |
| `caveat` | Optional, 1 to 160 characters, `supported` entries only: what the check does not show. It is shown beside every answer from the recipe. |

`hint` and `caveat` hold no control or invisible character. A process is cut at 3 seconds (the listing at 5), the whole recipe at 30. A timeout is `unknown` (`timeout`), an exit no rule names is `unknown` (`exit <n>`), a missing binary is `unknown` (`not-installed`), and a signal or a missing exit code is `unknown`; none is ever `logged-out`. When a harness lists providers the answer is `logged-in` if any is, `logged-out` only if every listed provider was checked and each answered `logged-out`, `expired` (`provider-expired`) if none is logged in and one is expired, and otherwise `unknown`.

A package worker declares its recipe the same way. No separate contribution point is needed: a recipe is part of the worker descriptor, checked by the same validator.

**`worker.secretFlags`.** No argv of a worker (`cli.versionArgv`, any capability's `argv`, `modelList`, `loginProbe.providers` and each model check) may carry a flag or subcommand that prints a credential, and the login runner checks the final argv again. The built-in sets are data in `src/registry/secret-flags.ts` with their evidence (the owner's list, and each CLI's own help for pi, opencode and claude), and guard G84 checks them. A worker block may add its own `secretFlags`, 1 to 32 entries of `{flag, short?}` (a flag, any value; `flag` is `--name` or a short alias of one to three characters after a single dash, such as `-abc`, while `short` stays one character) or `{command: [<1 to 4 words>]}` (contiguous exact argv elements, anywhere in argv). A long `flag` and every owner entry apply to every binary; a `short` and a `command` apply to the binary of the set that names them.

### What ships

| Harness | `loginProbe` | Note |
|---|---|---|
| claude | `supported`: `claude auth status --json`; exit 0 logged in, exit 1 logged out; `authMethod` gives the billing | `expiry` is `not-seen`. Its help and docs do not show that the check runs offline (the `caveat`), and it was not run with the network off. |
| pi | `supported`: the provider listing and `pi auth check --provider <p> --json --no-refresh` per provider | See Pi worker facts above: expiry signs from Pi's source. |
| codex, opencode, copilot, kiro, antigravity | `unknown`, with the reason | No recorded help names a status command with an exit meaning, or DoFlow agents never run it (kiro). The start form says "DoFlow cannot check <harness>'s login: <reason>." These harnesses are never blocked on login. |

A harness whose binary is not installed reads `unknown` (`not-installed`), never `logged-out`. Whether a harness can be started depends on its main-session shape, not on login: copilot, kiro and antigravity stay not startable for `no-main-session-shape`, and codex and opencode stay startable with an `unknown` login (the start form warns `login-unknown`).

### Where it shows

- **Doctor.** The `login:` cell is `<state> (login check, <at>[; <caveat>])` or `(run, <at>)`, with `(re-login: <hint>)` after `logged-out` and `expired`, `unknown (never checked)`, `unknown (no login check: <reason>)`, `unknown (stale; last <state> <at>)` or `unknown (<reason>, <at>)`.
- **`models --action sync`.** Runs each harness's recipe and prints the same cell as `login:`. Its `--json` carries `loginState` (one of the four states) per harness, read from the store; the catalogue file holds no login field, and an older file has its login fields dropped on read.
- **`worker --action start-options`.** The `login` field above, with `logged-out` the one answer that makes a harness not startable, and the warnings `login-expired` and `login-unknown`.
- **Console.** `GET /api/harness-login` answers `{v: 1, harnesses: LoginAnswer[]}` (at most 32, from `worker --action login-state`) to the loopback `local` session and to a paired `view` or `control` device. A core that lacks the action answers 501 `core-lacks-verb`; a body that breaks the contract answers 502 `core-contract-mismatch`. The Workers page's Login column reads it, and the start form says each harness's login in words, with the caveat in brackets before the full stop and one line per checked provider.

Sources: `src/runtime/worker/login.ts`, `login-store.ts`, `readiness.ts` and `supervisor.ts`, `src/registry/login-recipe.ts` and `secret-flags.ts`, `core/registry/harnesses.json` (`loginProbe`), `core/worker-evidence/{claude,pi,opencode}/`, `test/guards/secret-flags.test.ts`.

## Prompt nudge

A plain change request that names no `/do-*` skill can be suggested `/do`, once per session. The
suggestion is advisory: it never blocks or rewrites a prompt, and the model still does the task. The
decision's cost grows only linearly with the prompt's length (the whole prompt sees single regex scans, and only bounded
slices are lowercased), and the hook stays near its existing 100 ms
budget on an idle machine; it is not free, so a slow machine can feel it.

- **Where.** Claude Code and Codex only, through the existing per-prompt hook
  (`core/harnesses/shared/hooks/policies/user-prompt-submit.sh`). The other five harnesses
  (Antigravity, OpenCode, Pi, Copilot CLI, Kiro) get no hook for this. These harnesses get only the
  rewritten `description` of `do`, `do-implement` and `do-flow`, which steer a plain change request
  to `/do` first.
- **When.** The first prompt of a session that reads as a code-change request: it starts with a
  change verb (after a polite lead-in such as "please") and names a code noun or a file path. The
  check is keyword-based, so it can miss a real request and can fire on a prompt that is not one,
for example a request to write a poem about a named function in a file.
- **When it stays silent.** Questions, slash commands, review and explanation requests, short
  replies such as "thanks" or "continue", prompts that mention `/do` or a `/do-*` skill, subagent
  prompts, a session whose branch already has a feature folder under `agent-docs/doflow/`, and a
  session where a `/do-*` skill has already run. A session is nudged at most once; a prompt that
  mentions `/do` also ends the chance for that session.
- **What it says.** One note, in the same hook output as the first-prompt context or alone on a
  later prompt. It tells the model to mention in one sentence that `/do` would classify the task and
  apply DoFlow's checks, then to do what the user asked, without running `/do` itself or waiting for
  a reply. It names `/do` and no other skill.
- **What it never does.** It makes no model call and no network call, stores no prompt text (the
  prompt reaches `jq` on standard input and is never written out), starts no `node` or `doflow-run`
  process, and never emits a `block` decision. Any failure on its path (no registry, no `jq` regex
  support, an unwritable state folder) leaves the prompt un-nudged.
- **Where the rules live.** Data, not code: the `promptNudge` object in
  `core/registry/workflows.json` (message, verbs, nouns, lead-ins, blockers, path pattern). The
  decision program is `core/harnesses/shared/hooks/policies/prompt-nudge.jq`. The workflow engine and
  the task classifier do not read this key; the registry shape guard G25
  (`test/guards/prompt-nudge-registry.test.ts`) does.
- **Changing a rule.** `test/hooks/prompt-nudge.corpus.json` holds labelled prompts. `test/hooks/prompt-nudge.test.ts`
  runs every one through the shipped rules: a prompt labelled silent that nudges fails the test, while recall on the
  prompts labelled as requests is printed as a diagnostic line and never asserted. For a false alarm or a missed
  request, add the prompt to the corpus (`"expect":"silent"` or `"expect":"nudge"`), tighten or extend the
  `promptNudge` lists until the corpus passes, then run `node --test test/hooks/prompt-nudge.test.ts
  test/guards/prompt-nudge-registry.test.ts`. Extend a list for a missed request only when no silent prompt starts to nudge.

### Switching the nudge off

One setting file, `prompt-nudge`, holds one word on its first line (case and surrounding whitespace
are ignored):

| Scope | Path |
|---|---|
| Project | `<repo root>/.doflow/prompt-nudge` |
| User | `<config home>/prompt-nudge` |

| Value | Effect |
|---|---|
| `on` | the nudge may run (also the default when neither file exists) |
| `off` | no nudge |

When both files exist, the project file decides alone. A file that cannot be read, is empty, or holds
any other word counts as `off`. The setting affects only the nudge: the first-prompt context, the
session title and the other hooks are unchanged. The repo root is the nearest ancestor of the
session's working directory that holds a `.git` entry.

### First-prompt context on Claude Code

The same hook builds the first-prompt context: a Git block, up to 4,000 characters of the previous
compact summary, and a warning left by the prior session, plus a session title of the form
`branch — sha`. It now emits one nested `hookSpecificOutput` object for both Claude Code and Codex.
Claude Code ignores a top-level `additionalContext`, which is what the hook emitted before, so on
Claude Code this context was not delivered; it is now, once per session start (including after
compact, clear or resume). The hook emits the title in the form Claude Code documents; its rule is
to skip the title when the session already has one, and Codex never receives a title.

`DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE=main|worker` (case-insensitive; unset or empty is `main`) declares a launched session's role in the environment the hook process inherits. A `worker` never writes the project's compact summary, receives no git context or prior warning and never the `/do` prompt nudge or the default main lineage's summary (a launched worker can still receive a summary granted to it, or its own lineage's compact summary), and does not write `uncommitted-warning.txt` when its session ends (session logging and the project's `last_active` time are still updated), so both files are left for the main session. Any other value is treated as `worker` and writes one `[doflow] unknown DOFLOW_SESSION_ROLE` line to stderr. A prompt carrying `agent_id` or `agent_type` (a native subagent) never claims the summary or warning. Whether a given harness forwards a launcher's environment to its hooks is not verified here.

`DOFLOW_STARTED_RUN=w-<12 hex>` names the run of a session started by `worker --action start-session`. When `session-context` writes a session's `git-context.json` and the variable matches that shape exactly, the file also carries `started_run` with its value; any other value is ignored and the key is absent. `fleet --action tree` joins the started run and the session on it (see Started sessions).

## Git Lifecycle Intents

The `/do-git` skill provides cycle-aware commands:

- **start** - Begin a new task on the appropriate branch type
- **save** - Stage and commit with intelligent message from diff
- **sync** - Sync local branches with remote state
- **ship** - Ship current feature to integration branch
- **release** - Full release ritual: cut branch, bump version, merge to production, create tag
- **hotfix** - Create and propagate hotfix across all live lines
- **backport** - Cherry-pick a commit to another branch
- **status** - Report repository state and lifecycle position

Raw git operations still work via passthrough: `/do-git status`, `/do-git log --oneline`, etc.

The release version comes from `doflow-run git-state --next-version`. Its base tag is the highest `v*`
tag merged into HEAD or into the production branch, and the result carries a `warning` key when a
`package.json` exists and its version differs from that tag. A slug that is not letters, digits, dot,
underscore or dash (starting with a letter or digit, no `..`) is refused with exit 2 and
`{"error":"invalid-slug",...}`, by `doflow-run git-state --branch-name` and by `doflow-run paths` alike.

### Release checklist (beta cut)

1. Preflight: run `node src/release/preflight.ts`.
2. Rehearse: run `node src/release/rehearse.ts --node "$NODE_FLOOR"` (resolve floor Node once via `NODE_FLOOR="$(npx -y -p node@24.22.1 node -p process.execPath)"`).
3. Tag and push: create and push the release tag (`git tag v<version>` and `git push origin v<version>`). Tagging and pushing remain the user's explicit action.
4. Watch the workflow graph: the release workflow runs `preflight` and `test` in parallel, followed by `verify-artifact`, `draft-release`, `publish`, `verify-registry`, `promote`, and `publish-release`.
5. Verify and record: inspect `npm view @khoavu882/doflow dist-tags` and record the outcome in the cut record below.

#### Dist-tag policy

- `beta-candidate` holds a just-published prerelease until `verify-registry` passes and is empty otherwise.
- `beta` is the newest verified prerelease, moved only by `promote`.
- `latest` is moved by `promote` only for an approved stable tag, and otherwise only by the user (it points to `2.0.0-beta.4` now; UD-1 decides whether it follows each verified beta).
- Cutting a stable version stays the user's decision (UD-3); the workflow lets it through only behind the stable gate below.

#### Stable cut (`vX.Y.Z`, no hyphen)

A stable tag runs the `preflight` job in the GitHub environment `release-stable`, so GitHub holds the job until a required reviewer approves it; preflight then passes `--allow-stable` and reads the environment back (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/environments/release-stable`). A prerelease tag runs in the environment `release-prerelease` and never passes the flag. That environment needs no configuration (GitHub creates it on first use) and must stay unprotected: a prerelease must not wait on approval. Rule R14 refuses a `release.yml` that names `--allow-stable` anywhere else, for a prerelease, or without that exact binding.

The user configures this once, outside the repository: Settings > Environments > New environment `release-stable`, enable "Required reviewers" and add at least one reviewer. Optionally restrict its deployment tags to `v*`. Until that is done the stable cut fails closed: GitHub creates `release-stable` with no protection on first use, and preflight's `stable-allowed` check refuses any environment that is missing, unreadable, or names no required reviewer, before anything is published.

What the gate does and does not prove:
- The approver can be the person who pushed the tag unless "Prevent self-review" is on, and repository administrators can bypass the approval unless "Allow administrators to bypass" is off. Preflight reads neither setting, so in a single-maintainer repository the gate means only that someone holding the user's GitHub credentials approved the run.
- Preflight verifies how `release-stable` is configured, not that this run was approved, and R14 finds the flag by its text. The gate stops accidental stable cuts and forces a human step; it is not a defence against someone with write access to the workflow.

To check the gate before tagging, run `node src/release/preflight.ts --allow-stable` with `gh` signed in. It calls `gh` only once `package.json` is at the stable version (a prerelease version never looks the environment up), and `--offline` always refuses a stable cut.

#### Rollback

Every rollback command is the user's:
1. Repoint the channel to the previous good version: `npm dist-tag add @khoavu882/doflow@<good> beta`
2. Remove a stale candidate: `npm dist-tag rm @khoavu882/doflow beta-candidate`
3. Deprecate the faulty version: `npm deprecate @khoavu882/doflow@<bad> "Superseded by <good>"`
4. Never unpublish a version.
5. Delete a draft Release left by a failed run: `gh release delete <tag> --yes`
6. Record the row in the cut record below.

#### Cut record

| Slice | Tag | Re-cuts | Fault class | dist-tags after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.1 | unrecorded | env-node-version | unpublished |
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.2 | unrecorded | env-host-tool | unpublished |
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.3 | unrecorded | artifact (version deprecated) | unrecorded |
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.4 | unrecorded | none | unrecorded |
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.5 | unrecorded | none | latest: 2.0.0-beta.4, beta: 2.0.0-beta.5 |
| unrecorded | v2.0.0-beta.6 | unrecorded | unclassified | unpublished (latest: 2.0.0-beta.4, beta: 2.0.0-beta.5) |
